# PART I: PRE-LENTEN PERIOD (SUNDAYS OF THE PUBLICAN & PHARISEE, PRODIGAL SON, MEATFARE, AND CHEESEFARE)

## Sunday of the Publican and the Pharisee

*[Woodcut Headpiece: The Savior Emmanuel blessing within a central medallion, flanked by holy prophets with scrolls in an elaborate foliate border.]*

*THE TRIODION, THAT IS: THE THREE-ODE CANON.*[^38]  
*With the Holy God, containing its proper Order of Services.*

*THEY BEGIN THUS, ACCORDING TO THE NARRATIVE OF THE PARABLE OF THE PUBLICAN AND THE PHARISEE.*

*[Narrative Woodcut Illustration: The Parable of the Publican and the Pharisee in the Temple (Luke 18:9–14). The Pharisee prays before the altar with outstretched hands; the Publican beats his breast at the entrance; Christ the Savior with His disciples looks upon them.]*

*On Saturday evening, Little Vespers according to custom.*

*At Great Vespers, after the Opening Psalm, we recite the Kathisma: "Blessed is the man..." On "Lord, I have cried," we set 10 verses, and we sing 3 Resurrectional Stichera from the Octoechos, and 4 Anatolian, and 2 Idiomelon Stichera from the Triodion,[^39] repeating the first. Tone 1:*

*Sunday 1.*

*Sticheron 1, Tone 1:*  
Let us not pray like the Pharisee, brethren; for he that exalts himself shall be humbled.[^40] Let us humble ourselves before God, crying out like the Publican with fasting: O God, cleanse us sinners!

*Sticheron 2, Tone 1:*  
A Pharisee, overcome by vainglory, and a Publican, bowing down in repentance, came unto Thee, the only Master. But the one, boasting, was deprived of good things; while the other, speaking not a word, was accounted worthy of gifts. In these groanings, strengthen me, O Christ God, for Thou art the Lover of mankind!

*Repeat the first Sticheron.*

*Glory... Tone 8:*  
O Almighty Lord, I know how much tears can avail: for they brought up Hezekiah from the gates of death; they delivered the sinful woman from the transgressions of many years; they justified the Publican rather than the Pharisee.[^41] And I pray Thee: number me with them, and have mercy on me!

*Both now... The first Theotokion (Dogmatikon) of the current Tone of the Octoechos.*

*At the Litiya, the Sticheron of the Saint of the Monastery according to custom, and the Theotokion. Glory... Tone 3:*  
Having understood the difference between the Publican and the Pharisee, O my soul, hate thou the proud voice of the one, but emulate the contrite prayer of the other, and cry aloud: O God, cleanse me a sinner, and have mercy on me!

*Both now... Theotokion from the Alphabetical Stichera of the same Tone:*  
Without seed from the Holy Spirit, and by the Father's will, thou didst conceive the Son of God, Who from the Father was without mother before all ages, but for our sake came from thee without father, Whom thou didst bear in the flesh, and didst nourish as an Infant with milk. Therefore cease not to pray, that our souls may be delivered from tribulations!

*At the Aposticha, the Resurrectional and Alphabetical Stichera of the Octoechos. Glory... Tone 5:*

*Sunday of the Publican and the Pharisee.*

*Sticheron at the Aposticha, Tone 5:*  
Weighted down by mine iniquities, with mine eyes I cannot look up and behold the height of heaven; but receive me, O Savior, repenting like the Publican, and have mercy on me!

*Both now... Theotokion from the Octoechos, Tone 2:*  
Thou art a temple and a gate, a palace and the throne of the King, O all-honored Virgin, through whom my Deliverer, Christ the Lord, appeared unto those sleeping in darkness, being the Sun of Righteousness, wishing to enlighten those whom He created in His own image with His own hand. Therefore, O all-hymned one, as thou hast acquired motherly boldness toward Him, pray unceasingly that our souls may be saved!

*Troparion: "Rejoice, O Virgin Theotokos," is said thrice. And there takes place the blessing of the loaves, and their distribution, and the drinking of two cups of wine; and a great reading from the Epistles of the Apostles.*

*The office of the Saint who occurs on this Sunday is sung on Friday at Compline, or whenever the ecclesiarch decides, unless there should coincide a Feast of the Master or of the Patron Saint of the temple. For the Patron Saint of the temple, even if not a Feast of the Master, follows rather the Feast of the Master on its own Patronal Feast, on which a Vigil must be celebrated, and his commemoration is by no means omitted, nor is it transferred to another day; but we sing the service of the temple, just as on the Meeting of the Lord, on all the days on which the Meeting of the Lord occurs.*

*Chapter of Mark the Hieromonk concerning the Feast of the Meeting of the Lord,[^42] if the Forefeast of the Meeting should fall on this Sunday, or on the Sunday of the Prodigal Son, or of Meatfare, or Cheesefare; likewise concerning its Afterfeast (Chapter 42):*[^43]  
*Except on Cheesefare Sunday (on which there is no Afterfeast, but only the Apodosis of the Feast): On Saturday evening, at Little Vespers, as indicated above. At the Litiya, 1. Pay heed to the signs with the customary rubric, and we sing the appointed service, observing Chapter 42 on Festal days. On "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Octoechos 4, of the Triodion 3, and of the Forefeast or Feast 3 (if it be the Afterfeast; likewise everywhere understand: where the Forefeast is, there also is the Afterfeast of the Feast, unless a particular rubric indicates otherwise in its place). Glory... from the Triodion. Both now... Theotokion of Tone 1. At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Temple; if it be the Afterfeast, then of the Feast occurring on that day. On "Lord, I have cried" of the Triodion, Glory... likewise at the Praises of the Triodion. Both now... Theotokion, or of the Forefeast, or on the Afterfeast, of the Feast. At the Litiya: 1 of the Forefeast. At the Blessing of Loaves: "Rejoice, O Virgin Theotokos" thrice; and a reading from the Epistles of the Apostles, etc. Reading of the Feast, or of the Life of Saint Luke.*  
*At Matins, on "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Resurrection twice. Glory... Both now... of the Forefeast, etc., up to the Kathisma. After the first Kathisma: Kathismata of the Resurrection with Heirmoi on 4, and of the Theotokos on 2, and of the Forefeast on 4...*

*Sunday 1.*

*...and in the Triodion on 4. Sessional hymn: After Ode 3, Kontakion and Ikos of the Forefeast, and Sessional hymn in the Triodion on 2. Glory... Both now... of the Forefeast. Sessional hymn of the Resurrection. Glory... in the Triodion. Both now... of the Forefeast. At the Praises, Resurrectional Stichera 4, and of the Forefeast on 2, and in the Triodion 2. Glory... in the Triodion, Podoben: (see below on fol. 17). At the Hours, Troparion of the Resurrection; Glory... of the Forefeast; Both now... Theotokion; at the 1st Hour, of the Forefeast, alternating with the Triodion. At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Tone on 4, and in the Triodion Ode 3 on 4, and of the Forefeast Ode 6 on 4; and on the Afterfeast say thus: After the Entrance, Troparion of the Resurrection and of the Forefeast; Glory... Kontakion in the Triodion; Both now... of the Forefeast; Prokeimenon, Apostle, Gospel, and Communion Verse of the day and of the Theotokos.*

*When the Feast of the Meeting of the Lord falls on one of the aforesaid Sundays:*  
*At Little Vespers, Resurrectional Stichera of the Tone on 4; Glory... Both now... of the Feast. At the Aposticha, one Resurrectional Sticheron, and of the Feast 3, Tone 7, with their verses, as written for Great Vespers. Troparion of the Resurrection; Glory... Both now... of the Feast. At Great Vespers, Stichera of the Resurrection 3, and in the Triodion 3, and of the Feast 4; Glory... in the Triodion; Both now... of the Feast; Readings of the Feast. At the Litiya, Stichera of the Feast 3, Tone 1... In the Triodion, those that are at the Praises; Glory... likewise at the Praises of the Triodion; Both now... of the Feast. At the Aposticha, Resurrectional Stichera; Glory... in the Triodion; Both now... of the Feast. At the Blessing of Loaves, Troparion of the Feast thrice; and a reading from the Epistles of the Apostles, etc. Reading of the Feast, or from the Life of Saint Luke.*  
*At Matins, on "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Resurrection twice; Glory... Both now... of the Feast. After the Kathismata, Sessional hymns of the Resurrection, two of them; and the evening reading of Sunday concerning the Publican and the Pharisee. The Polyeleos and the Troparia "The Angelic Council";[^44] Hypakoe of the Tone, and a great reading. After "Praise the Name of the Lord," Sessional hymns of the Feast, all once; and a reading of the Feast. Gradual hymns of the Tone. Prokeimenon of the Feast; "Let every breath," and Gospel of the Feast. "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ," and Psalm 50. Sticheron of the Feast, Tone 6: "Let the gate of heaven open..." Canons: of the Resurrection with Heirmos on 4, and in the Triodion on 4, and of the Feast on 6. For the Canon of the Feast, both choirs together sing the Heirmos. After Ode 3, Kontakion, Ikos, and Sessional hymn in the Triodion; Glory... Both now... of the Feast. After Ode 6, Kontakion and Ikos of the Feast, and reading from the Prologue. At the Liturgy, we sing the appointed hymn. Exaposteilarion of the Resurrection; Glory... in the Triodion; Both now... of the Feast. At the Praises, Resurrectional Stichera 4, and of the Feast on 4, with the verses of the Feast; Glory... of the Feast; Both now... "Most blessed art thou..." Some Typika command to sing at the Praises: Resurrectional Stichera 3, and in the Triodion 2, and of the Feast 3; Glory... of the Feast; Both now... "Most blessed art thou..." Troparion of the Resurrection only, and the customary dismissal. And the exit into the narthex; Glory... Both now... Gospel Sticheron; and the 1st Hour. At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes as appointed, of the Tone on 4, and in the Triodion Ode 3 on 4, and of the Feast Ode 6 on 4. Introit verse: "The Lord has made known His salvation; His righteousness has He openly shown in the sight of the heathen."[^45] After this, Troparion of the Resurrection and of the Feast; Glory... Kontakion in the Triodion; Both now... Kontakion of the Feast; Prokeimenon, Apostle, Alleluia, Gospel, and Communion Verse of the day and of the Feast.*

*If the Apodosis of the Meeting should fall on the aforesaid Sundays:*  
*Except at Little Vespers: on Saturday evening, at Little Vespers, and at Great Vespers, and at Matins, and at the Liturgy, the office of the Feast is taken according to custom with the apodosis. Except at Great Vespers: Stichera of the Feast on "Lord, I have cried" on 4; Both now... Theotokion of Tone 1. If it be the Temple...*

*Sunday of the Publican and the Pharisee.*

*...of the Meeting, Both now... of the Feast. At the Litiya, Stichera of the Feast, and in the Triodion 5 at the Praises, without the verse. At the Blessing of Loaves, Troparion: "Rejoice, O Virgin Theotokos" twice, and of the Feast once. At Matins, at the Praises: Stichera of the Resurrection 4, and of the Feast with the verses 4; Glory... in the Triodion. At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Tone on 6, and of the Feast Ode 4 on 4. After the Entrance, Troparion of the Resurrection and of the Feast, and the rest of the day and of the Feast.*

*AT MATINS, after the Six Psalms, "God is the Lord" in the Tone of the Octoechos; and we say the Resurrectional Troparion twice, and the Theotokion once. Ordinary recitation of the Psalter, and Sessional hymns of the Octoechos. And a reading in the Commentary Gospel from Luke for the present day: Sessional homily of Chrysostom ("Seeing the verdant and distant..."), another Sessional homily of Cyril of Alexandria ("As in divine glory...").[^46] After the Evlogitaria, Troparia: "The Angelic Council"; Hypakoe, Gradual hymns or Resurrectional hymns of the Tone. Prokeimenon, "Let every breath," the Gospel of the cycle. "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ," Psalm 50. Glory... Through the prayers of the Apostles... Both now... Through the prayers of the Theotokos... Have mercy on me, O God... And this Sticheron, Tone 6:*[^47]

*Penitential Sticheron, Tone 6:*  
Reflecting, wretched one, upon the multitude of evil deeds which I have committed, I tremble at the fearful Day of Judgment; but trusting in the mercy of Thy lovingkindness, like David I cry aloud unto Thee: Have mercy on me, O God, according to Thy great mercy!

*Then the Deacon or Priest says:*  
**Deacon:** O God, save Thy people...  
*Lord, have mercy (12 times).*

*Canon of the Resurrection with Heirmos on 4, and the Cross-Resurrectional on 2, and of the Theotokos on 2 [if a Saint occurs with Polyeleos: Canon of the Resurrection with Heirmos on 4, and of the Saint on 4]; and in the Triodion on 6. A Composition of George.*[^48]

*Tone 6, Ode 1. Heirmos:*  
As Israel walked on foot through the deep as upon dry land, beholding their pursuer Pharaoh engulfed, they cried: Let us sing unto God a song of victory!

*Troparion:*  
Guiding all by parables, O Christ, unto the amendment of life, Thou dost exalt the Publican for his humility, having shown the Pharisee cast down by his haughtiness.

*Troparion:*  
Honoring the dignity of humility which exalts on high, and beholding the grievous fall of pride, emulate the good deeds of the Publican, and abhor the malice of the Pharisee.

*Troparion:*  
Through haughtiness every good thing is made void, but through humility every evil thing is destroyed; which let us embrace, O ye faithful, abhorring the model of vainglory.

*Sunday 1.*

*Troparion:*  
Desiring His disciples to be humble-minded, the King of all, admonishing them, taught them to emulate the groaning and humility of the Publican.

*Glory...*  
*Troparion:*  
Like the Publican I groan, and with unceasing laments, O Lord, I now approach Thy compassion: have compassion also upon me, who now lead my life in humility.

*Both now...*  
*Theotokion:*  
Mind, counsel, expectation, body, soul, and spirit I cast upon thee, O Sovereign Lady: deliver me from cruel enemies and tribulations, and from the wrath to come, and save me.

*Katavasia: I shall open my mouth...*[^49]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*  
There is none holy as Thou, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and hast established us upon the rock of Thy confession.[^50]

*Troparion:*  
From the dunghill of passions the humble man is exalted; but from the height of virtues every proud-hearted man falls grievously. Let us flee the pattern of his wickedness!

*Troparion:*  
Vainglory squanders the wealth of righteousness, but humility scatters the multitude of passions. Make us who strive to emulate the Publican partakers of his portion, O Savior!

*Troparion:*  
Like the Publican, beating our breasts, we cry out with compunction: O God, cleanse us sinners, that we may receive His forgiveness!

*Troparion:*  
Let us come to emulation, O ye faithful, cultivating meek humility, dwelling in sighs of heart, in weeping, and in prayer, that we may obtain divine pardon.

*Glory...*  
*Troparion:*  
Let us cast away, O ye faithful, boastful pride, grievous exalting of ourselves, abominable puffing up, and the most wicked burden of the Pharisee, unbefitting God.

*Both now...*  
*Theotokion:*  
Having hope in thee, the only refuge, let me not fall away from good expectation; but may I obtain thy help, O pure one, being delivered from every harm of cruel foes.

*Sunday of the Publican and the Pharisee.*

*Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*[^51]  
*Sessional Hymn:* Humility exalted the Publican who was held fast by evils, when he sighed and cried out unto the Creator: "Cleanse me!" But haughty exaltation cast down from righteousness the wretched, boastful Pharisee. Therefore let us emulate good things, departing from evils.

*Glory...*  
*Sessional Hymn:* Humility of old exalted the Publican who wept and sighed: "Cleanse me!" and he was justified. Let us all emulate him, who have fallen into the depth of evils: let us sigh from the depth of the heart unto the Savior: "We have sinned, cleanse us, O only Lover of mankind!"

*Both now...*  
*Theotokion:* Speedily accept our supplications, O Sovereign Lady, and offer them unto Thy Son and God, O all-immaculate Mistress; deliver from distress those who flee unto thee; crush plots, and cast down the audacity of those who impiously arm themselves against thy servants, O all-pure one.

*Ode 4. Heirmos: Christ is my power, the Lord and God, the venerable Church chants in a manner befitting God, crying out from a pure mind, keeping festival in the Lord.*[^52]  
*Troparion:* The Word, having humbled Himself even unto the form of a servant,[^53] showed forth an excellent way of exaltation: humility. Emulating Him, everyone that humbles himself is exalted.

*Troparion:* The Righteous One was exalted, and the Pharisee fell down; but the Publican, weighed down by a multitude of evils, humbled himself, and was exalted, being unexpectedly justified.

*Troparion:* Exaltation appeared as an advocate for poverty from the wealth of virtues; and again, humility of justification appeared as wealth from extreme poverty, which let us acquire!

*Troparion:* O Master, Thou hast said that Thou dost oppose the boastful, and dost give Thy grace unto the humble, O Savior.[^54] Send down Thy grace now upon us who have humbled ourselves!

*Glory...*  
*Troparion:* Ever leading us up unto divine exaltation, the Savior and Master showed forth high-making humility: for with His own hands He washed the feet of the disciples.[^55]

*Both now...*  
*Theotokion:* As thou hast given birth unto the unapproachable Light, O Virgin, dispel the darkness of my soul with the radiant dawn of illumination, and guide my life into the paths of salvation.

*Sunday.*

*Ode 5. Heirmos: By Thy divine light, O Good One, illumine, I pray Thee, the souls of those who lovingly wake early unto Thee, that they may know Thee, O Word of God, as the true God, Who callest them from the darkness of sins.*[^56]  
*Troparion:* Let us strive to emulate the virtues of the Pharisee, and to rival the humility of the Publican, hating in both the improper conceit and the ruin of fallings.

*Troparion:* The vain course of righteousness was reproved when the Pharisee yoked pride therein; but immediately the burden of unrighteousness was made void when the Publican acquired humility as a traveling companion in high-making virtue.

*Troparion:* The Pharisee thought to run as a charioteer in virtues; but the Publican, running on foot swifter than a Lydian chariot,[^57] well outstripped him, having yoked humility unto compassion.

*Troparion:* Having all understood the Parable of the Publican with our mind, come, let us rival his tears, offering a contrite spirit unto God, seeking forgiveness of sins.

*Glory...*  
*Troparion:* Being of sound mind, let us cast far away the haughty and cruel, proud and arrogant disposition of the Pharisee, cruel and boastful, lest we be stripped of divine grace.

*Both now...*  
*Theotokion:* O Good Lady, send forth the rod of power unto all of us who flee unto thee,[^58] granting us to hold dominion in the midst of all enemies, and delivering us from every harm.

*Ode 6. Heirmos: Beholding the sea of life surging with the storm of temptations, and fleeing unto Thy calm haven, I cry unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O Greatly Merciful One!*[^59]  
*Troparion:* The Publican and the Pharisee ran together the course of life; but the one, held fast by haughty pride, was shamefully drowned; while the other was saved by humility.

*Troparion:* The Pharisee passed through life in virtues, and the Publican in sins; but the one fell away from humility through intellectual puffing up; while the other is exalted, having shown himself humble-minded.

*Sunday of the Publican and the Pharisee.*

*Troparion:* Passing through the troublesome course of life, let us emulate the watchful mourning of the Publican; and let us flee the haughtiness of the abominable Pharisee, and we shall live.

*Troparion:* Let us rival the character and humility of Christ the Savior, desiring to receive the unending dwelling of joy, taking up our abode in the land of the living.

*Glory...*  
*Troparion:* Thou hast shown forth high-making humility unto Thy disciples, O Master: girded about Thy loins with a towel, Thou didst wash their feet, and didst command them to emulate this example.

*Both now...*  
*Theotokion:* When I was created naked in simplicity and unartful life, the enemy clothed me with a double garment of transgression and with the grossness of the flesh. But now, through thy mediation, O Maiden, I am saved.

*Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*[^60]  
*Kontakion:* Having once justified the Publican when he cried out, O bountiful and good Savior, Thou didst cleanse that sinner. And overlooking my all-defiled transgressions, save me also, O wretched man, O Long-suffering One, Who alone art good to reconcile!

*Ikos:* I have become like unto the haughty-minded Pharisee, O Savior, exalting myself in virtues; and I have fallen down unto the earth in ruin. As Thou didst humble the Publican, O compassionate Word, humble me also, and save me who am haughty and boast vainly with lofty thought; and grant unto my contrite soul and heart, calling out unto Thee: "Cleanse me, O God, the only Lover of mankind, and grant me forgiveness!"  
**Choir:** *Who alone art good to reconcile.*

*PROLOGUE, THAT IS A BRIEF FOREWORD UNTO THE SYNAXARIA,*[^61]  
*That is, Collections, for the Triodion Synaxaria.*  
*The Synaxaria of Nikephoros Kallistos Xanthopoulos for the Eminent Feasts of the Triodion, showing the reason concerning each of them, how and when they had their beginning, and for what reason they are so held, and are ordered by the holy God-bearing Fathers, with certain particular bonds; which begin from the Publican and the Pharisee, and end even unto All Saints.*

*Sunday.*

*The Synaxarion of the Menaion—that is, the Monthly Calendar—ought therefore to be read first at the Seventh Ode, as is the custom; and thereafter the following.*  
*Thou shalt say, therefore, after the reading thereof, thus:*

*Verses for the Triodion:*  
Unto the Creator of things above and things below:  
The Three-Ode Canon in truth of songs from the Angels;  
Receive now also from men the Triodion.  
*Verses on the Publican and the Pharisee:*  
Whoso lives in the manner of a Pharisee, be far from the Church:  
For Christ is within; the humble man is to be received.  

On this present day, with God, we begin the Triodion. Which Book, in truth, many from among our holy and God-bearing Fathers, the Hymnographers, excellently and as was fitting, being awakened by the Holy Spirit, composed for vigilant singing. And the first of them who devised this—namely, three Odes, in representation, as I deem, of the Holy and Life-Originating Trinity—was the great Versifier Cosmas,[^62] during the Great and Holy Week of the Passions of our Lord God and Savior Jesus Christ, having composed three Odes with acrostics corresponding in title to almost every day. Following his example, others of the Fathers, and most beautifully of all, Theodore and Joseph the Studites,[^63] having composed chants for the other weeks of the holy Great Fast, delivered them first unto their own Studios Monastery. Afterward, having arranged the most notable odes and written them in verse, they also gathered and collected in this Book other hymns from the Fathers in some measure. Since, therefore, Sunday contains within itself the first day, being honored by Christ's Resurrection, being first, and eighth, and last: they rightly did so, that unto the second day, that is Monday, they assigned the First Ode; unto the third day, that is Tuesday, the Second Ode; unto the fourth, that is Wednesday, the Third; unto the fifth, that is Thursday, the Fourth; unto the sixth day, that is Friday, the Fifth Ode; and unto the seventh, that is Saturday, the Sixth and Seventh Odes, and the last two: that is, the Eighth and Ninth; which two days, that is Saturday and Sunday, all men commonly hold as the most eminent of all days. As indeed the blessed Cosmas also appointed for Great Saturday, having composed a Four-Ode Canon there, although afterward the most wise Emperor Leo...[^64]

*Sunday of the Publican and the Pharisee.*

...having given command, Mark the Monk, Bishop of Hydrus,[^65] brought and arranged that into a complete Canon. Furthermore, it is called Triodion *catachrestice* [in an extended sense]:[^66] for it does not always contain Three-Ode Canons, since complete and full Canons are also translated. But I consider that it has such a name from the most plentiful and greater part within it, or on account of those Odes of Great Week which were composed first, as was said. The purpose, therefore, and design of our holy Fathers in the writing of these Triodion Odes and Synaxaria was this: that throughout the entire Book of the Triodion they might briefly recall from the beginning, and impress upon the memory of all, as many divine benefits as have been wrought concerning us: namely, how, having been created by Him, and having cast away His commandment which was given unto us for our discipline, we became exiles from the delight of Paradise, and were cast out through the envy of the godly malice of the serpent and enemy, who was brought unto nothing on account of his arrogance; and how we lived alienated from good things, being guided by the devil; and how the Son and Word of God, having had compassion in His mercy, bowed the heavens and came down, and chose the Virgin for His dwelling, and for our sake became Man; and through the life which was ordered according to Him, showed the way into heaven: that is, first of all through humility, through fasting, through the putting away of malice, and through other works; and how He suffered, and rose from the dead, and ascended again into heaven, and sent the Holy Spirit unto His holy Disciples and Apostles; and how He, the Son of God and perfect God, was proclaimed by them according unto all things; and what the divine Apostles, by the grace of the All-Holy Spirit, accomplished, gathering together all from the ends of the earth through their preaching, that they might fill the world on high: which was the purpose from the beginning of God Himself, Who deigned to create us. But among these things of the Triodion, that is, of the Three-Ode Canon, this is the purpose: these present three Feasts—the first of the Publican and the Pharisee; the second of the Prodigal Son; and the third of the Second Coming of Christ—have been devised by the holy Fathers as a certain anticipatory discipline and stirring up, to the end that we might prepare ourselves and be fit for the spiritual ascetic labors of the Fast, having put away the base habit acquired by custom. And therefore, before all things, they set forth for us the Parable of the Publican and the Pharisee, and called it a Proclamatory Song. For just as those who wish to go forth unto bodily warfare receive from their Hetmans instruction concerning the time of war...[^67]

*Sunday.*

...receive instruction and knowledge, that they may clean and polish their swords, and thoroughly equip themselves in everything else pertaining unto war, and casting far away every hindrance, may come in readiness unto the struggles, and prepare all things in sufficiency as much as is needed; and often also, before the battle, they do not endure to delay against the enemies, and discourses, histories, and examples are brought before them, stirring up their souls unto emulation, and putting far away rottenness, fear, sloth, and whatever else might be dangerous: so also the divine Fathers, as if by a trumpet, prefigure the forthcoming deployment of the Lenten army: that if there be any preeminent passion in our souls, that is, an affection, and that for a long time has wrought poison, we might cleanse it; and moreover, that striving to acquire whatever of good works was lacking unto us, and being armed according to decency, we might thus betake ourselves, prepared, unto the Lenten ascetic struggles and labors. Since, therefore, the very first armor unto virtue is Repentance and Humility, and on the other hand, the greatest impediment and hindrance thereunto is boastfulness and puffing up: for these reasons the aforementioned holy Fathers set forth and reveal the present Parable from the divine Gospel, worthy of faith, as the first of all. Admonishing us, that through the Pharisee indeed we should beware of pride and high conceit of ourselves; and through the Publican we should esteem highly that which is contrary unto that passion, that is, humility and repentance, and should receive the same. For pride and puffing up is the first and worst passion among passions, so that on account thereof not only did the fall from heaven befall the devil, who was formerly the Daystar,[^68] and afterward became and was named darkness; but also unto the beginning of the human race, that is unto Adam, banishment from the delight of Paradise occurred on account of that same passion. Therefore, after such a manner do the Holy Fathers admonish us, that no one on account of virtues, even if they be perfectly fulfilled, should exalt himself, nor despise or surpass his neighbor, but should always be humble. For the Lord resists the proud, but gives grace unto the humble.[^69] And it is indeed a better thing when a sinner turns, than when a virtuous man exalts himself and becomes proud. "For I say unto you," He said, "that the Publican went out justified rather than the Pharisee."[^70] The Parable shows, therefore, that no one ought to exalt himself, even if he should perform good works...

*Sunday of the Publican and the Pharisee.*

...fulfilling them; but always to abase and humble oneself, and to pray with the whole soul unto the Lord God, even if one be encompassed by the extreme of evils, being assured of this, that salvation is not far off. A Publican, in truth, is one who receives tolls from princes, and buys them through extreme injustice, and from thence acquires gains.[^71] A Pharisee, on the other hand, is as one set apart, and surpassing others in knowledge. A Sadducee is from a certain Zadok, which is interpreted "righteous": for *Tsedek* is righteousness. There were also three sects among the Hebrews: Essenes, Pharisees, and Sadducees; which latter acknowledged and believed that there is neither resurrection, nor Angel, nor Spirit.[^72] Through the prayers of all Thy holy Hymnographers, O Christ our God, have mercy on us. Amen.

*Ode 7. Heirmos: The God-fashioned furnace was once prefigured as an image of the virgin birthgiving; for it consumed not the holy youths who were singing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!*[^73]  
*Troparion:* Exalted by works of justification, the Pharisee was grievously puffed up by the snares of vainglory, boasting immeasurably; but the Publican, soaring on the light wing of humility, drew near unto God.

*Troparion:* Having used humility after the manner of a ladder, the Publican was exalted unto heavenly height; but the wretched Pharisee, having exalted himself with corrupt pride and folly, descended unto the lowest nethermost hell.

*Troparion:* Snaring the righteous, the subtle one steals them away by forms of vainglory; and the sinful he binds with the snares of despair. But fleeing from both these evils, let us strive to emulate the Publican.

*Troparion:* Falling down in prayer unto our God, with tears and fervent sighing, emulating the high-making humility of the Publican, let us sing, O ye faithful: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Glory...*  
*Troparion:* Admonishing Thy disciples, Thou hast said, O Master, not to mind high things, teaching them to condescend unto the humble, O Savior.[^74] Therefore we cry unto Thee, O ye faithful: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Sunday.*

*Both now...*  
*Theotokion:* O pure Lady, we know thee as Jacob's beauty and the divine ladder which he beheld stretched out from below unto the height,[^75] bringing down the incarnate God from on high, and leading earthly men immediately upward.

*Ode 8. Heirmos: From the flame Thou didst pour out dew upon the holy ones, and didst consume with water the sacrifice of the righteous one; for Thou dost accomplish all things, O Christ, only by willing: Thee do we exalt unto all ages!*[^76]  
*Troparion:* By a humble-minded disposition, sighing, the Publican found the Lord merciful and was saved; but by the cruel pattern of a boastful tongue, the Pharisee fell away from righteousness.

*Troparion:* Let us flee, O ye faithful, from the perversity of the Pharisee's boastfulness, and from the naming of purity; emulating well the Publican's pardoned humility and disposition.

*Troparion:* Let us utter the words of the Publican, O ye faithful, in the holy Church: "O God, cleanse me!" that with that man we may obtain forgiveness, being delivered from the ruin of the boastful Pharisee.

*Troparion:* Let us all emulate the groaning of the Publican, and conversing with God, let us cry aloud unto Him with fervent tears: "O Lover of mankind, we have sinned; O Compassionate and Bountiful One, cleanse and save us!"

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord.*  
*Troparion:* God was inclined by the groaning of the Publican; and having justified him, He showed forth unto all that we should ever bow down with sighing and tears, asking for remission of transgressions.

*Both now...*  
*Theotokion:* I know no other protection than thee: I offer thee as an intercessor, O pure and all-immaculate Lady; I stir thee up as a mediatress unto Him Who was born of thee: show me free from all that afflict me!

*Ode 9. Heirmos: It is not possible for men to see God, upon Whom the ranks of Angels dare not gaze; but through thee, O All-Pure One, the Incarnate Word appeared unto mortals: Whom magnifying, with the heavenly hosts we call thee blessed.*[^77]  
*Troparion:* Having received from Christ humility as the way of exaltation and the pattern of salvation, let us emulate the disposition of the Publican...

*Sunday of the Publican and the Pharisee.*

...casting far away the haughtiness of exaltation, and entreating God with a humble-minded disposition.

*Troparion:* Let us cast away pride of soul, and acquire an upright disposition in humility of mind; let us not strive to justify ourselves, but let us hate the boastfulness of vainglory, and entreat God with the Publican.

*Troparion:* Let us offer unto the Fashioner of mercy prayers like the Publican's, turning away from the unacceptable prayers and boastful voices of the Pharisees, which bring judgment upon the neighbor: that we may draw unto ourselves divine mercy and light.

*Troparion:* Weighed down by the accumulation of many transgressions, in the abundance of malice I have surpassed the Publican; and I have acquired the boastful haughtiness of the Pharisee, being utterly destitute of all good things: O Lord, spare me!

*Troparion:* Account those who for Thy sake are poor in spirit worthy of Thy blessedness;[^78] for according to the teaching of Thy commandment, we offer unto Thee a contrite spirit: accept it, O Savior, and save those who serve Thee!

*Glory...*  
*Troparion:* The Publican having of old prayed unto God, having entered into the Temple in faith, went out justified; for having come with sighing and tears, and with contrition of heart, he put off the entire burden of sins through cleansing.

*Both now...*  
*Theotokion:* Grant unto us who worthily honor thee, O All-Pure Lady, and magnify thy birthgiving, to sing, glorify, and bless thee, O only Blessed One; for thou art the boast of Christians and an acceptable intercessor unto God.

*Exapostilarion of the Octoechos; and another from the Triodion.*  
*Tone 2, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Же́ны ѹ҆слы́шите):*[^79]  
*Glory...*

*Sunday.*

Let us flee the most evil boastfulness of the Pharisee, and let us learn the excellent humility of the Publican, that we may be exalted, crying out unto God with him: "Cleanse Thy servants, O Christ the Savior, Who of Thine own will wast born of the Virgin, and didst endure the Cross, and hast saved the world by Thy divine power!"

*Both now... Theotokion, Podoben:*  
The Fashioner of creation and God of all took corruptible flesh from thy spotless womb, O all-hymned Mother of God, and renewed all my corrupt nature, having left thee after childbearing even as thou wast before childbearing; therefore, with faith, praising thee, we all cry out: Rejoice, O glory of the world!

*At the Praises, Resurrectional Stichera: Tone 4; and 1 Anatolian; and 2 from the Triodion. On 6. Tone 3:*  
*Verse: Let every breath praise the Lord.*  
*Sticheron 1, Tone 3:*  
Having understood the difference between the Publican and the Pharisee, O my soul, hate thou the proud voice of the one, but emulate the contrite prayer of the other, and cry aloud: O God, cleanse me a sinner, and have mercy on me!

*Verse: Arise, O Lord my God, let Thy hand be lifted up; forget not Thy poor unto the end (Ps. 9:33 [LXX Ps. 9:33 / MT Ps. 10:12]).*[^80]  
*Repeat the same Sticheron.*

*Verse: I will confess Thee, O Lord, with my whole heart, I will tell of all Thy marvelous works (Ps. 9:2 [LXX Ps. 9:2]).*[^81]  
*Sticheron 2, Tone 3:*  
Having hated the boastful voice of the Pharisee, O ye faithful, and having emulated the contrite prayer of the Publican, not minding high things, let us cry aloud with compunction: O God, cleanse our sins!

*After the others, at the Praises: 4 Stichera of the Octoechos; and 4 of the Triodion. Tone 1: "Let us not pray like the Pharisee..." and "A Pharisee, overcome by vainglory..." (see fol. 2)[^82], not repeating these two.*  
*Glory... Tone 8:*  
Thou hast condemned the Pharisee who justified himself by works, O Lord; and Thou hast justified the Publican who humbled himself and with groanings asked for cleansing. For Thou dost not give heed unto proud thoughts, and dost not despise a contrite heart. Therefore we also unto Thee...

*Sunday of the Publican and the Pharisee.*

...fall down before Thee in humility, Who didst suffer for our sake: grant us forgiveness and great mercy.

*Both now... Theotokion: Most blessed art thou...*  
*Great Doxology. After the Trisagion, the Resurrectional Troparion only. Ectenia, and Dismissal.*  
*And the customary Litiya in the narthex, in which we chant: Glory... Both now... The Morning Gospel Sticheron. And the Studite Catechesis.*  
*And the First Hour.*

*It should be known that all the Morning Gospel Stichera, from this present Sunday until the Sunday of All Saints, are chanted at the Morning Litias in the narthex at "Glory... Both now..."*[^83]

*Let it be known that from this Sunday we begin to read in the domestic dialect,*[^84] *after the dismissal of the Litiya in the narthex, the Catechesis—that is, the Instructions—of our Holy Father Theodore the Studite.*[^85] *And if the Hegumen be present, it is read by him; but if not, by the ecclesiarch, while the brethren stand and listen with attentiveness. And after the completion of the reading, we say the Troparion of the Venerable Theodore, Tone 8: "Instructor of Orthodoxy...";*[^86] *God is the Lord; and the complete Dismissal.*

*AT THE LITURGY. The Beatitudes of the Octoechos; and from the Canon, Ode 6. Prokeimenon and Alleluia of the Tone. Epistle to Timothy, Pericope 296 (2 Tim. 3:10–15). Gospel according to Luke, Pericope 89 (Luke 18:10–14). Communion Hymn: Praise the Lord... (Ps. 148:1).*[^87]

*It should be known that throughout this entire week the thrice-accursed Armenians keep their loathsome fast, which is called Artzivourion.*[^88] *We, however [the monastics], partake of cheese and eggs on every day [this also on Wednesday and Friday, at the ninth hour].*[^89] *And the laity eat meat, overturning their so great and grievous heresy.*

*If the Feast of the Meeting of the Lord fall on Meatfare Saturday:*[^90]  
*The Service for the Departed is chanted in its entirety at the cemetery,*[^91] *both at evening and in the morning. But if there be no cemetery church, it is chanted on the Saturday before the Sunday of the Prodigal Son; and there, on Meatfare Saturday, the service is chanted for the Feast alone.*

*[Narrative Woodcut Illustration: The Parable of the Prodigal Son (Luke 15:11–32). On the left, the younger son departs on horseback; in the background, he tends swine in poverty; in the foreground, the merciful father embraces the returning son, servants bring the first robe and shoes, and the fatted calf is prepared. Inscription: "The Parable of the Prodigal Son. Luke 15."]*

## Sunday of the Prodigal Son

*The beginning of the service and account of the Parable of the Prodigal Son.*

*We celebrate the Vigil as indicated on folio 1 (see fol. 1).*[^92]  
*On Saturday evening, at the Vigil, after the customary Kathisma. At "Lord, I have cried":*  
*Resurrectional Stichera, 3; and Anatolian, 4; and of the Triodion, two idiomela, on 3, repeating the first. Tone 1:*

*Sticheron 1, Tone 1:* Into a sinless and life-giving land was I entrusted; but having sown sin, with the sickle I reaped the ears of sloth, and by handfuls bound the sheaves of my deeds, which I spread not upon the threshing-floor of repentance. But praying unto Thee, our pre-eternal Husbandman and God: with the wind of Thy tender-heartedness winnow the chaff of my deeds, and grant unto my soul wheat—even forgiveness, shutting me into Thy heavenly granary, and save me!

*Sticheron 2, Tone 1:* Let us understand, O brethren, the power of the mystery: for from sin...

*Sunday of the Prodigal Son.*

...having run back unto the fatherly house, the most good Father, coming forth to meet the prodigal son, kisses him, and again bestows the tokens of His own glory, and accomplishes mystical rejoicing for those on high, slaying the fatted calf, that we may live worthily together: unto the Father Who loved mankind and slew the calf, and unto the glorious Sacrifice, the Savior of our souls.

*Glory... Tone 2:*  
Ah, of how many good things have I, wretch that I am, deprived myself! Alas, from what a kingdom have I, passionate one, fallen away! I have squandered the riches which I received; I have transgressed the commandment. Woe is me, impassioned soul! Henceforth shalt thou be condemned unto the eternal fire. Therefore, before the end, cry aloud unto Christ God: "As the prodigal son receive me, O God, and have mercy on me!"

*Both now... Dogmatikon Theotokion of the Tone (Tone 2).*

*AT THE LITIYA, the customary [prayers]. Glory... Tone 4:*  
As the prodigal son have I also come, O Compassionate One, who have spent my entire life in departure; I have squandered the wealth which Thou gavest me, O Father: receive me repenting, O God, and have mercy on me!

*Both now... Theotokion of the same Tone, from the Alphabetical Stichera:*  
Look down upon the supplications of thy servants, O all-immaculate Lady, quelling the uprisings of cruel trials against us; for thee alone do we have as an unshakable and sure foundation, and thy protection have we acquired: let us not be put to shame, O Sovereign Lady, who call upon thee; hasten unto the prayer of those who faithfully cry unto thee: Rejoice, O Sovereign Lady, help of all, joy, and protection of our souls!

*AT THE APOSTICHA. Resurrectional Stichera. Glory... Tone 6:*  
Having squandered the wealth of the Father's gift, I was pastured with irrational beasts, wretch that I am; and desiring their food, I wasted away with hunger without being filled. But having returned unto the tender-hearted Father, I cry aloud with tears:

*Sunday of the Prodigal Son.*

..."Receive me as a hired servant, who fall down before Thy love for mankind, and save me!"

*Both now... Theotokion, Tone 2:*  
My Maker and Redeemer, Christ the Lord, O Most Pure One, having come forth from thy womb, clothed Himself in me, and freed Adam from the first curse. Therefore, O Most Pure One, as the Mother of God and Virgin in truth, we cry unto thee unceasingly with the angelic greeting: Rejoice, O Sovereign Lady, protectress and shelter, and salvation of our souls!

*The rest of the order of the Vigil according to custom, as above (see fol. 3).*[^92]  
*If, however, the Forefeast, or the Feast, or the Afterfeast, or the Apodosis of the Meeting of the Lord should occur: the rubric for this is written in the Sunday of the Publican (see fol. 3).*[^93]

*AT MATINS. After the Sessional Hymn, we read in the Commentary:*[^94] *the Gospel according to Luke concerning the Prodigal Son.*[^95] *After Psalm 50: Reading for the day of Chrysostom:*[^96] *"Beloved, listen to Christ Himself." And we chant the Polyeleos,*[^97] *the customary two psalms; and we add unto them also the third psalm, Psalm 136: "By the rivers of Babylon" (Ps. 136:1–9 [LXX Ps. 136:1–9 / MT Ps. 137:1–9]),*[^98] *with the beautiful Alleluia. And a reading from the same Homily of Chrysostom. Hypakoe, and the rest, as indicated in the preceding Sunday.*  
*Canons, 3: Octoechos on 4; and Triodion on 6, the work of Joseph.*[^99]  
*Ode 1, Tone 2. Heirmos: Moses' song...*[^100]

*Troparion:* O Jesus God, receive now also me repenting, even as the prodigal son, who have lived my entire life in slothfulness, and have provoked Thee to anger.

*Troparion:* The divine riches which Thou gavest me aforetime have I evilly squandered, and have departed far from Thee, living prodigally, O tender-hearted Father: receive me therefore also as I turn back unto Thee.

*Glory...*  
*Troparion:* Having stretched forth now fatherly embraces, receive me also, O Lord, as the prodigal, O All-Compassionate One, that with thanksgiving I may glorify Thee.

*Both now...*  
*Theotokion:* Having shown forth upon me all Thy goodness, O God, overlook the multitude of my transgressions, as the Benefactor, through the divine entreaties of Thy Mother.

*And the other Heirmoi of the Canons that are recited:*  
*Ode 1. Heirmos:* Moses' Song...

*Sunday of the Prodigal Son.*

*Ode 1. Alternate Heirmos:* Having taken up Moses' Song, cry aloud, O soul: He was my helper and protector unto salvation; this is my God, and I will glorify Him (Exod. 15:1–2).[^101]  
*Or:* I shall open my mouth... (Tone 4).[^102]

*Ode 3. Heirmos: My barren mind...*[^103]  
*Troparion:* I have become utterly outside myself, deliriously cleaving unto the inventors of passions; but receive me, O Christ, even as the prodigal.

*Troparion:* Emulating the voice of the prodigal, I cry: "I have sinned, O Father!" As him, therefore, embrace me also now, and cast me not away.

*Glory...*  
*Troparion:* Having mercifully stretched forth Thine arms, O Christ, receive me returning from the far country of sin and passions.

*Both now...*  
*Theotokion:* O fair among women, enrich me also with fair forms of virtue, who am impoverished by many sins, O Pure One, that I may glorify thee.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:* Make my barren mind fruitful, O God, appearing as my good Cultivator and Planter of good things by Thy tender mercy.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "The soldiers keeping watch" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*[^104]  
Hasten to open unto me the Father's embraces! Prodigally have I spent my life; but looking upon the inexhaustible wealth of Thy compassions, O Savior, despise not now my impoverished heart. For unto Thee, O Lord, in compunction I cry: "I have sinned, O Father, against heaven and before Thee!" (Luke 15:18, 21).[^105]

*Glory... The same. Both now... Theotokion:*  
O undefiled, pure Virgin Mother of God, only protectress and shelter of the faithful: deliver from perils, afflictions, and cruel circumstances all who place their hope in thee, O Virgin, and save our souls through thy divine prayers.

*Ode 4. Heirmos: Foreseeing Thy birth from the Virgin...*[^106]  
*Troparion:* The wealth of good things which Thou gavest me, O heavenly Father, have I evilly squandered, being enslaved unto alien citizens; therefore I cry unto Thee: "I have sinned against Thee! Receive me as the prodigal of old, stretching forth Thine embraces unto me!"

*Troparion:* I have been enslaved unto every malice, having wretchedly bowed down unto the workers of passions, and have become outside myself through lack of humility: have compassion...

*Sunday of the Prodigal Son.*

...on me, O Savior, heavenly Father, who flee unto Thy many compassions.

*Glory...*  
*Troparion:* I am filled with all shame, not daring to look up unto the height of heaven, for irrationally have I bowed down unto sin; but now, turning back, I cry aloud with compunction: "I have sinned against Thee: receive me, O King of all!"

*Both now...*  
*Theotokion:* As the help of mercy, the firm hope for all Christians, and the pure refuge of the saved: save me, O Virgin, by thy motherly supplications, and vouchsafe me the life to come.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:* Foreseeing Thy birth from the Virgin, the Prophet preached, crying aloud: "I heard report of Thee, and was afraid; for from Teman, and from the Holy Mount overshadowed, art Thou come, O Christ" (Hab. 3:2–3).[^107]

*Ode 5. Heirmos: The night having passed...*[^108]  
*Troparion:* I was enslaved unto alien citizens, and departed into a corrupting country, and was filled with shame; but now, turning back, I cry unto Thee, O Compassionate One: "I have sinned against Thee!"

*Troparion:* Open unto me now Thy fatherly tender-heartedness, who turn back from evils, O heavenly Father, and cast me not away, Who hast exceeding great mercy.

*Glory...*  
*Troparion:* I dare not look up on high unto the height, for beyond measure have I provoked Thee to anger, O Christ; but knowing Thy merciful compassions, I cry aloud: "I have sinned against Thee: cleanse and save me!"

*Both now...*  
*Theotokion:* O all-holy Virgin full of grace, who didst bear the cleansing of all: lighten the heavy burden of my transgressions through thy prayers.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:* The night having passed, the day hath drawn near, and the Light hath shone forth upon the world; therefore the angelic ranks hymn Thee, and glorify Thee, O Lord God.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:* The depth of transgressions compasses me continually, and the tempestuous surge of sins overwhelms me: pilot me unto the haven of life, O Christ God, and save me, O King of glory![^109]

*Sunday of the Prodigal Son.*

*Troparion:* The fatherly riches have I grievously squandered, and having become impoverished, I am filled with shame, being enslaved unto fruitless thoughts; therefore I cry unto Thee, O Lover of mankind: "Have compassion on me and save me!"

*Glory...*  
*Troparion:* Pining away with hunger for all good things, and having estranged myself from Thee, O All-Good One: have compassion on me who turn back now, and save me, O Christ, who hymn Thy love for mankind.

*Both now...*  
*Theotokion:* Having given birth unto the Savior and Master Christ, vouchsafe me salvation, O Virgin, who am destitute of all good things, O pure Maiden, that I may hymn thy great wonders.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:* In the depth of sins am I held fast, O Savior, and in the abyss of life am I tossed about; but as Jonah from the monster, lead me up also from the passions, and save me (Jonah 2:1–10).[^110]

*Kontakion, Tone 3, Podoben: "Today the Virgin" (Дѣ́ва дне́сь):*[^111]  
From Thy fatherly glory have I senselessly departed, having squandered in evil ways the riches which Thou didst deliver unto me; therefore do I offer unto Thee the voice of the prodigal: "I have sinned before Thee, O compassionate Father! Receive me repenting, and make me as one of Thy hired servants" (Luke 15:18–19).[^112]

*Ikos:* As Christ the Savior daily teaches us with His own voice in the Scriptures, let us hear concerning the prodigal and chaste one again; and by faith let us emulate his good repentance. Unto Him Who knows the secrets of all, with humility of heart let us cry: "We have sinned against Thee, O compassionate Father, and are nevermore worthy to be called Thy children as before; but as Thou art by nature the Lover of mankind, receive me, and make me as one of Thy hired servants!"

*And there is a reading before the Synaxarion in the Menaion, and then in the Triodion.*

*Synaxarion on the Sunday of the Prodigal Son.*[^113]

*Verses:*[^114]  
*If any man be a prodigal, let him even so be of good courage;*  
*For unto all is the door of God's abundant compassion opened.*

On this day we celebrate the recall of the prodigal son, which our most divine Fathers arranged as second in the Triodion for such a reason as this: Forasmuch as there be certain men who, being conscious in themselves of many abominable deeds, and having lived right dissolutely from...

*Sunday of the Prodigal Son.*

...the years of their youth, occupying themselves in drunkenness and debauchery, and having thus fallen into the depth of evils, come unto despair—that is to say, unto the loss of hope concerning their own salvation. Which despair is the fruit and yield of pride; and from thence they are in no wise willing to attain unto striving after virtue, but laying evil upon evil, fall into yet worse vices. The holy Fathers, therefore, bearing themselves kindly and fatherly toward such men, and desiring to turn them away from despair, arranged this present parable here after the first, plucking up by the roots the passion of despair, and stirring them up unto the regaining of virtue, and setting forth through the prodigal the divine mercies, which love mankind and are exceedingly good, toward those who have sinned right grievously; and demonstrating from this parable before Christ's very eyes that there is no sin which can overcome His judgment that loves mankind.

Two, therefore, are the sons of the Man—that is, of the God-Man, the Word: the righteous, and the sinners. The elder, then, is he who abides with Him in the commandments of God and in good order, and in no wise departs from Him. The younger, on the other hand, is he who lusted after sin, and by reason of abominable deeds would not have cohabitation with God, and vainly forfeited the divine love for mankind which he beheld in himself, having lived dissolutely, as one who preserved not whole the image of God wherein he had been created; and having surrendered himself unto insidious wrath, through voluptuous pleasures he ministered unto its desires, yet was not able to satisfy his lust. For sin is a certain insatiable thing, which deceives through habitual custom for the sake of temporal sweetness; and for this cause it is likened unto the carob pods, which are the food of swine. For carob pods at first offer something sweet, but afterward produce something bitter and full of roughness; all of which sin has within itself. Scarcely, therefore, having come to himself and returned unto his senses, the prodigal son, as one who was already perishing from hunger of virtue, comes unto the Father, saying: "Father, I have sinned against heaven and before Thee, and am not worthy to be called Thy son." And He receives him repenting, not upbraiding him, but embracing and enfolding him, He shows forth divine and fatherly mercies, and gives him a garment—Holy Baptism; and a seal and earnest—the Grace of the All-Holy Spirit; and unto this also shoes, that his feet might not be wounded by serpents and scorpions...

*Sunday of the Prodigal Son.*

...treading according to the Gospel, but rather might be able to crush their heads (Luke 10:19).[^115] And afterward, in exceeding great joy, the Father slays for him the fatted calf—His only-begotten Son—and grants him to be a partaker of His Body and Blood; even though the elder son marveled at His unreckonable mercy, and said and uttered many things. Yet the Lover of mankind appeases him also and bids him be silent, entreating him softly with gentle and kind words, saying: "Thou art ever with me, and it is a fitting thing to rejoice and partake of the feast with the Father, for this my son was dead beforehand in sin, and is alive again, having done penance for that which he senselessly committed; and having been lost, having departed from me through the habit of luxuries, he is found by me, who suffered out of mine own mercies, and called him by way of compassion." This parable may also be understood and interpreted concerning the Jewish people and ourselves. For this reason, then, was this parable appointed here by the holy Fathers, plucking up, as was said, despair and the dread of undertaking good deeds, and admonishing him who has sinned like that prodigal unto repentance and sorrow for sins. Which in truth is an exceeding great bulwark against the striking of the adversary's arrows, and a mighty defense.

Through Thine ineffable love for mankind, O Christ our God, have mercy on us. Amen.

*Ode 7. Heirmos: Likening themselves unto the Cherubim...*[^116]  
*Troparion:* Unto bodily pleasures have I bowed down most wretchedly, and have been utterly enslaved unto passionate inventions, and have become estranged from Thee, O Lover of mankind; but now I cry with the voice of the prodigal: "I have sinned, O Christ, despise me not, as Thou alone art plenteous in mercy!"

*Troparion:* I cry aloud: "I have sinned!" in no wise daring to look up unto the height of heaven, O King of all, for in folly have I alone provoked Thee to anger, having rejected Thy commandments; therefore, as Thou alone art good, cast me not away from Thy presence!

*Glory...*  
*Troparion:* Through the supplications of the Apostles of the Lord, and Prophets, and Venerables, and venerable Martyrs...

*Sunday of the Prodigal Son.*

...and Righteous Ones, forgive me all wherein I have sinned, having provoked Thy goodness, O Christ, that I may hymn Thee unto all ages.

*Both now...*  
*Theotokion:* Having appeared more radiant than the Cherubim and Seraphim, O Mother of God, and of all the heavenly hosts, with them entreat Him Whom thou didst incarnate—the divine Word of the unoriginate Father, O all-immaculate one—that we all may be accounted worthy of eternal good things.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:* Likening themselves unto the Cherubim, the Children in the furnace held choral dance, singing: "Blessed art Thou, O God, for in truth and in judgment hast Thou brought all these things upon us by reason of our sins, Who art supremely hymned and supremely blessed unto all ages!" (Dan. 3:27–28).[^117]

*Ode 8. Heirmos: In the bush unto Moses...*[^118]  
*Troparion:* O Thou Who camest down upon the earth to save the world by voluntary poverty through Thine abundant tender mercy: save me, who am now impoverished of every good deed, as Thou art merciful!

*Troparion:* Having departed far from Thy commandments, I have been enslaved all-wretchedly unto delusion; yet receive me who now turn back, as the prodigal of old, who fall down before Thee, O heavenly Father!

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord.*  
*Troparion:* Having clothed myself in corrupting thoughts, I was darkened and departed far from Thee, being utterly outside myself, O All-Compassionate One; therefore save me who fall down before Thee in repentance.

*Both now...*  
*Theotokion:* O pure Birthgiver of God, only restoration of the fallen: restore me also, who am utterly crushed and brought low by all manner of sins.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:* Him Who prefigured the virgin wonder unto Moses in the bush on Mount Sinai of old: praise ye, bless, and supremely exalt unto all ages! (Exod. 3:2; Dan. 3:57).[^119]

*Ode 9. Heirmos: Who among the earthborn hath heard...*[^120]  
*Troparion:* Behold, O Christ, the sorrow of my heart! Behold my turning back! Behold my tears, O Savior, and despise me not; but embrace me again by reason of Thy tender mercy, numbering me among the multitude of the saved, that with thanksgiving I may hymn Thy mercy!

*Sunday of the Prodigal Son.*

*Troparion:* As the thief I cry: "Remember me!" As the Publican, moved to compunction, I beat my breast and call out now: "Cleanse me!" And as the prodigal receive me, O All-Compassionate One; deliver me from all mine evils, that I may sing of Thy supreme condescension!

*Troparion:* Sigh now, O my wretched soul, and cry aloud unto Christ: "O Lord, Who for my sake didst voluntarily become poor: enrich me, who am impoverished of every good deed, with the wealth of virtues, as Thou alone art good and plenteous in mercy!"

*Glory...*  
*Troparion:* The joy which Thou once didst work, O Good One, at the voluntary turning back of the prodigal: work this now also in me, wretch that I am, stretching forth unto me Thy venerable hands, that being saved I may hymn Thy supreme condescension!

*Both now...*  
*Theotokion:* By the radiant lightnings of thy prayers, O Virgin, enlighten the spiritual eyes of my heart, darkened by malice, and lead me unto the paths of repentance, that worthily I may hymn thee who, beyond word, didst incarnate the Word!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:* Who among the earthborn hath heard such a thing, or who hath ever seen, that a Virgin was found having conceived in the womb, and without travail gave birth to a Child? Such is thy wonder, and thee, O Birthgiver of God Mary, do we magnify!

*Exapostilarion: Resurrectional. And of the Triodion:*  
*Tone 2, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Же́ны ѹ҆слы́шите):*[^121]  
The riches which Thou gavest me, O Good One, have I, wretch that I am, having departed unprofitably, evilly squandered, O Savior; having lived prodigally, I dissipated them unto the demons for years. Therefore, receive me as I turn back, even as the prodigal, O Compassionate Father, and save me!

*Another, the same melody:*  
I have squandered and wasted Thy riches, O Lord, and have become subject unto the evil demons, wretch that I am. But O all-merciful Savior, have compassion on the prodigal, and cleanse me who am defiled, restoring unto me the first robe of Thy Kingdom!

*Theotokion, the same melody:*  
O holy Virgin Mother, great proclamation of the Apostles, Martyrs, Prophets, and Venerables: make Thy Son and Lord merciful unto us thy servants, O Birthgiver of God, when He shall sit to judge each according unto his desert!

*Sunday of the Prodigal Son.*

*AT THE PRAISES. Resurrectional Stichera, 4; and Anatolian, 1; and of the Triodion, 3 idiomela. Tone 2. Verse: Let every breath praise the Lord (Ps. 150:6):*

*Sticheron 1, Tone 2:* The voice of the prodigal I offer unto Thee, O Lord: I have sinned before Thine eyes, O Good One; I have squandered the wealth of Thy gifts; but receive me repenting, O Savior, and save me!

*Sticheron 2, Tone 4. Verse: Arise, O Lord my God, let Thy hand be lifted up; forget not Thy poor unto the end (Ps. 9:33 [LXX Ps. 9:33 / MT Ps. 10:12]):*  
As the prodigal son have I also come, O Compassionate One, having spent my entire life in departure; I have squandered the riches which Thou gavest me, O Father: receive me repenting, O God, and have mercy on me!

*Sticheron 3, Tone 8. Verse: I will confess unto Thee, O Lord, with my whole heart; I will tell of all Thy marvelous works (Ps. 9:2 [LXX Ps. 9:2 / MT Ps. 9:1]):*  
Having squandered prodigally the wealth of my father's substance, and having dissipated it, I became destitute, dwelling in the land of wicked citizens; and no longer enduring to dwell together with them, turning back I cry unto Thee, the Compassionate Father: "I have sinned against heaven and before Thee, and am no more worthy to be called Thy son; make me as one of Thy hired servants, O God, and have mercy on me!" (Luke 15:18–19).[^122]

*Glory... Tone 6:*  
O Good Father, I have departed far from Thee; forsake me not, neither show me to be unprofitable unto Thy Kingdom! The all-wicked enemy hath stripped me naked, and hath taken my wealth; the gifts of my soul hath he prodigally dissipated. Having arisen, therefore, and turned back, I cry unto Thee: make me as one of Thy hired servants, O Thou Who for my sake didst stretch forth Thine all-pure hands upon the Cross, that snatching me away from the cruel beast, Thou mayest clothe me in the first robe, as Thou alone art plenteous in mercy![^123]

*Both now... Theotokion: Most blessed art thou...*  
*Great Doxology. Ectenia, and Dismissal.*  
*And the customary Litiya, at which we chant: Glory... Both now... The Morning Gospel Sticheron. And the Catechesis is read. And the First Hour in the narthex, and the complete Dismissal.*

*Sunday of the Prodigal Son.*

*AT THE LITURGY. The Beatitudes of the Octoechos on 6; and from the Triodion, on 4. Prokeimenon and Alleluia of the Tone. Epistle for the 34th Sunday, to the Corinthians, Pericope 135 (1 Cor. 6:12–20). Gospel according to Luke, 16th Sunday, Pericope 79 (Luke 15:11–32). Communion Hymn: Praise the Lord... (Ps. 148:1); Another: I will take the cup of salvation... (Ps. 115:4 [LXX Ps. 115:4 / MT Ps. 116:13]).*[^124]

*It should be known that in Meatfare Week we do not chant Alleluia. And we cut off also one Kathisma of the Psalter from Matins, and say it at Vespers. The Canticles from the Psalter we do not say. We omit also the Mid-Hours, and the Molieben Canon unto the All-Pure Birthgiver of God chanted at Compline. And Small Compline is chanted. We do likewise also in Cheesefare Week, except on Wednesday and Friday; but concerning all this, seek the rubric there. And throughout the entire week, on each day at Matins, we read the Homily of St. Macarius concerning fathers and monastics,*[^125] *its incipit being: "My little children, my heart rejoices for your sake."*

*If the Feast of the Meeting fall on the Sunday of the Prodigal Son, or on Monday, or on Tuesday of Meatfare Week: the Feast is surrendered [its Apodosis occurs] on Friday of the same week. If, however, the Meeting of the Lord fall on Wednesday, or on Thursday, or on Friday, or on Meatfare Saturday: the Feast is surrendered on Tuesday of Cheesefare Week.*[^126]

*MEATFARE THURSDAY.*  
*If the Feast of the Patron Saint of the temple, of a holy man or a holy woman, fall on Meatfare Saturday: then we chant the service in the temple on Saturday without postponement; and the Saturday service [for the Departed] we chant on Meatfare Thursday.*

*MEATFARE FRIDAY.*  
*If the Forefeast of the Meeting occur: for the Apodosis of the Meeting does not take place. And we omit the service of the Afterfeast of the Meeting and Meatfare Saturday; and we chant the service of the Forefeast of the Meeting beforehand on Friday of that week, together with the holy [Unmercenaries] Cyrus and John and with the Triodion. At "Lord, I have cried": Stichera of the Forefeast, 3; and of the Unmercenaries, 3; Glory... of the Saints; Both now... of the Forefeast. At Matins: Canon of the Forefeast with the Heirmoi on 6; to the Saints, with the refrains. Triodion: Stichera at the Praises we chant on 4. And to the Meeting itself we chant on Meatfare Sunday. And the service for the departed we chant in order, according to custom, on Meatfare Saturday, as always.*

*The Apodosis of the Meeting on Meatfare Saturday (see fol. 30, 32)*[^127] *does not take place; but the Feast is surrendered on this present Friday, which is of Meatfare.*

*If the Finding of the Head of John the Forerunner, or of any of the Great Saints, fall on Meatfare Saturday: the entire feast of the Forerunner is chanted beforehand on this Meatfare Friday. Likewise also for a Great Saint.*

*Let it be known that the order of the Saints occurring on Meatfare Saturday and Sunday is chanted at the preceding Compline. Do likewise concerning Cheesefare Saturday and Sunday, or as the Superior wills; except where the memory of a Great Saint occurs.*

*[Narrative Woodcut Illustration: Commemoration of all departed Orthodox Christians. Depiction of the diverse manners of human death: on the left, men falling from cliffs, attacked and devoured by wild beasts, and drowning in turbulent waters; in the center, violent death in war by the sword, falls from heights, and destruction by fire; on the right, peaceful Christian dormition, showing a pious man upon his deathbed attended by a priest vested in epitrachelion administering the Holy Mysteries, with a candle and burial rites. Inscription above the woodcut: "Concerning those who have reposed in piety through diverse manners of death."]*[^128]

## Meatfare Saturday

*We perform the commemoration of all Orthodox Christians departed from the ages, our fathers and brethren.*[^129]

*ON FRIDAY EVENING. After the customary Kathisma:*  
*At "Lord, I have cried": Stichera of the Octoechos, 3; and of the Triodion, 3. Tone 8, Podoben: "In Eden of old" (И҆́же въ є҆де́мѣ):*[^130]

*Sticheron 1, Tone 8, Podoben: "In Eden of old" (И҆́же въ є҆де́мѣ):* Today, O faithful, making commemoration by name of all the dead from the ages who lived in piety in the faith: let us hymn the Savior and Lord, earnestly beseeching for them that in the hour of judgment they may give a good defense unto Him, our very God Who judges all the earth, that having obtained a place at His right hand in joy, in the portion of the Righteous and in the radiant lot of the Saints, they may be accounted worthy of His heavenly Kingdom!

*Meatfare Saturday.*

*Sticheron 2:* O Savior, Who with Thine own Blood didst redeem mankind, and by Thy death didst deliver us from bitter death, and by Thy Resurrection dost grant us eternal life: give rest, O Lord, unto all who have reposed in piety, whether in deserts, or in cities, or in the sea, or upon the earth, or in every place—kings, hierarchs, and chief priests, monastics, and laymen—in every age of all humankind, and account them worthy of Thy heavenly Kingdom!

*Sticheron 3:* By Thy rising from the dead, O Christ, death no longer has dominion over those who have died in piety. Therefore we earnestly pray: give rest unto Thy servants in Thy courts and in the bosom of Abraham, from Adam even unto this day, who have purely served Thee—our fathers and brethren, friends and kinsmen together, every man who in life served faithfully and hath passed away unto Thee in diverse manners and manifold ways, O God, and account them worthy of Thy heavenly Kingdom!

*Glory... Tone 8:*  
I weep and lament when I contemplate death, and behold lying in the graves our beauty, fashioned according unto the image of God, become shapeless, inglorious, devoid of form! O marvel! What is this mystery that hath befallen us? How have we been surrendered unto corruption? How have we been yoked unto death? Truly by the command of God, as it is written, Who grants rest unto the departed![^131]

*Both now... Dogmatikon Theotokion of the Tone (Tone 8): The King of heaven, through His love for mankind...*

*Instead of the Prokeimenon, we chant Alleluia in Tone 8. Verse: Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken unto Thyself, O Lord, and their memorial is from generation to generation (Ps. 64:5; 101:13 [LXX Ps. 64:5; 101:13 / MT Ps. 65:4; 102:12]). Verse: Their souls shall dwell amid good things (Ps. 24:13 [LXX Ps. 24:13 / MT Ps. 25:13]).*[^132]

*AT THE APOSTICHA. Martyrion, 1; and two Idiomela for the Departed by Kyr John of Damascus...*

*Meatfare Saturday.*

*...Damascene, of the Tone in order. And we chant the refrains aforesaid: Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen... and: Their souls shall dwell amid good things.*

*Glory... Tone 6. Idiomelon:*  
My beginning and my substance was Thy creating command; for wishing to fashion me as a living creature from invisible and visible nature, having fashioned my body from the earth, Thou didst give me a soul by Thy divine and life-creating breathing. Therefore, O Savior, give rest unto Thy servants in the lands of the living and in the dwellings of the Righteous![^133]

*Both now... Theotokion, the same Tone:*  
Through the prayers of her who gave Thee birth, O Christ, and of Thy Martyrs, Apostles, Prophets, Hierarchs, Venerables, and Righteous Ones, and of all the Saints: give rest unto Thy departed servants!

*Troparion, Tone 8:*  
O Thou Who by depth of wisdom dost order all things in love for mankind, and dispensest unto all that which is profitable, O only Creator: give rest, O Lord, unto the souls of Thy servants, for they placed their hope in Thee, our Maker, Fashioner, and God![^134]  
*Glory... the end of the Troparion:* For they placed their hope in Thee, our Maker, Fashioner, and God.  
*Both now... Theotokion:*  
Thee do we have as a rampart and a haven, and an acceptable intercessor before God Whom thou didst bear, O unwedded Virgin Birthgiver of God, and the salvation of the faithful!  
*Ectenia, and Dismissal.*

*After the dismissal of Vespers, we celebrate in the narthex the Panikhida over the Kolyvo for the departed according to custom. Canon from the Octoechos for the Departed, of the Tone in order. And the Priest goes unto the cemetery church,*[^135] *and there performs likewise; and the Dismissal.*

*CONCERNING THE FEAST OF THE MEETING AND THE FINDING OF THE HEAD OF JOHN THE FORERUNNER.*  
*If the Forefeast of the Meeting or the Feast of the Finding of the Head of John the Forerunner fall on Meatfare Saturday: the rubric for this is on folios 38 and 30 (see fol. 38, 30).*[^136]  
*If, however, the Feast of the Meeting of the Lord fall on Meatfare Saturday: the service is chanted for the Feast alone on Meatfare Saturday. The service for the departed is chanted in its entirety in the cemetery church; but if there be no cemetery church, then on the preceding Saturday (see fol. 30, 17).*[^137]  
*If there be the Afterfeast of the Meeting on Meatfare Saturday: then we omit the service of the Afterfeast, and chant the Saturday service of the departed. The Apodosis of the Meeting on Meatfare Saturday does not take place.*

*Meatfare Saturday.*

*If the Patronal Feast of a Saint fall on Meatfare Saturday: see the rubric on the preceding Thursday, which is of Meatfare, folios 38 and 24 (see fol. 38, 24).*[^138]

*AT MATINS. After the Six Psalms: Alleluia in Tone 8. And we say the verses: Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen... Verse: Their souls shall dwell amid good things; written at Vespers. Troparion, Tone 8: O Thou Who by depth of wisdom..., twice; Glory... Both now... Theotokion: Thee do we have as a rampart and a haven... And the customary Kathisma: The Lord said unto my Lord... (Ps. 109 [LXX Ps. 109 / MT Ps. 110]); and after it, the Martyria Sessional Hymns in the Octoechos of the Tone of the week, three; Glory... Rest... Both now... Theotokion: the first Resurrectional [Theotokion] of the Tone. And the Homilies concerning the Departed are read: on the First Epistle to the Corinthians concerning the end, the Commentary of Chrysostom (see fol. 54);*[^139] *and another Homily of the same Father, its incipit being: "Word is often wont to console pain" (see fol. 44);*[^140] *both these and other Homilies of John, and works of Andrew (see fol. 42);*[^141] *and the Homily of Gregory the Hieromonk, its incipit being: "The celebration of the memory of those who have reposed in faith..."*[^142]

*After the reading we chant the Amomos (Ps. 118 [LXX Ps. 118 / MT Ps. 119]),*[^143] *with the refrains: Blessed art Thou, O Lord; in Tone 2, in two stases. At "Glory..." we do not chant Alleluia, but verses for the departed. After the First Stasis, the Ectenia:*

**Deacon:** Again and again in peace let us pray unto the Lord.  
**Choir:** Lord, have mercy.  
**Deacon:** Again we pray for the remission of the sins of the servants of God reposed in blessed memory and commemorated by us: the most holy Ecumenical Patriarchs; the pious Kings and Queens; the most reverend Metropolitans; the right-believing Great Princes and Princesses; the God-loving Archbishops and Bishops; and the entire priestly and monastic order, our fathers and brethren lying here [and here inscribed in the memorial books],*[^144] and all Orthodox Christians everywhere, for whom we make commemoration.  
**Choir:** Lord, have mercy.  
**Deacon:** That they may be forgiven... That the Lord God... The mercy of God and the Kingdom of Heaven...  
**Choir:** Lord, have mercy (forty times).  
*Whenever the Priest says the diaconal petitions after a Kathisma, or at the Canon, when he commemorates the departed. After this, Exclamation:*  
**Priest:** For Thou art the resurrection, the life, and the repose...

*And we chant the Second Stasis, at which the refrain is: O Savior, save me!*  
*At the end: Let my soul live... three times (Ps. 118:175 [LXX Ps. 118:175 / MT Ps. 119:175]).*  
*Troparia of the Amomos (Evlogitaria for the Departed), Tone 5. Refrain: Blessed art Thou, O Lord, teach me Thy statutes! (Ps. 118:12 [LXX Ps. 118:12 / MT Ps. 119:12]):*[^145]

*Troparion:* The choir of the Saints hath found the Fountain of Life and the door of Paradise; may I also find the way through repentance: I am the lost sheep; call me, O Savior, and save me!

*Blessed art Thou, O Lord, teach me Thy statutes!*

*Troparion:* O Thou Who of old didst fashion me out of nothingness by Thine hand, and didst honor me with Thine own divine image, but by reason of transgression of Thy commandment didst return me again...

*Meatfare Saturday.*

...unto the earth from whence I was taken, lead me back unto that which is according to Thy likeness, that I may be adorned again with mine ancient beauty!

*Blessed art Thou, O Lord!*

*Troparion:* I am the image of Thine ineffable glory, even though I bear the wounds of transgressions: have compassion upon Thine own creation, O Master, and cleanse me by Thy tender mercy; and grant unto me the longed-for homeland, making me again a citizen of Paradise!

*Blessed art Thou, O Lord, teach me Thy statutes!*

*Troparion:* Ye who preached the Lamb of God, and were slain as lambs, and have been translated unto the ageless and ever-existing life, O holy Martyrs: entreat Him earnestly that He may grant unto us remission of debts!

*Blessed art Thou, O Lord, teach me Thy statutes!*

*Troparion:* Ye who walked the narrow and sorrowful way; all ye who in life took up the Cross as a yoke, and followed Me in faith: come and receive the honors and heavenly crowns which I have prepared for you!

*Blessed art Thou, O Lord!*

*Troparion:* Give rest, O God, unto Thy servants, and establish them in Paradise, where the choirs of Thy Saints, O Lord, and the Righteous shine as stars; give rest unto Thy departed servants, overlooking all their transgressions!

*Glory...*  
*Troparion:* The tri-radiant light of the one Godhead let us piously hymn, crying aloud: Holy art Thou, O unoriginate Father, and co-unoriginate Son, and Divine Spirit! Enlighten us who in faith serve Thee, and snatch us away from the eternal fire!

*Both now...*  
*Theotokion:* Rejoice, O pure one, who didst bear God in the flesh without seed unto the salvation of all, through whom the human race has found salvation; may we also find Paradise, O pure and blessed Birthgiver of God!

*Alleluia, alleluia, [alleluia,] glory to Thee, O God! (thrice).*

*We chant the Ectenia for the Departed aforesaid: Again and again... Choir: Lord, have mercy (forty times). The Priest says the prayer: "O God of spirits and of all flesh..."*[^146] *And after the Exclamation, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5 (For the Departed):*  
Give rest, O our Savior, unto Thy servants with the Righteous, and settle them in Thy courts, as it is written; overlooking, as Thou art good, their transgressions, voluntary and involuntary, known and unknown, O Lover of mankind![^147]  
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:* O Christ God, Who didst shine forth upon the world from the Virgin, and through her didst reveal sons of light: have mercy on us!

*And we read the Homily concerning the Departed in the Commentary of Chrysostom, that on the First Epistle to the Corinthians at the end (see fol. 54).*[^148]  
*And we say also the Biblical Canticles: Let us sing unto the Lord...*[^149]
