*CANON of the Saint to whom the temple is dedicated, with the Heirmos, on 6; and of the Triodion, on 8. And we chant also the Second Ode in the Triodion on 8; but the Second Biblical Canticle from the Psalter we do not say, but the rest we say.*  
*Canon, the work of the Studite [St. Theodore the Studite],*[^150] *its acrostic in the Triadica and Theotokia being: "Of unworthy Theodore."*[^151]  
*Tone 8, Ode 1. Heirmos:*

*Meatfare Saturday.*

*Ode 1, Tone 8. Heirmos:* Let us, O peoples, sing a song unto our marvelous God, Who freed Israel from servitude, singing and crying a song of victory: let us sing unto Thee, the only Master! (Exod. 15:1–2).[^152]  
*Refrain:* Give rest, O Lord, unto the souls of Thy departed servants!

*Troparion:* Let us all pray unto Christ, making commemoration today of the dead from the ages, that He may deliver from the eternal fire those who have reposed in faith and in the hope of eternal life!

*Troparion:* By the depth of Thy judgments, O Christ, most wisely hast Thou foreordained for each the end of life, its place and manner: therefore, those whom the grave has covered in every land, save at the judgment, O All-Compassionate One!

*Troparion:* O Thou Who hast established the boundaries of our life, reveal those who have reposed from the night of this life to be sons of the day that knows no evening, O Lord: Orthodox priests, kings, and all Thy people!

*Troparion:* Those whom the water covered, and war mowed down; those whom an earthquake overtook, and murderers slew; and those whom fire consumed: rank the faithful, O Merciful One, in the portion of the Righteous!

*Troparion:* Overlooking all our carnal debts, O our Savior, at every age of all the race of mankind, set them uncondemned before Thy judgment-seat as they give answer unto Thee, their Creator!

*Glory... Triadicon:*  
Three of one nature I hymn, distinct persons united: the unbegotten Father, the begotten Son, and the Holy Spirit—one unoriginate kingdom, authority, and Godhead!

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
Truly hast thou been revealed as a Heaven upon earth, greater than the highest heaven, O unwedded Virgin; for from thee has shone forth the Sun in the world, reigning in righteousness!

*Katavasia: (see fol. 33).*[^153]

*Ode 2. Heirmos:* See, see that I am your God, begotten of the Father before the ages, and in the last times incarnate without man of the Virgin, and Who destroyed the sin of the Forefather Adam, as I love mankind! (Deut. 32:39).[^154]

*Meatfare Saturday.*

*Troparion:* "See, see that I am your God, Who by righteous judgment hast established the boundaries of life, receiving into incorruption from corruption all who have reposed in hope of resurrection!" (Deut. 32:39).[^155]

*Troparion:* From the four ends of the earth, O Lord, receiving those who have reposed in faith—in the sea, or upon the earth, or in rivers, springs, or lakes, or in pits, who became food for beasts, and birds, and reptiles: give rest unto all!

*Troparion:* In Thine own hand holding all things, O Lord, Thou hast foreordained those who have dissolved into the four elements; at Thy coming, having gathered them together, resurrect them all, forgiving their transgressions committed in ignorance or knowledge!

*Troparion:* O fearful second coming of Thine, O Lord! For coming upon the earth like lightning, Thou shalt raise up all Thy creation to be judged: account those who have lived in faith in Thee then, having gone out to meet Thee, to be with Thee!

*Glory... Triadicon:*  
O all-perfect Unity, supremely divine and in three Persons, unbegotten Father, and only-begotten Son, and Spirit Who proceedest from the Father and art revealed through the Son: one Essence and Nature, Lordship and Kingdom, save us all!

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
Ineffable is the wonder of thy childbearing, O Virgin Mother! For how dost thou give birth, yet remainest pure therein? How dost thou bear a Child, yet knowest no experience of man at all? As the Word of God, marvelously born of thee beyond nature, knows!

*Katavasia: See, see... (Deut. 32:39).*

*Ode 3. Heirmos:* O Word of God, Who with Thine own hand didst establish the heavens: in the light of Thy true knowledge establish the hearts of us who place our hope in Thee! (Ps. 101:26 [LXX Ps. 101:26 / MT Ps. 102:25]; 1 Sam. [1 Kgs.] 2:2).[^156]

*Meatfare Saturday.*

*Troparion:* Account those who have traversed the course of life, O God, to be clothed in the crown of righteousness in pious glory, and to delight in heavenly blessings!

*Troparion:* Give rest, O God, when Thou shalt try all things by fire, unto those who were snatched away suddenly, consumed by lightning, frozen by frost, and smitten by every manner of wound!

*Troparion:* Those who have sailed across the ever-troubled sea of life, steered by an Orthodox way of life, vouchsafe, O Christ, to reach the incorruptible haven of Thy life!

*Troparion:* Those who were consumed as food by every manner of marine creatures and birds of heaven, O Christ, resurrect by Thy judgments in glory on the last day, O God!

*Glory... Triadicon:*  
In spirit I unite the Divine Unity simply in three Persons together, indivisible in severance; for even as the speed of lightning, so the tri-radiant Persons are beheld in oneness!

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
Incomprehensible is thy wonder! For without man thou dost give birth, and after childbearing, O pure one, thou keepest thy virginity intact; therefore the multitude of Angels and the race of mankind sing of thee unto the ages!

*Katavasia: O Word of God, Who with Thine own hand didst establish... Kontakion of the temple.*

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 5, Podoben: "The Word co-unoriginate" (Собезнача́льное Сло́во):*[^157]  
O Thou Who for our sake didst endure the Cross and death, and didst slay Hades and raise the dead with Thyself: give rest, O Savior, unto those who have departed from among us, as Thou art God Who lovest mankind; and at Thy trembling and fearful coming, O Giver of life, as Thou possessest a multitude of mercy, vouchsafe them Thy Kingdom!  
*Glory... the same. Both now... Theotokion:*  
Show forth thy swift protection, help, and mercy upon thy servants, and calm the waves of vain thoughts, O pure one; and raise up my fallen soul, O Birthgiver of God: for I know, yea, I know, O Virgin, that thou canst do whatsoever thou desirest!

*And the reading.*[^158] *Ode 4. Heirmos:*

*Meatfare Saturday.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:* O Word, the Prophet with godlike vision beheld the overshadowing mountain, the only Birthgiver of God, from whom Thou wast to become incarnate, and with trembling glorified Thy power (Hab. 3:3).[^159]

*Troparion:* Fathers and forefathers, grandfathers and great-grandfathers, from the first even unto the last, who have died in righteousness of law and in true faith: remember them all, O our Savior!

*Troparion:* Those who departed life in faith on a mountain, on a journey, in inhabited places, and in wildernesses—monks and laymen, youths and elders—settle, O Christ, with Thy Saints!

*Troparion:* Those who departed unexpectedly from sorrow and joy, who in faith have exchanged this earthly life, who suffered in prosperity or in adversity: give rest unto them all, O our Savior!

*Troparion:* Those whom the sword slew, and whom a horse carried away, whom hail, snow, and torrential rain overwhelmed, whom a brick crushed, or dust buried: give rest, O Christ our Savior!

*Glory... Triadicon:*  
Marvellously the Godhead is one and three, entirely undivided according to each Person: for He is Father, Son, and Holy Spirit, worshipped as one God!

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
Direct us by thy prayers, steering us who are storm-tossed by the wave of sin into saving havens, O Mother of God, delivering us from all grievous tribulations!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:* My spirit wakes early unto Thee, O God, for the commandments of Thy coming are light; therein enlighten our mind, O Master, and guide us into the path of life! (Isa. 26:9).[^160]

*Troparion:* Making commemoration today of the departed who in piety died from the ages, O Lord, earnestly we cry unto Thee: give rest unto all with Thy Saints!

*Troparion:* From every generation and generations, those whom Thou hast received among kings and princes, or among monastics who lived in an Orthodox manner, O Compassionate One: deliver them from eternal torment!

*Meatfare Saturday.*

*Troparion:* Knowing that which is profitable for all whom Thou hast fashioned, and whom Thou hast permitted, O Lord, to die unexpectedly by sudden falls: deliver them from all torment, O our God!

*Troparion:* From the fire ever-burning, and from the darkness without light, the gnashing of teeth, and the worm tormenting without end, and from every punishment deliver, O our Savior, all who have died in faith!

*Glory... Triadicon:*  
O co-enthroned, unoriginate Unity, oneness in division, having one nature conversely in Persons: gather us together into the one desire of Thy commandments!

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
More honorable than the flame-formed Seraphim hast thou been revealed, O pure one, having borne Jesus the Savior, Who is inaccessible unto them, and Who by incarnation deified the earthly mixture of mortal men!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:* Receive me, O Lover of mankind, who am held fast by my transgressions, and fall down before Thy compassions, even as the Prophet, O Lord, and save me! (Jonah 2:1–9).[^161]

*Troparion:* Having dissolved the pangs of death, having risen, O Author of life and our God: give rest unto Thy servants who have reposed from the ages!

*Troparion:* By Thine unutterable judgments, those whom magical potions, poisons, and sudden stranglings have slain: give rest with the Saints, O Lord!

*Troparion:* When Thou shalt judge, and all creation shall stand naked and uncovered before Thy face: then, O Compassionate One, spare those who have served Thee faithfully, O God!

*Troparion:* When Thine Archangel shall sound with the last trumpet for the resurrection of life of all men: then, O Christ, give rest unto Thy servants!

*Troparion:* From the ages, all the faithful whom Thou hast received, O God, every race of mortal...

*Meatfare Saturday.*

...men, vouchsafe unto the ages to glorify Thee together with those who serve Thee!

*Glory... Triadicon:*  
O Godhead three in Persons, co-enthroned, Father, Son, with the Holy Spirit: Thou art my God, holding all things together by Thine almighty power!

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
Leap for joy, O Forefather Jesse, for from thy root has blossomed the Flower of life: Christ God, Who saves the world through the pure Maiden! (Isa. 11:1).[^162]

*Kontakion, Tone 8:* With the Saints give rest, O Christ, unto the souls of Thy servants, where there is neither sickness, nor sorrow, nor sighing, but life everlasting![^163]

*Ikos:* Thou alone art immortal, Who didst make and fashion man: for we mortals were formed from the earth, and unto the selfsame earth shall we return, even as Thou didst command, Who didst fashion me and say unto me: "Dust thou art, and unto dust shalt thou return!" (Gen. 3:19); whither all we mortals depart, making over the tomb our lamentation the song: Alleluia![^164]

*Synaxarion on the Commemoration of the Departed.*[^165]  
*Verses:*  
Remember not, O Word, the sins of the departed,  
Leaving not Thy good compassions unrevealed unto the dead.

On this day, the most divine Fathers enacted that commemoration should be celebrated for all persons who from the ages have ended their lives in piety, for the following reason: because some often underwent an untimely and premature death while abroad in strange lands—that is to say, in the sea, in impassable and uninhabited mountains, in clefts of rocks, and in chasms of the earth; or suffered death by famines, pestilential plagues, wars, conflagrations, frosts, and every other manner of mortality. Likewise, because some, being poor and destitute, did not attain unto the prescribed psalms, hymns, and memorial diptychs, the divine Fathers, being moved with compassion, handed down and instituted as a rule that the Catholic Church should celebrate a common commemoration for all these together, having themselves received this tradition first from the Holy Apostles; so that those who, by reason of any circumstance, could not individually obtain these prescribed rites and suffrages, might be admitted and received...

*Meatfare Saturday.*

...unto this present general commemoration. Demonstrating also thereby that what is performed on their behalf gains for them great benefit. According to the first manner, therefore, God's Church performs commemorations for the souls. According to the second manner, moreover, since they desired to set Christ's Second Coming before our eyes on the morrow, it is fitting also to celebrate commemoration for the souls, as it were imploring the fearful and righteous Judge, and avoiding negligence, that He might exercise His customary unhindered compassion toward them, and settle and establish them in the promised delight. In another understanding, moreover, as the Saints wished to set forth the expulsion of Adam on the coming Sunday, they intend as it were a kind of rest and completion of all things that have been done concerning us through this present rest; so that from henceforward they might begin anew as from the beginning: for finally, an examination will be made by the incorruptible Judge concerning all other deeds from all hours of our life; and furthermore, that by inspiring awe in men thereby, they might make them ready and capable for the struggles of the Fast.

On Saturday, moreover, we always celebrate the commemoration of souls, because *Sabbaton*—that is, Saturday—signifies "rest" in Hebrew. And concerning the deceased, seeing that they have rested and ceased from worldly matters pertaining to temporal life and from all other affairs, we make supplications and prayers for them on the day of rest; which is canonical and upheld, that it should take place every Saturday. Nevertheless, today we make commemoration collectively, praying for every pious soul. For the divine Fathers knew that these commemorations performed for the departed—that is to say, alms and Liturgies—bring them great relief and benefit; and they commanded that this be done both privately and in common, that is, in the assembly in church, having received this from the Holy Apostles, as has been said. As also Dionysius the Areopagite says.[^166] And that that which is performed on their behalf brings benefit to souls is evident both from many other true proofs, and no less from the history concerning Saint Macarius,[^167] who, walking along the road, found the dry skull of a godless pagan man, and asked whether they ever had any comfort or relief in hell; and that skull answered, saying: "They have great alleviation and relief when thou prayest for the departed, O Father!" For that great Saint was wont to do this...

*Meatfare Saturday.*

...and he prayed to God, desiring to receive knowledge whether such benefit came from thence unto souls that had previously departed. Moreover, Gregory the Dialogist saved Emperor Trajan through prayer,[^168] having heard from God that he should never thenceforth pray for the ungodly. Furthermore, Empress Theodora snatched the abhorrent Theophilus from torments and saved him through holy men and confessors, as is recorded in history.[^169] Gregory the Theologian also commends gifts for the dead as good, in his Funeral Oration which he delivered for his brother Caesarius.[^170]

The great Chrysostom, moreover, in his Epistle to the Philippians speaks thus: "Let us consider the benefit of those who have departed; let us give them present assistance—alms, I say, and oblations. For this thing brings them great traffic and exceedingly great profit and benefit. For not in vain nor by chance was this enacted and handed down unto the Church of God by His all-wise Disciples: that at the dread Mysteries the priest should make remembrance of those who have fallen asleep in faith." And again: "In thy disposition and testament with thy children and kin, let thy deed contain fellowship with the Judge's name; let it contain also remembrance of the poor, and I will be guarantor for them!"[^171]

The renowned Athanasius also says: "Even if he who ended his life in piety were scattered into the air, do not neglect to light oil and candles at his grave, calling upon Christ God: for these things are acceptable unto God and bring a great reward. And if the departed be a sinner, that thou mayest loose his sins; but if he be righteous, that an increase of reward may be given him. If, moreover, there be one who, being a stranger and destitute, has none who in such circumstances would have care and solicitude for him: then God, being righteous and good, grants unto him also, because of his destitution, His mercy according to that which He Himself knows beyond our comprehension. In another manner, also, he who offers oblations for such ones becomes a partaker of the reward, because he showed love for the relief of his neighbor: even as he who anoints another with fragrant oil smells sweetly thereof himself first. But as for those who do not fulfill that which was entrusted and committed unto them and the testaments in such matters, they shall assuredly have judgment! Until, therefore, the Second Coming of Christ shall arrive, whatsoever is performed for the dead brings benefit, as the divine Fathers say, and most of all unto those..."

*Meatfare Saturday.*

...unto those who performed even a little of good works: seeing that living, they were found in negligence. And although divine Scripture relates that some dishes are sour (as is proper and profitable) for the instruction and reformation of many men, nevertheless God's love for mankind preponderates most of all. For if an equal balance of good and evil deeds be found, God's goodness toward man outweighs the scale; and if the scale be weighed down somewhat with evil deeds and tilt downward, the selfsame mercy and generosity of God holds it back again.[^172]

It is necessary to know also that all being there will recognize one another—even those whom they do not know now, and whom they have never seen: as the divine Chrysostom says, proving and confirming this from the Gospel Parable of the Rich Man and Lazarus (Luke 16:19–31).[^173] Yet not by any bodily form, for all will have one stature and age, nor will they receive that recognition from congenital appearance, but by the clear and piercing eye of the soul; as the Theologian also says in his Funeral Oration upon Caesarius: "Then, he says, the joyous and glorified appearance of Caesarius was revealed unto me, as it were often in a dream, O dearest brother!"[^174]

Moreover, the great and most noble Athanasius—though he says not so in his *Questions to Prince Antiochus*, yet in his *Discourse on the Departed* he affirms—that until the general resurrection of all from the dead, it is granted unto the Saints to know one another and to rejoice together; but the sinners will not have even this.[^175]

Unto the holy Martyrs also it is granted to look upon the things that take place among us, and to visit us. Therefore at that time one will recognize another, since the hidden and concealed deeds of all will be exposed.

It is necessary to know also that now the souls of the righteous dwell in certain appointed places, and those of sinners contrariwise: the former truly rejoicing in hope, but the latter grieving in expectation of terrible torments. For the Saints have not yet received the promises of good things, as the divine Apostle says: "God having provided some better thing for us, that they without us should not be made perfect" (Heb. 11:40).[^176]

It is necessary also to know that not all those who fall into precipices, into fire, and into the sea, and depart this life through the aforesaid mortal accidents—such as cold, hunger, and the like—suffer this according to God's commandment. For these are the judgments of God, whereof...

*Meatfare Saturday.*

...some occur according to His good pleasure, and others according to His permission. Therein also they happen for knowledge, for warning, and for the amendment of others; and by His foreknowing Word He knows and understands all things, and they happen by His will, even as the Holy Gospel speaks concerning the sparrows (Matt. 10:29). He does not predestine, therefore, that it must be so, except that it happens to some to depart suddenly from this world, and for one to be strangled, another to die, one old, another young; but He once circumscribed the common and human span of years, and diverse manners of deaths. Within so long a span of years, therefore, diverse manners of death are brought about. Not that God circumscribed this from the beginning, yet He foreknew it; and regarding the appointment of each one's life individually, the divine counsel designates from before the time and manner of his death. And if Basil the Great speaks concerning the determination of life,[^179] he designates and recalls that word: "Earth thou art, and unto earth shalt thou return" (Gen. 3:19). For the Apostle says, writing to the Corinthians: "Because ye receive unworthily, for this cause many are weak and sickly among you, and many sleep or die" (1 Cor. 11:30).[^180]

And David: "Take me not away in the midst of my days!" (Ps. 101:25 [LXX Ps. 101:25 / MT Ps. 102:24]);[^177] and: "Thou hast made my days as a span measured out" (Ps. 38:6 [LXX Ps. 38:6 / MT Ps. 39:5]; Ps. 138:16 [LXX Ps. 138:16 / MT Ps. 139:16]).[^178]

And Solomon: "My son, honor thy father, that thou mayest live long; and again, that thou die not before thy time" (Ecclus. 3:6, 3:12; Eccles. 7:17).[^181] And in Job, God says unto Eliphaz: "I would have destroyed you and turned you to nothing, had I not refrained for the sake of Job My servant" (Job 42:8).[^182]

Hence it is shown that there is no fixed boundary of life. But if anyone says this, understand by "boundary" the divine will and His pleasure. For to whomsoever He wills, He adds years, and unto another He diminishes them, ordering all things for profit. And when God wills and permits, He orders both the manner and the hour. The boundary of each man's life, therefore, as the great Athanasius says, is the will and counsel of God.[^183] By this word, and by the depth of Thy judgments, O Christ, one will also soften that saying of Basil the Great who says that deaths are brought on when the boundaries of life are fulfilled; and by boundaries of life we understand the will of God. For if there were a fixed boundary of life, why do we pray to God, and bring requests to physicians, and pray for our children?

It is necessary to know this also: that baptized infants enjoy delight; but the unillumined and pagans go neither into...

*Meatfare Saturday.*

...delight, nor will they go into Gehenna.[^184] The soul, therefore, departing from the body, has no care concerning the earthly things here, but is continually concerned about the things there.

We celebrate the Third-day memorial, therefore—understand this both bodily and spiritually—because on the third day the appearance of the deceased man changes. The Ninth-day memorial, moreover, because at that time the whole form of the man dissolves, only the heart alone being preserved. The Fortieth-day memorial, furthermore, because at that time even the heart itself perishes. Seeing that birth also proceeds in the selfsame manner: for on the third day the heart is formed; on the ninth day it is knit into flesh; and on the fortieth day it is formed into the complete appearance.[^185] For these aforesaid reasons, therefore, we make commemoration for the souls: whom do Thou establish, O Master Christ, in the tabernacles—that is, the dwellings—of Thy righteous ones, and have mercy upon us, as Thou alone art immortal! Amen.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:* In the beginning having founded the earth, and by Thy Word established the heavens, blessed art Thou unto the ages, O Lord God of our Fathers! (Ps. 101:26 [LXX Ps. 101:26 / MT Ps. 102:25]; Dan. 3:26, 52).[^186]

*Troparion:* Making commemoration of those who have departed in piety from the ages, we cry: Blessed art Thou unto the ages, O Lord God of our Fathers!

*Troparion:* Unto those who died suddenly in piety, who fell from every manner of precipice, wood, iron, and stone of every kind: give rest, O God, unto those who have reposed in faith!

*Troparion:* At Thy fearful coming, O Compassionate One, place on the right hand among Thy sheep those who in life served Thee in an Orthodox manner, O Christ, and have departed unto Thee!

*Troparion:* Rank Thy servants in the choir of Thine elect, O Christ, to cry unto Thee: Blessed art Thou unto the ages, O Lord God of our Fathers!

*Troparion:* Having fashioned dust from the earth, and quickened flesh with the spirit, O compassionate Savior: those whom Thou hast received, give rest, O God, in the life that grows not old!

*Glory... Triadicon:*  
As the Godhead in three radiants, by the mingled unity of one light let the Father, the Son, and the Divine Spirit be hymned...

*Meatfare Saturday.*

...Spirit: one in Essence, yet three in Hypostases!

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
In virginity we sing a harmonious song unto thee, naming thee the Mountain of God, O Virgin, in whom the Word, having dwelt in flesh, deified us spiritually in Himself!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:* Him Who was glorified on the holy mountain, and in the bush through fire revealed the mystery of the Ever-Virgin unto Moses: sing ye the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages! (Exod. 3:1–6; Dan. 3:56–88).[^187]

*Troparion:* O Thou Who of old didst crush the shadow of death, shining forth like the sun from the tomb: make all who have died in faith to be sons of Thy resurrection unto the ages, O Lord of glory!

*Troparion:* O Witness of unseen and hidden things, when Thou shalt uncover the works of darkness and the counsels of our hearts: then enter not into reckoning with all who have reposed in faith! (1 Cor. 4:5).[^188]

*Troparion:* When Thou shalt sit upon the Throne, and command those gathered by the trumpet from the ends of the earth to stand before the judgment-seat: then spare all men, O Christ, as Thou art merciful! (Matt. 24:31, 25:31).

*Troparion:* Unto those who died suddenly from casualties, and from vehement clamor, swift speed, crushing, strangulation, and trampling of horses: grant relief unto the ages, O Lord of glory, unto those who have reposed in faith!

*We bless the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord!*  
*Triadicon:* As a Unity in Essence I sing of Thee, as a Trinity in Persons I honor Thee, O Father, Son, and Most Holy Spirit; the unoriginate dominion of Thy Kingdom I glorify unto the ages!

*Both now... Theotokion:*  
A fountain sealed with the stream of life hast thou been revealed, O Virgin Birthgiver of God: for without man having borne the Lord, thou waterest the faithful with the water of immortality unto all ages! (Song of Sol. 4:12).[^189]

*We praise, we bless, and we worship the Lord, singing and supremely exalting Him unto all ages.*  
*Heirmos:* Him Who was glorified on the holy mountain...  
*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

*Meatfare Saturday.*

*Ode 9. Heirmos:* ...revealed unto the Lawgiver upon the Mountain by fire and the bush, the birth-giving of the Ever-Virgin, which is salvation unto us the faithful, with unceasing hymns we magnify! (Exod. 3:2; Luke 1:46–55).[^190]

*Troparion:* Where the dwelling of those who rejoice is found, O Lord, among Thy saints: vouchsafe all who have reposed from the ages in faith and hope to rejoice!

*Troparion:* Those who have died by the divine threatening of mortal thunders, being hurled down in any manner from heaven, those swallowed up by the earth that clove asunder, and those drowned in the surging sea: give rest, O Christ, unto all the faithful![^191]

*Troparion:* Those who have died from poisonous bites, from being swallowed by serpents, from trampling of horses, and from strangling and hanging by a neighbor: give rest unto those who served Thee in faith!

*Troparion:* Every age—the elder and the infant, youth and child, boys and sucklings at the breast, both male and female nature: give rest, O God, unto the faithful whom Thou hast received!

*Troparion:* Every one by name of those who have reposed in faith from the ages and from generation to generation: vouchsafe them, O Christ, to stand uncondemned before Thee at Thy coming!

*Glory... Triadicon:* O God, One in Trinity, glory unto Thee unceasingly! For though each is God, yet One in essence is the Father, the Son, and the Spirit, in three-radiant personal properties.

*Both now... Theotokion:* Beyond understanding is thy childbearing: for thou bearest Him Who was before all, and feedest with milk ineffably the Food-giver of the world; and thou holdest Him Who upholds all things, Christ our only Deliverer, O all-immaculate one!

*Exapostilarion. Tone 3, Podoben: "Having adorned heaven with stars" (Небо звѣздами):* O Thou Who rulest over the living and the dead as God: give rest unto Thy servants in the tabernacles of Thine elect! For though they sinned, O Savior, yet they departed not from Thee! *Twice.*[^192]

*Glory... Tone 2, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены услышите):*

*Meatfare Saturday.*

*Exapostilarion (continued):* ...give rest unto Thy servants in the land of the living, O Lord, where sickness, together with sorrow and sighing, have fled away (Isa. 35:10); cleanse, as Lover of mankind, whatsoever they sinned in life, for Thou alone art sinless, and the merciful Master of the dead and the living!

*Both now... Theotokion:* O Mary, Bride of God, unceasingly pray unto Christ for us Thy servants, with the God-speaking Prophets, the assemblies of Martyrs, Hierarchs, and Venerable ones, and all the Righteous, that we may become co-heirs of the heavenly Kingdom!

*At the Praises: Stichera on 4. Tone 8, Podoben: "In Eden of old" (Въ Едемѣ рай):*[^193]

*Sticheron 1:* Come before the end, all ye brethren, beholding our dust and the infirmity and lowliness of our nature; let us discern our end and the instruments of the fleshy vessel, and that man is ash, food for worms and corruption, and that our dry bones are wholly devoid of breath! (Ezek. 37:1–10). Let us peer into the tombs: where is the glory? Where the beauty of form? Where the eloquent tongue? Where the eyebrows, or where the eye? All are ash and shadow; therefore spare us all, O Savior!

*Sticheron 2:* Why is man deceived, boasting of himself? Why is he troubled in vain, who is mud and shines but for a little while? Why does dust not consider that it is a mixture of ashes, a deposit of corruption and putrefaction? If, then, we men are mire, why have we cleaved unto the earth? And if we are kinsmen of Christ, why do we not hasten unto Him, casting aside all temporary and fleeting life, and following after the incorruptible life, which is Christ, the illumination of our souls?

*Sticheron 3:* O Thou Who with Thine own hand didst fashion Adam, and didst establish the boundary of incorruption and mortality, O Savior, and of life in grace, altering corruption and translating him unto the first life: do Thou Thyself, O Master, give rest with the Righteous unto Thy servants whom Thou hast taken from us, and in the choir of Thine elect...

*Meatfare Saturday.*

*Sticheron 3 (continued):* ...Thine elect; and inscribing their names in the Book of Life, and raising them at the voice of the Archangel and the trumpet-call, vouchsafe them Thy heavenly Kingdom! (1 Thess. 4:16; Rev. 20:12).

*Sticheron 4:* Christ is risen, having shattered the bonds of Adam the first-formed and destroyed the power of Hades! Take courage, all ye dead: death is put to death, and Hades is led captive with it, and Christ is King, Who was crucified and is risen! He hath granted us incorruption of flesh; He raises us and bestows resurrection upon us; and may He vouchsafe that glory with gladness unto all who with unwavering faith have fervently believed in Him!

*Glory... Tone 2:* As a flower withers, and as a shadow passes away, so every man is dissolved (Job 14:2); but when the trumpet sounds again, all the dead, as in an earthquake, will arise unto the meeting with Thee, O Christ God! (1 Cor. 15:52). Then, O Master, rank those whom Thou hast translated from us in the tabernacles of Thy saints, even the spirits of Thy servants, O Master, forever!

*Both now... Theotokion:* Rejoice, O Mary Mother of God, temple indestructible and most holy; even as the Prophet cries: Holy is Thy temple, marvelous in righteousness! (Ps. 64:5 [LXX 64:5 / MT 65:4]).[^194]

*At the Aposticha: Stichera of Theophanes from the Octoechos. Tone 6, Podoben: "Having placed all their hope" (Все упованіе):*

*Sticheron 1:* Possessing incomprehensible compassion toward us and an inexhaustible wellspring of divine goodness, O Plenteous in mercy: settle in the land of the living those who have passed unto Thee, O Master; in the beloved and longed-for dwellings grant them an inheritance that remains forever! For Thou hast shed Thy blood for all men, O Christ, and with a life-bearing price hast redeemed the world!

*Meatfare Saturday.*

*Verse:* Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken, O Lord (Ps. 64:5 [LXX 64:5 / MT 65:4]).[^195]  
*Troparion:* Thou didst willingly endure life-creating mortification, and didst pour forth life, and gave ever-existing sweetness unto the faithful: which do Thou now grant unto those who have reposed in hope of resurrection, forgiving all their transgressions by Thy grace, as Thou alone art sinless, and alone good and the Lover of mankind; that for all things Thy name may be sung, O Christ, and being saved we may glorify Thy love for mankind!

*Verse:* Their soul shall dwell among good things (Ps. 24:13 [LXX 24:13 / MT 25:13]).[^196]  
*Troparion:* Ruling over the living with divine-princely authority, and holding dominion over the dead, knowing Thee to be Christ, we pray: give rest, O Lover of mankind, with Thine elect unto Thy faithful servants who have departed unto Thee, the only Benefactor, in places of refreshment, in the splendors of the saints! For Thou art desirous of mercy, and savest as God those whom Thou hast fashioned according to Thine own image, O Thou Who alone art plenteous in mercy!

*Glory... Tone 6. By John of Damascus:* A grief unto Adam was the tasting of the tree of old in Eden, when the serpent vomited out his venom: for on that account all-human death entered, devouring man! But the Master having come cast down the serpent, and granted us rest. Unto Him, therefore, let us cry aloud: Spare, O Savior, those whom Thou hast received, and give them rest with Thine elect![^197]

*Both now... Theotokion:* Thou art our God, Who in wisdom dost work and accomplish all things! Thou didst send Prophets, O Christ, to foretell Thy coming, and Apostles to preach Thy marvelous works; and the former indeed foretold Thy coming...

*Meatfare Saturday.*

*Theotokion (continued):* ...while the latter enlightened the nations through Baptism; and the Martyrs, suffering, received the things they longed for; and the choir of both prays unto Thee, together with her that bore Thee: Give rest, as God, unto the souls whom Thou hast taken to Thyself, and vouchsafe us Thy Kingdom, O Thou Who didst endure the Cross for my sake, the condemned one, my Deliverer and my God!

*Reader:* It is good to give praise to the Lord... (Ps. 91:2 [LXX 91:2 / MT 92:1]). Trisagion. *Then Troparion, Tone 8:* By the depth of wisdom... *Glory... Both now...* Thee, a wall and a haven... (see fol. 32)[^198] *And the usual Dismissal.*

*And the litany for the departed:* Have mercy on us, O God... *And the dismissal for the departed. Then the Singers sing:* Unto the servants of God, all Orthodox Christians who have reposed from the ages, for whom we make commemoration: Memory eternal! *Thrice.*

*The First Hour. Thereat the Troparion and Kontakion for the departed, and the rest; and the complete Dismissal. And we commemorate the Saint whose day it is, and the Many Years. In like manner also at the other Hours. Troparion:* By the depth of wisdom... *Both now... Theotokion of the Hours. After the Trisagion, Kontakion:* With the Saints give rest... *Glory... Both now... Kontakion of the Mother of God:* To thee, the Champion Leader...

*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes from the Canon, Odes 3 and 6.*  
*Prokeimenon, Tone 6:* Their soul shall dwell among good things (Ps. 24:13 [LXX 24:13 / MT 25:13]). *Verse:* Unto Thee, O Lord, have I lifted up my soul (Ps. 24:1 [LXX 24:1 / MT 25:1]).  
*Epistle of the day: to the Corinthians, Pericope 146 (1 Cor. 6:12–20):* Brethren, all things are lawful unto me...  
*Another Epistle, for the departed: to the Thessalonians, Pericope 270 (1 Thess. 4:13–17):* Brethren, I would not have you to be ignorant...  
*Alleluia, Tone 6:* Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen... (Ps. 64:5 [LXX 64:5 / MT 65:4]).  
*Gospel of the day: from Luke, Pericope 105 (Luke 21:8–9, 25–27, 33–36):* The Lord said: Take heed that ye be not deceived...  
*Another Gospel, for the departed: from John, Pericope 16 (John 5:24–30):* The Lord said unto those who came unto Him: Truly, truly, I say unto you...[^199]  
*Kinonikon:* Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken, O Lord, and their memorial is from generation to generation (Ps. 64:5 [LXX 64:5 / MT 65:4]; Ps. 101:13 [LXX 101:13 / MT 102:12]). Alleluia.

*Be it known that this order takes place without change also on the Saturday of Holy Pentecost.*[^200]

*[Full-Page-Width Woodcut Illustration: The Dread Judgment (Last Judgment). Inscription at top: ("СТРАШНЫЙ") and ("СУДЪ"). Depiction of Christ the righteous Judge enthroned in glory within an oval mandorla, attended by the Mother of God, Saint John the Baptist, and the angelic host rolling up the heavens. Below the throne stands the Etimasia (Throne of Preparation) with the open Book of Life and the Holy Cross, before which Adam and Eve kneel in supplication. To the left, the Apostles seated as judges, the righteous souls, and an angel weighing deeds in the scales; to the right, Moses pointing out Christ to the condemned, and sinners driven away into the fiery river. Below, the fiery stream flows into the gaping jaws of Hades; personifications of the earth and the sea surrender their dead to the sound of angelic trumpets; and on the lower left, the gates of Paradise are opened by the Apostle Peter with angels welcoming the righteous.]*[^201]

## Sunday of Meatfare

*On Saturday evening, the usual Vigil.*

*At "Lord, I have cried": Stichera of the Resurrection, 3; and Anatolian, 3; and from the Triodion, 4. Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Все возложивше):*[^202]

*Sticheron 1:* When Thou shalt come, O most righteous Judge, to execute righteous judgment, seated upon the Throne of Thy glory: a fiery river terrifying before Thy judgment-seat draws all men along (Dan. 7:10); while the heavenly powers stand before Thee, and men are judged in fear according to what each has done! Then spare us, O Christ, and vouchsafe us the portion of those being saved, as Thou art compassionate, who pray unto Thee in faith!

*Sunday of Meatfare.*

*Sticheron 2:* The books will be unrolled (Dan. 7:10; Rev. 20:12), and the deeds of men will be revealed before Thine unendurable judgment-seat! The whole valley will resound with the fearful gnashing of wailing, beholding all who have sinned sent away unto eternal torments by Thy righteous judgment, weeping unavailingly, O Compassionate One! Therefore we beseech Thee, O Good One: spare us who hymn Thee, O Thou Who alone art plenteous in mercy!

*Sticheron 3:* The trumpets will sound (1 Cor. 15:52; 1 Thess. 4:16), and the tombs will be emptied, and all human nature will arise in trembling! Those who did good rejoice in joy, expecting to receive their reward; but those who sinned tremble, bitterly weeping, being sent into torment and separated from the elect! O Lord of glory, take pity on us as Thou art good, and vouchsafe us the portion of those who have loved Thee!

*Sticheron 4:* I weep and lament when I bring to mind the eternal fire, the outer darkness, and the deep abyss (Matt. 25:30, 41), the fierce worm, the gnashing of teeth that slumbers not (Mark 9:44, 48), and the endless pain and torment for those who have sinned immeasurably and angered Thee, the All-good One, by their evil way! Of whom I, the wretched one, am the first and chief (1 Tim. 1:15). But, O Judge, save me by Thy mercy, as Thou art compassionate!

*If it be the Forefeast or Afterfeast of the Meeting of the Lord: Then Stichera of the Resurrection, 3; and from the Triodion, 4; and of the Forefeast or Feast, 3 (see fol. 3).*[^203]

*Glory... Tone 8:* When the thrones are set in order, and the books are unrolled, and God sits in judgment (Dan. 7:9–10): oh, what fear will there be then, while the Angels stand by in trembling, and the fiery river flows![^204] What...

*Sunday of Meatfare.*

*Glory (continued):* ...shall we do then, men guilty of many sins? And when we hear Him calling the blessed of His Father into the Kingdom, but sending away the sinners into torment (Matt. 25:34, 41): who will endure that fearful sentence? But O only Lover of mankind, Savior, King of the ages: before the end draws near, turn me in repentance and have mercy upon me!

*Both now... Theotokion of the current Tone of the Octoechos.*

*At the Litiya, the usual order. If it be the Feast of the Meeting of the Lord, then we say another Glory..., Both now... of the Feast. In like manner do we also on Cheesefare Sunday.*

*Glory... Tone 6. Idiomelon:* Having understood the commandments of the Lord, let us live accordingly: let us feed the hungry, let us give drink to the thirsty, let us clothe the naked, let us bring in strangers, let us visit the sick and those in prison (Matt. 25:35–36); that He Who is to judge all the earth may say unto us also: "Come, ye blessed of My Father, inherit the Kingdom prepared for you!" (Matt. 25:34).[^205]

*Both now... Theotokion, in the same Tone:* Under thy protection, O Lady, all we earthborn flee, crying aloud unto thee: O Mother of God, our hope, deliver us from immeasurable transgressions, and save our souls!

*At the Aposticha: in the Octoechos, and according to the Alphabet, 3.*

*Glory... Tone 8:* Woe is me, O darkened soul! How long wilt thou not recoil from evils? How long wilt thou lie in despondency? Why dost thou not ponder the fearful hour of death? Why dost thou not tremble wholly before the dread judgment-seat of the Savior? What, then, wilt thou answer, or what wilt thou say? Thy deeds will stand forth to convict thee; thine actions will accuse and testify against thee! Henceforth, O soul, the time...[^206]

*Sunday of Meatfare.*

*Glory (continued):* ...is at hand! Run, anticipate, cry aloud in faith: "I have sinned, O Lord, I have sinned against Thee! But I know Thy tender compassions, O Lover of mankind, O Good Shepherd: separate me not from standing at Thy right hand, for the sake of Thy great mercy!" (Ps. 50:3 [LXX 50:3 / MT 51:1]; John 10:11; Matt. 25:33).

*Both now... Theotokion, in the same Tone:* O unwedded Virgin, who ineffably conceived according to the flesh, Mother of God Most High: receive the prayers of thy servants, O all-immaculate one, granting unto all the cleansing of transgressions; receiving now our entreaties, pray that we all may be saved!

*Troparion:* O Virgin Mother of God... *Thrice. And the rest of the order of the Vigil. And there is a reading from the Acts of the Apostles.*

*Concerning the Saint of the day, see above (see fol. 30).*[^207]  
*If the Feast of the Meeting of the Lord fall on this Sunday, the rule for this is written on the Sunday of the Publican and the Pharisee (see fol. 3).*  
*Know also this: that on this Meatfare Sunday the Apodosis of the Meeting never occurs (see fol. 4, and Patos Mlp., see fol. 24).*

*If the Finding of the Precious Head of John the Forerunner fall on Meatfare Sunday or Cheesefare Sunday:*[^208]

*At Small Vespers and at Great Vespers, as indicated for the Forefeast of the Meeting (see fol. 3). And at the Litiya: Sticheron of the Saint whose Temple it is; and the Automela that are in the Triodion at the Praises; and the Automela of the Forerunner that are at the Aposticha. Glory... of the Forerunner, Tone 2: A divine mind... Both now... in the Triodion; or Theotokion in the same Tone, which is according to the Alphabet.*

*At the Aposticha: Stichera of the Resurrection. Glory... of the Forerunner, Tone 6: A divine treasure... Both now... in the Triodion. At the Blessing of Loaves, Troparion: O Virgin Mother of God... Thrice. And a reading from the Epistles of the Apostles. And the rest in Chrysostom. And the anointing of the Saint.*

*At Matins: At "God is the Lord": Troparion of the Resurrection, twice. Glory... of the Forerunner. Both now... Theotokion of the current Tone of the Octoechos. After the Kathismata, Sessional hymns of the Resurrection with their Theotokia. And a reading in the Commentary Gospel. And the Blameless [Kathisma 17]. Hypakoe of the Tone. And a reading in the Gospel. Polyeleos of the Forerunner, and all his Sessional hymns, once each. And a reading of the Forerunner. Songs of Ascents [Anabathmoi], and the Prokeimenon of the Tone. "Let every breath praise the Lord." The Resurrectional Gospel, and the rest as of the Resurrection. The Canon of the Resurrection with its Heirmos on 4; and from the Triodion on 4; and of the Forerunner on 4. Katavasia from the Triodion. After Ode 3: Kontakion and Ikos of the Forerunner, and his Sessional hymns, 2. Glory... Both now... Sessional hymn from the Triodion. And a reading of the Forerunner. After Ode 6: Kontakion and Ikos from the Triodion. And a reading in the Prologue. At Ode 9...*

*Sunday of Meatfare.*

*Rubric (continued):* ...we sing "More honorable than the Cherubim." Exapostilarion of the Resurrection. Glory... of the Forerunner. Both now... from the Triodion. At the Praises: Stichera of the Resurrection, 4; and of the Forerunner, 3; and Idiomelon, Tone 6: *The all-honorable head...* We say the verses of the Forerunner: *The righteous shall rejoice in the Lord* (Ps. 63:11 [LXX 63:11 / MT 64:10]); second verse: *The righteous man shall flourish like a palm tree* (Ps. 91:13 [LXX 91:13 / MT 92:12]); then verse: *Arise, O Lord my God, let Thy hand be lifted up* (Ps. 9:33 [LXX 9:33 / MT 10:12]); and immediately the Idiomelon Sticheron in the Triodion. Glory... the same. Both now... *Most blessed art thou, O Virgin Mother of God...* Great Doxology. Troparion of the Resurrection only. Litany and Dismissal. And the usual procession into the narthex; Glory... Both now... Gospel Sticheron. And the First Hour. At the Hours, Troparion of the Resurrection. Glory... of the Forerunner. Both now... Theotokion of the Hours. After the Our Father, Kontakion in the Triodion and of the Forerunner, alternating. At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Tone on 6; and of the Forerunner, Ode 3, on 4. After the Little Entrance, Troparion of the Resurrection and of the Forerunner. Glory... his Kontakion. Both now... Kontakion in the Triodion. Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, Gospel, and Kinonikon, first of the day, then of the Forerunner.

*At Matins: At "God is the Lord": Troparion of the Resurrection of the Tone. And the usual Stichologies. Sessional hymns from the Octoechos. And we read in the Commentary Gospel, whereof the beginning is: "Fearful is this present day..." And the Polyeleos. Hypakoe of the Tone. We read also the Homily concerning the Poor by Saint Gregory the Theologian in three parts, whereof the beginning is: "Men, brethren, and fellow-paupers..." And the homily of Chrysostom, whereof the beginning is: "Beloved brethren, assemble yourselves and give ear..." And another homily of the same saint, whereof the beginning is: "Behold, the time of repentance has drawn near..." Songs of Ascents [Anabathmoi] of the Tone. Gospel. And the rest, even as was shown on the preceding Sunday.*[^209]

*The Canon of the Resurrection and the Theotokion on 6; and from the Triodion on 8. If it be the Forefeast or Afterfeast of the Meeting of the Lord: The Canon of the Resurrection on 4; and of the Forefeast or Feast on 4; and from the Triodion on 6. Katavasia from the Triodion. In like manner also if it be the Feast of the Meeting of the Lord itself, only the Katavasia is of the Feast.*

*The Canon, the work of the Lord Theodore the Studite. Tone 6.*[^210]

*Ode 1. Heirmos:* He was my Helper and Protector unto salvation; this is my God, and I will glorify Him; the God of my father, and I will exalt Him; for gloriously hath He been glorified! (Exod. 15:1–2).

*Refrain:* Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee!

*Troparion:* Pondering the dread day of Thine all-ineffable coming...

*Sunday of Meatfare.*

*Troparion (continued):* ...I tremble, and reflecting thereon, with terror do I foresee the day whereon Thou shalt come to judge the living and the dead, O my God almighty!

*Troparion:* When Thou shalt come, O God, amidst myriads and thousands of the angelic and heavenly hosts: vouchsafe also me, wretched as I am, to meet Thee upon the clouds, O Christ! (1 Thess. 4:17).

*Troparion:* Come, O my soul, take to heart that day and hour when God shall come manifestly; and lament and weep, that thou mayest be found pure in the hour of trial! (Ps. 49:3 [LXX 49:3 / MT 50:3]).

*Troparion:* The unquenchable fire of Gehenna amazes and terrifies me, and the bitter worm and the gnashing of teeth; but remit, forgive me, O Christ, and number me among the assembly of Thine elect! (Mark 9:43–48; Matt. 8:12).

*Troparion:* That longed-for voice of Thine, calling Thy Saints unto joy—may I also, wretched one, hear it, and find the ineffable delight of the Kingdom of Heaven! (Matt. 25:34).

*Troparion:* Enter not into judgment with me, bringing forth my deeds, examining words, and trying impulses; but overlooking my grievous offenses in Thy tender compassions, save me, O Almighty One! (Ps. 142:2 [LXX 142:2 / MT 143:2]).

*Glory... Triadikon:* O Trinity in three Hypostases, Unity sovereign and Lady over all, perfected primal and transcendent Principle: do Thou Thyself save us, O Father, Son, and all-holy Spirit!

*Both now... Theotokion:* Who ever gave birth to a Son unsown, according to the law of the Father? For the Father begets Him without a mother. O most marvelous wonder! For thou, O pure Maiden, hast given birth to Him Who is at once God and man!

*Katavasia: He was my Helper and Protector...*

*Ode 3. Heirmos:* Establish, O Lord, upon the immovable rock of Thy commandments my shaken heart; for Thou alone art holy and righteous! (1 Kings 2:2 [LXX 1 Reg. 2:2 / 1 Sam. 2:2]).[^211]

*Troparion:* The Lord comes, and who shall endure His fear? Who shall appear...

*Sunday of Meatfare.*

*Troparion (continued):* ...to appear before His face? But be thou ready, O my soul, for the meeting! (Mal. 3:2; Amos 4:12).

*Troparion:* Let us anticipate the end, and weep, and make supplication unto Christ before the end: for dreadful is the judgment-seat, whereon we all shall stand uncovered! (2 Cor. 5:10).

*Troparion:* Have mercy, O Lord, have mercy upon me, I cry unto Thee, when Thou shalt come with Thine Angels to render unto all according to the desert of their deeds! (Matt. 16:27; Rom. 2:6).

*Troparion:* How shall I endure the unendurable wrath of Thy judgment, O Lord, having disobeyed Thy commandment? But spare, spare me in the hour of judgment! (Nah. 1:6).

*Troparion:* Turn back, sigh, O wretched soul, before the festival of this life reaches its end, before the Lord shuts the doors of the bridal chamber! (Matt. 25:10–12).

*Troparion:* I have sinned, O Lord, as no other among men has sinned, having transgressed beyond all men: before the judgment, be Thou merciful unto me, O Lover of mankind! (1 Tim. 1:15).

*Glory... Triadikon:* O simple, uncreated Trinity, Nature without beginning, praised in a Trinity of Hypostases: save us who in faith worship Thy dominion!

*Both now... Theotokion:* By an unsown birthgiving, O all-immaculate Maiden, thou didst nurture the living Word, incarnate in thy womb without change: glory, O Mother of God, unto thy childbearing!

*Katavasia: Establish, O Lord...*

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thy tomb, O Savior" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*[^212] Dreadful is Thy tribunal, and righteous is Thy judgment, but my deeds are grievous! Yet do Thou Thyself, O merciful One, anticipating me, save me and deliver me from torment; rescue me, O Master, from the portion of the goats, and vouchsafe me to stand at Thy right hand, O most righteous Judge! (Matt. 25:32–33).

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:* In thy womb, by the Divine Spirit, O all-immaculate and pure Maiden, thou didst contain the Creator of all and thy God and Maker, and didst give birth unto Him without corruption; glorifying Him, we praise thee, O Virgin, as the palace of the King of glory and the redemption of the world!

*Sunday of Meatfare.*

*Other Sessional Hymns, Tone 6:*[^213] I reflect upon that dreadful day, and I weep over my evil deeds: how shall I answer the immortal King? And with what boldness shall I, prodigal that I am, look upon the Judge? O compassionate Father, only-begotten Son, and Holy Spirit: have mercy upon me!

*Glory...:* In the valley of weeping, in the place which Thou hast appointed, when Thou shalt sit, O merciful One, to execute righteous judgment: expose not my hidden things, neither put me to shame before the Angels; but spare me, O God, and have mercy upon me! (Joel 3:12 [LXX 4:12]; Ps. 83:7 [LXX 83:7 / MT 84:6]).

*Both now... Theotokion:* O good Mother of God and Virgin, hope of the world: I implore thine only and dreadful protection; take pity upon a defenseless people, and entreat the merciful God that our souls may be delivered from every menace, O only blessed one!

*And a reading.*[^214]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:* The Prophet heard of Thy coming, O Lord, and was afraid: that Thou wast to be born of a Virgin and appear unto men; and he said: "I heard report of Thee, and was afraid; glory to Thy power!" (Hab. 3:2 [LXX]).[^215]

*Troparion:* The day has arrived, the judgment is already at the doors! Be vigilant, O my soul, where kings and princes, rich and poor are gathered together, and each one of men shall receive according to the worth of what he has done! (James 5:9; Rev. 20:12).

*Troparion:* In his own rank, monk and hierarch, old and young, slave and master shall be examined; widow and virgin shall be judged; and woe unto all then who have not possessed a blameless life!

*Troparion:* Incorruptible is Thy judgment, Thy tribunal admits no concealment of sophistry; it is stolen not by the rhetoric of orators, nor is it deflected by the false testimony of witnesses, but is righteous: for before Thee, O God, stand revealed the secret things of all! (Rom. 2:16; 1 Cor. 4:5).

*Troparion:* May I not enter into the land of weeping, may I not see the place of darkness, O Christ my Word; neither let me be cast out with bound hands and feet outside Thy bridal chamber, having defiled the garment of incorruption, O impassible Lord! (Matt. 22:11–13).

*Sunday of Meatfare.*

*Troparion (continued):* When Thou shalt separate the sinners from the righteous, judging the world, number me as one of Thy sheep, parting me from the goats, O Lover of mankind, that I may hear that blessed voice of Thine! (Matt. 25:32–34).

*Troparion:* Hearing the sorrowful words of the rich man tormented in the flame, I weep and lament, wretched as I am, being subject unto the selfsame condemnation; and I pray: Have mercy upon me, O Savior of the world, in the time of judgment! (Luke 16:24).

*Troparion:* When the examination is made and the books of deeds are opened, what wilt thou do, O wretched soul? What wilt thou answer before the judgment-seat, having no fruit of righteousness to offer unto God and thy Maker? (Dan. 7:10; Rev. 20:12).

*Glory... Triadikon:* I glorify the Son from the Father and the Spirit, as light and ray from the sun: the One indeed by way of generation, for He is begotten; the Other by way of procession, for He proceeds; the co-beginningless Divine Trinity, worshipped by every creature![^216]

*Both now... Theotokion:* A Virgin giving birth unto an Infant, yet preserving virginity: pure Maiden, thou wast revealed as having given birth unto Him Who is God and man, the selfsame One in both forms; thy wonder, O Virgin Mother, strikes with awe every hearing and thought!

*Katavasia: The Prophet heard...*

*Ode 5. Heirmos:* Seeking Thee at dawn from early morning out of the night, O Lover of mankind, enlighten me, I pray Thee, and guide me also in Thy commandments, and teach me ever to do Thy will! (Isa. 26:9).[^217]

*Troparion:* Insuperable trembling and fear are there! For the Lord shall come, and with Him the deed of every man: who then will not weep for himself here in this life? (Wis. 1:9; Rev. 22:12).

*Troparion:* The river of fire confounds me, melts me, and scrapes me; the gnashing of teeth and the darkness of the abyss: how, or by having done what, shall I propitiate God? (Dan. 7:10; Matt. 8:12).

*Sunday of Meatfare.*

*Troparion (continued):* Spare, spare Thy servant, O Lord, lest Thou deliver me unto the bitter tormentors, the cruel Angels, among whom there is no rest to be found there!

*Troparion:* Prince and captain are there together; the rich and the ignoble, the great together with the small shall be judged equally: woe, then, unto everyone who is unprepared! (Job 34:19; Rev. 6:15).

*Troparion:* Remit, pardon, O Lord, and forgive all wherein I have sinned against Thee, and show me not forth there before the Angels in the condemnation of the fire and of endless shame! (Dan. 12:2).

*Troparion:* Spare, O Lord, spare Thine own creation! I have sinned, forgive me: for Thou alone art pure by nature, and none other besides Thee is free from defilement! (Job 14:4; Ps. 50:7 [LXX 50:7 / MT 51:5]).

*Glory... Triadikon:* Thee, O Trinity, I praise as a Unity by nature, unoriginate, incomprehensible, sovereign royal all-perfected Unity: good Light, and Life, and Creator of the world!

*Both now... Theotokion:* In thy childbearing beyond nature, O pure Maiden, the laws of nature were manifestly dissolved for thee; for without seed thou dost give birth unto the pre-eternal God begotten of the Father!

*Katavasia: Seeking Thee at dawn from early morning...*

*Ode 6. Heirmos:* I cried with my whole heart unto the compassionate God, and He heard me from the lowest hell, and brought up my life from corruption! (Jonah 2:3, 7 [LXX 2:3, 7]).[^218]

*Troparion:* At Thy dreadful coming, O Christ, when Thou shalt appear from heaven, the thrones shall be set, and the books opened: spare, spare then, O Savior, Thine own creation! (Dan. 7:9–10).

*Troparion:* There nothing can help when God is the Judge: neither eloquence, nor skill, nor glory, nor friendship, but only the power that comes from deeds, O my soul!

*Troparion:* Fear is there alike for princes, poor and rich, O my soul! Neither shall a father be able, nor a mother to help, nor a brother to deliver from condemnation! (Ps. 48:8 [LXX 48:8 / MT 49:7]).

*Sunday of Meatfare.*

*Troparion (continued):* Reflecting, O my soul, upon the dreadful judicial cross-examination, be utterly filled with awe! Prepare a word of defense, that thou be not condemned unto eternal bonds!

*Troparion:* May I not hear, O Lord: "Take what is thine and depart," sent away from Thee; nor: "Depart into the fire of the accursed!" but rather the longed-for voice of the righteous! (Matt. 20:14; 25:41).

*Troparion:* Deliver me, O Lord, from the gates of Hades, from the chasm of unilluminated darkness in the nether regions, from the unquenchable fire, and from every other eternal torment! (Job 10:21–22).

*Glory... Triadikon:* I sing the Godhead of the Trinitarian Unity: the Father, the Son, and the Divine Spirit, the dominion of a single Principle, divided in three personal hypostases!

*Both now... Theotokion:* Thou art the Gate through which alone Jesus entered and went forth, breaking not the seals of virginity, O pure Maiden: He Who created Adam, and is thy Son! (Ezek. 44:2).

*Katavasia: I cried with my whole heart...*

*Kontakion, Tone 1:* When Thou shalt come, O God, upon the earth with glory, and all things tremble, and a river of fire flows before the judgment-seat, and the books are opened, and secret things exposed: then deliver me from the unquenchable fire, and vouchsafe me to stand at Thy right hand, O most righteous Judge! (Dan. 7:9–10; Matt. 25:33).[^219]

*Ikos:* Pondering Thy dreadful tribunal, O all-good Lord, and the day of judgment, I fear and tremble, being reproved by mine own conscience, when Thou shalt sit upon Thy throne and make examination: then none shall be able to deny sins, when truth convicts and fear lays hold! Loudly indeed shall the fire of Gehenna roar then, and sinners shall weep: therefore have mercy upon me before the end, and spare me, O most righteous Judge![^220]

*Synaxarion on the Sunday of Meatfare.*[^221]  
*Verses:*  
When Thou, O Judge of all, shalt sit to judge the earth,  
Deem me also worthy of that voice: "Come ye!" (Matt. 25:34).

*Sunday of Meatfare.*

*Synaxarion (continued):*

On this day, we celebrate the commemoration of the Second and Impartial Coming of Christ, which the most divine Fathers appointed after those two Parables [of the Publican and the Prodigal Son]: to the end that no one, having learned from those parables of God's benevolence toward man, should begin to live carelessly, saying: "God is the Lover of mankind, and whenever we turn away from sin, He is ready and willing to advance us in His goodness." They ordered this dreadful day here, so that through the contemplation of death and the expectation of those fearsome woes, having struck awe into those lying in negligence, they might convert and bring them unto virtue, and teach them not only to place their trust in God's loving-kindness, but also to consider that He is a righteous Judge, Who renders unto each according to his works (Matt. 16:27; Rom. 2:6). Furthermore, after those days that preceded, it was needful that the Judge should also appear. And in a certain manner, this present feast is set forth today as it were the end of all feasts, since it shall indeed be the very last and final one of all concerning us.

It is proper, moreover, to see and consider that on the coming Sunday next at hand, they place the beginning of the world and Adam's very expulsion from Paradise.

This present Sunday, however, they have established on Meatfare as the end of all our affairs and of that world itself—curbing, as I deem, and restraining luxury, gluttony, and insatiable lust through the dread of the Feast, and likewise calling us unto fellow-suffering and showing mercy unto our neighbors. And in another way: since through living luxuriously we were cast out from Eden and fell under condemnation and a curse, in accordance therewith this present Feast is placed here, seeing that on the second Sunday, by way of prefiguration—that is, representation—we are to be cast out from Eden through Adam, until Christ, having come, shall again gather us and lead us into Paradise!

Moreover, this present day is named the Second Coming of Christ, because the first time He also came bodily unto us, yet exceedingly quietly and without glory.

Now, however, He shall come with supernatural wonders and with pre-eminent radiance from Heaven, and with His Body, so that He shall be recognized by all: that this is He Who came formerly and redeemed the human race, and Who is to come again to judge it and to examine whether it kept well the things given and entrusted unto it!

When, however, that Coming shall be, no one knows. For this indeed...

*Sunday of Meatfare.*

*Synaxarion (continued):*

...the Lord concealed and kept hidden from the Apostles (Mark 13:32),[^222] although He revealed that certain signs are to precede it, which signs some of the Saints have explained at greater length in their writings.

They say also that after seven thousand years, the Coming shall take place.[^223] And before His Coming, Antichrist shall come, and shall be born, as Saint Hippolytus of Rome says,[^224] of an impure woman and a supposed virgin, being of the Jews, of the tribe of Dan, who was a son of Jacob (Gen. 49:17). And he shall walk as if living according to Christ, and shall work wonders which Christ also performed, and shall raise the dead; yet according to phantasm—that is, of delusive apparition—he shall perform all things: both his birth, and flesh, and all other things, as says the Apostle.

"And then," he says, "shall be revealed the son of perdition in all power and signs and lying wonders" (2 Thess. 2:3, 9).[^225] And yet the devil himself shall not be clothed in a body nor transformed, as John of Damascus says,[^226] but a man born of fornication shall receive all the operation of Satan, and shall suddenly emerge and arise.

Thereafter he shall be deemed by all to be benevolent and modest. And there shall be a great famine then, and in that famine he shall as it were help and rescue people; and he shall study the Divine Scriptures, and shall exercise himself in fasting; and he shall be compelled by the people, and they shall proclaim him king. And he shall greatly love the Jewish race, and shall settle in Jerusalem, and shall rebuild their temple.

And seven years before, as Daniel says,[^227] Enoch and Elijah shall come, preaching unto the people that they should not receive him; but he, having seized them, shall torment them, and thereafter shall cut off their heads (Rev. 11:3, 7). Those, however, who are called pious shall flee far away, and having found them among the mountains, he shall tempt and seduce them through demons.

Yet those seven years shall be shortened for the sake of the elect (Matt. 24:22).[^228] And there shall be a great famine, when all the elements shall be overturned, so that all shall almost vanish and perish.

Thereafter, suddenly, as it were lightning from heaven, shall be the Coming of the Lord (Matt. 24:27, 29–30; Mark 13:24–26; Luke 21:25–27; 1 Cor. 15:52; 1 Thess. 4:16; Rev. 20:10),[^229] when His Precious Cross shall go before Him, and a river of fire terribly foaming shall flow before Him, cleansing all the earth from filth and defilement! At that hour Antichrist shall immediately be seized, and his ministers, and he shall be given over unto eternal fire. And when the Angels sound the trumpet, there shall come gathered together into one heap from the ends of the earth and from all...

*Sunday of Meatfare.*

*Synaxarion (continued):*

...elements, the whole human race unto Jerusalem, since it is the center of the world.[^230] And there the Thrones are prepared for judgment; and people stand solely with their bodies and souls, when all shall be transformed into incorruption (1 Cor. 15:52) and shall have one form, and the very elements shall receive a change for the better. And with a single word the Lord shall separate the righteous from the sinners (Matt. 25:32); and the doers of good things shall depart, bearing away eternal life, but unto the sinners, eternal torment (Matt. 25:46); and there shall never be any end or cessation of either!

It is necessary to know also this: that at that hour Christ will not seek in us fasting, nor nakedness, nor any wonders—though these things also are good—but virtues far more noble than these: that is to say, almsgiving and mercy!

For He shall say unto the righteous six certain things: "For I was hungry, and ye gave Me to eat; I was thirsty, and ye gave Me drink; I was a stranger, and ye took Me in; naked, and ye clothed Me; I was sick, and ye visited Me; I was in prison, and ye came unto Me. For inasmuch as ye did it unto one of these least, ye did it unto Me" (Matt. 25:35–40). Which virtues everyone can fulfill according to his strength.

At that hour, then, every tongue shall confess that Jesus Christ is Lord, to the glory of God the Father (Phil. 2:11). The torments, moreover, which the Holy Gospel has handed down, are these: "There shall be weeping and gnashing of teeth" (Matt. 8:12); "Their worm shall not die, and their fire shall not be quenched" (Mark 9:44); and that other: "Cast him into the outer darkness" (Matt. 22:13). Which things all, having received clearly and distinctly, the Church of God understands: that delight and the Kingdom of Heaven is for the Saints a co-dwelling with God, and that for those Saints there is to be continual illumination and exaltation. In like manner also, that torment, and darkness, and the like, is estrangement from God, and the loss and ruin of souls before conscience, as those who for the sake of negligence and temporal luxury have forfeited the Divine radiance. By Thine ineffable love for mankind, O Christ God, make us worthy of Thy longed-for voice, and number us among those standing at Thy right hand, and have mercy upon us! Amen.[^231]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:* We have sinned, we have transgressed, and we have done unjustly before Thee; neither have we kept nor done as Thou hast commanded us: but deliver us not up utterly, O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou! (Dan. 3:29–30; 3:52 [LXX]).[^232]

*Sunday of Meatfare.*

*Troparion (continued):* Let us fall down and weep before that judgment, all we faithful, when the heavens shall perish, the stars fall, and all the earth be shaken, that before the end we may find merciful the God of our fathers! (Matt. 24:29; Heb. 1:11–12; 2 Pet. 3:10).

*Troparion:* Incorruptible is the cross-examination, dreadful is the judgment there, where the Judge cannot be deceived, where there is no accepting of persons for bribes: but spare me, O Master, and deliver me from all Thy dreadful wrath! (2 Chron. 19:7 [LXX 2 Paral. 19:7]; Rom. 2:11).

*Troparion:* The Lord comes to judge: who shall endure the vision of Him? Tremble, O wretched soul, tremble, and prepare thy works for departure, that thou mayest find Him merciful and compassionate, the blessed God of our fathers! (Mal. 3:2).

*Troparion:* I fall down before Thee and offer unto Thee my words as tears: I have sinned as the harlot never sinned, and I have transgressed as no other on earth! But take pity, O Master, upon Thine own creation, and call me back! (Luke 7:37–38).

*Troparion:* The unquenchable fire confounds me, and the bitter gnashing of teeth; soul-destroying hell terrifies me; never am I filled with good compunction! But O Lord, Lord, before the end establish me in Thy fear! (Matt. 8:12; 25:41).

*Troparion:* Turn back, O my soul, repent, uncover thy secret things! Say unto God Who knows all things: "Thou knowest my hidden secrets, O only Savior; yet do Thou Thyself have mercy upon me, as David sings, according to Thy mercy!" (Ps. 50:3 [LXX 50:3 / MT 51:1]).

*Glory... Triadikon:* Three in One by essence, and three in Persons, do I praise these: the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit; one power, will, and operation; one thrice-holy God; one kingdom, supreme in single beginning![^233]

*Both now... Theotokion:*

*Sunday of Meatfare.*

*Theotokion (continued):* The Most Comely God comes forth from the bridal chamber of thy womb, as a King arrayed in a purple robe woven by God, dyed by the mystery of thy most pure blood, O unwedded Bride, and He reigns over the earth! (Ps. 44:3 [LXX 44:3 / MT 45:2]).

*Katavasia:* We have sinned, we have transgressed, and we have done unjustly before Thee... (Dan. 3:29–30 [LXX]).

*Ode 8. Heirmos:* Him Whom the heavenly hosts glorify, and before Whom the Cherubim and Seraphim tremble: let every breath and creation praise, bless, and supreme-exalt unto all ages! (Dan. 3:57ff).[^234]

*Troparion:* Reflecting upon the meeting of Thy dread Second Coming, O Lord, I tremble at Thy threatening, I am afraid of Thy wrath: from this very hour I cry: "Save me unto the ages!"

*Troparion:* When Thou, O God, judgest all things, who, being earthborn and dust, shall endure it? For the unquenchable fire then, and the worm that gnaws exceedingly, shall receive the condemned unto the ages! (Mark 9:44, 48).[^235]

*Troparion:* When Thou shalt summon every breath together, O Christ, to judge them: great shall be the terror then, great the distress, with only good deeds to help unto the ages!

*Troparion:* O Judge of all, my God and Lord, may I then hear Thy longed-for voice! May I behold Thy great light, may I dwell within Thy tabernacles, may I see Thy glory, rejoicing unto the ages!

*Troparion:* O Righteous Judge, Savior, have mercy, and deliver me from the fire and the threatening which I am justly to receive at the judgment! Grant me remission before the end, by reason of virtue and repentance!

*Troparion:* When Thou sittest as Judge, being compassionate, and showest Thy dread glory, O Christ: oh, how great a terror then, while the furnace burns, and all fear Thine invincible judgment seat!

*Bless we the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord!* (Dan. 3:56 [LXX]).

*Triadikon:* I revere God as One according to essence, and I praise three Hypostases with distinction—diverse, yet not alien, seeing that...[^236]

*Sunday of Meatfare.*

*Triadikon (continued):* ...the Godhead is one in three Persons: for there is the Father, the Son, and the Divine Spirit.

*Both now... Theotokion:* Having come forth from thy most radiant womb as a Bridegroom from His chamber, Christ the Champion shone as a great light unto those in darkness: for shining forth as the Sun of Righteousness, He enlightened the world! (Ps. 18:6 [LXX 18:6 / MT 19:5]; Isa. 9:2; Mal. 4:2).

*We praise, we bless, we worship the Lord, singing and supreme-exalting Him unto all ages!*

*Heirmos:* Him Whom the heavenly hosts glorify...

*Ode 9. Heirmos:* Ineffable is the birthgiving of a seedless conception, a seedless nativity of a Mother that knew not man: for the birthgiving of God renews nature. Therefore all generations orthodoxly magnify thee as Mother of our God! (Luke 1:48).[^237]

*Troparion:* The Lord comes to punish sinners, but to save the righteous. Let us weep and wail, and take to heart the perception of that day, wherein having uncovered the hidden and secret things of men, He shall render unto each according to his desert! (Matt. 16:27; Rom. 2:6, 16).

*Troparion:* Moses was filled with fear and trembling when he beheld Thee from behind: how then shall I, wretched that I am, stand upright, beholding Thy face then, when Thou shalt come from Heaven? But spare me, O Compassionate One, in Thy merciful regard! (Exod. 33:20–23; Heb. 12:21).[^238]

*Troparion:* Daniel feared the hour of trial: what then shall I suffer, O Lord, on that very dread day that is coming? But grant me before the end to serve Thee in a manner well-pleasing, and to obtain Thy Kingdom! (Dan. 7:15, 28).

*Troparion:* The fire is prepared, and the worm is made ready; yet gladness, glory, relief, unwaning light, and joy are for the righteous! And who is so blessed that he will not desire to escape the torment of the former, and inherit the latter? (Matt. 25:34, 41; Mark 9:44).

*Troparion:* Let not the fury of Thy wrath turn me away from Thy face, O Lord, neither let me hear the accursed voice...

*Sunday of Meatfare.*

*Troparion (continued):* ...dispatching me into the fire! But may I also enter then into the joy of Thine incorruptible bridal chamber with Thy Saints! (Matt. 25:21, 41).

*Troparion:* The mind is wounded, the body covered with scabs, the spirit languishes, the word has failed, life is deadened, the end is at the doors: therefore, O my wretched soul, what wilt thou do when the Judge comes to examine thy deeds? (Matt. 24:33).

*Glory... Triadikon:* O Father of the Only-begotten Son, Sole Begetter of the One; and Thou, the One Radiance of the One Light, the Radiance of Light; and Thou, the One Holy Spirit, proceeding solely from the One God, truly being Lord of the Lord: O Holy Trinity, Unity, save me who theologize concerning Thee![^239]

*Both now... Theotokion:* The wonder of thy childbirth amazes me, O All-immaculate One: how dost thou conceive the Incomprehensible One without seed? Tell me, how dost thou remain a virgin, having given birth as a Mother? Having received with faith that which is beyond nature, worship Him Who was born: for whatsoever He wills, He can also accomplish!

*Katavasia. Heirmos:* Ineffable is the birthgiving of a seedless conception...

*Exapostilarion of the Resurrection. Then the present one, Tone 2, Podoben: "Let us assemble with the disciples" (Соꙋчени́кѡмъ):*[^240]

Reflecting upon the dread day of judgment and Thine ineffable glory, O Lord, I tremble exceedingly, and shaking with fear I cry: When Thou shalt come upon the earth with glory to judge all things, O Christ God, deliver me from every torment, and vouchsafe me, O Master, to stand at Thy right hand! (Matt. 25:33).

*Another Exapostilarion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены ꙋ҆слы́шите):*

Behold, the day of the Lord Almighty comes! And who shall endure the terror of His coming? For it is a day of wrath, and a burning furnace, wherein the Judge shall sit, and render unto each according to the desert of his deeds! (Mal. 3:2; 4:1; Rom. 2:5–6).

*Theotokion:*

Reflecting upon the hour of examination and the dread coming of the Master Who loves mankind, I tremble wholly, and mourning I cry unto Thee: O my most righteous Judge, and only Plenteous in mercy, receive me in repentance, through the prayers of the Mother of God!

*At the Praises, Stichera of the Resurrection on 5, and of the Triodion on 4.*

*Sunday of Meatfare.*

*Tone 6. Idiomela. Verse: Let everything that hath breath praise the Lord! (Ps. 150:6).*

*Sticheron:* I ponder that day and hour, when we are all to stand naked and as condemned men before the inexorable Judge! Then the trumpet shall resound mightily, and the foundations of the earth shall be shaken, and the dead shall rise from the graves! And all shall be of one age, and the hidden secrets of all shall stand revealed before Thee: and those who never repented shall wail and weep, and depart into outer fire; but the portion of the righteous shall enter into joy and gladness, into the heavenly bridal chamber! (1 Cor. 15:52; 1 Thess. 4:16; Matt. 25:30, 46).[^241]

*Verse: I will give praise unto Thee, O Lord my God, with my whole heart, I will tell of all Thy marvelous works! (Ps. 9:2 [LXX 9:2 / MT 9:1]).*

*Sticheron:* Oh, what an hour then, and what a dread day, when the Judge sits upon His fearsome throne! The books are unfolded, and deeds are exposed, and the hidden things of darkness are brought to light! Angels haste about, gathering together all the nations. Come, listen, kings and princes, slaves and freemen, sinners and the righteous, rich and poor: for the Judge comes to judge the whole inhabited world! And who shall stand before His face, when Angels stand forth exposing deeds, thoughts, and intents, both of the night and of the day? Oh, what an hour then! But before the end draws nigh, hasten, crying out, O my soul: "O God, having turned me back, save me, as Thou alone art compassionate!" (Dan. 7:10; 1 Cor. 4:5; Rev. 20:12).[^242]

*Tone 8. Verse: I will be glad and rejoice in Thee, I will chant unto Thy name, O Thou Most High! (Ps. 9:3 [LXX 9:3 / MT 9:2]).*

*Sticheron:* The Prophet Daniel, having been a man of desires, when he beheld the dominion of God, cried out in this manner: "The Judge sat, and the books were opened!" Take heed, O my soul: dost thou fast? Despise not thy neighbor! Dost thou abstain from foods?...[^243]

*Sunday of Meatfare.*
