*Sticheron (continued):* ...condemn not thy brother, lest being sent away into the fire, thou melt away like wax: but that without stumbling Christ may bring thee into His Kingdom! (Dan. 7:10; 9:23; 10:11; Rom. 14:10).

*Tone 1. Verse: Arise, O Lord my God, let Thy hand be lifted up, forget not Thy poor unto the end! (Ps. 9:33 [LXX 9:33 / MT 10:12]).*

*Sticheron:* Let us cleanse ourselves beforehand, brethren, with the queen of virtues: for behold, she is come, bestowing upon us a wealth of good things! She quenches the festering of the passions, and reconciles the fallen unto the Master. Therefore let us receive her with gladness, crying unto Christ God: "O Thou Who didst rise from the dead, preserve us uncondemned, who give glory unto Thee, the only Sinless One!"[^244]

*Glory... Again the same verse:* (Ps. 9:33). *Both now... Thou art most blessed...*

*If there be the Forefeast of the Meeting of the Lord, as occurred in year 1000 [A.D. 1640]:[^245] then we omit the Forefeast at the Praises, and we sing the Resurrectional Stichera and those in the Triodion. Glory... in the Triodion. Both now... Thou art most blessed... Thus do we also on Cheesefare Sunday. Likewise if the Feast of the Meeting falls on either of these two Sundays: After the conclusion of the Stichera of the Feast, and the Idiomelon appointed for Glory of the Feast, we recite it with the verse of the Feast. Then we say the verse: Arise, O Lord my God; and we sing the Doxasticon Sticheron which is in the Triodion for the day. Glory... is omitted. Both now... Thou art most blessed... The Great Doxology, and the Dismissal. The customary Litiya in the Narthex. Glory... Both now... The Gospel Sticheron. The First Hour. And the rest, even as was set forth on the preceding Sunday, together with the Catechesis of the Studite.*

*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Resurrection on 6; and from the Triodion, Ode 6 on 4. Prokeimenon, Tone 3: Great is our Lord, and great is His power, and of His understanding there is no number! (Ps. 146:5 [LXX 146:5 / MT 147:5]). Verse: Praise ye the Lord, for a psalm is a good thing! (Ps. 146:1 [LXX 146:1 / MT 147:1]). The Epistle to the Corinthians, Pericope 140 (1 Cor. 8:8–9:2). Alleluia, Tone 8: Come, let us rejoice in the Lord, let us make a joyful noise unto God our Savior! (Ps. 94:1 [LXX 94:1 / MT 95:1]). Verse: Let us come before His presence with thanksgiving, and make a joyful noise unto Him with psalms! (Ps. 94:2 [LXX 94:2 / MT 95:2]). The Gospel according to Matthew, Pericope 106 (Matt. 25:31–46). Communion Hymn: Praise ye the Lord from the heavens! (Ps. 148:1). Another: Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous! (Ps. 32:1 [LXX 32:1 / MT 33:1]). At the Trapeza, we read from the Festal Homilies, and the Sermon of Palladius the Monk, whose beginning is: "Now make thy confession, O soul..."*[^246]

*Sunday of Meatfare, at Vespers.*

*We do not chant a Kathisma; nor indeed on the other Sundays at any time. At "Lord, I have cried," the Stichera as usual on 6: from the Octoechos, Tone 3 [3 stichera]; and from the Menaion for the Saint of the day, 3. But the Stichera in the Triodion we leave aside, as also in the Octoechos, and we sing the Idiomelon of the day, both choirs together, Tone 8:*

*Idiomelon, Tone 8:* Having given ourselves over to pampering the belly, we took upon us our first nakedness, overcome by the bitter taste, and were driven out from God! But let us ascend unto repentance and cleanse our senses, against which our warfare is waged, making an ascent of the Fast: let us establish our hearts with the hope of grace, not with foods, wherein they that walked profited nothing! And the Lamb of God shall be eaten by us on the sacred and light-bearing night of the Resurrection—the Sacrifice offered up for our sake, communicated unto the disciples on the evening of the Mystery, dissolving the darkness of ignorance by the light of His Resurrection! (Gen. 3:6–7; Heb. 13:9; Matt. 26:26–28; John 1:29).[^247]

*Verse 1: Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes, O Thou that dwellest in heaven! Behold, as the eyes of servants look unto the hands of their masters, as the eyes of a maiden unto the hands of her mistress: so do our eyes look unto the Lord our God, until He have mercy upon us! (Ps. 122:1–2 [LXX 122:1–2 / MT 123:1–2]).*

*And again the same Sticheron:* Having given ourselves over to pampering the belly...

*Verse 2: Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us: for we are exceedingly filled with contempt! Our soul is exceedingly filled: let the reproach be to them that are at ease, and the contempt to the proud! (Ps. 122:3–4 [LXX 122:3–4 / MT 123:3–4]).*

*For the Martyrs:* O Martyrs of the Lord, ye sanctify every place and heal every sickness! And now we pray you: entreat that our souls be delivered from the snares of the enemy!

*Glory... if the Saint has an Idiomelon for the day; Both now... Theotokion in the same Tone. But if not, then Glory... Both now... Theotokion, Tone 8:* The heavenly hosts hymn thee, O Full of Grace, unwedded Mother! And we glorify thine unsearchable childbirth, O Mother of God: pray that our souls may be saved!

*Order of the Cheesefare Typikon.*[^248]

*Thus do we from this Sunday forth: in the evening at Vespers, at the Aposticha, and at Matins, every day even unto Lazarus Friday.*

*If the Meeting of the Lord falls on Monday, or on Tuesday, or on Thursday of Cheesefare Week:*[^249]

*In the evening at Small Vespers: At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Feast on 4. Glory... Both now... of the Feast. At the Aposticha: The Idiomelon of the day in the Triodion, twice. Glory... Both now... of the Feast. Troparion of the Feast. Litany, and the Dismissal.*

*At Great Vespers and at Matins, the entire service of the Feast is celebrated without change, exactly as was indicated: for we sing nothing else from the Triodion. On whichever day it falls during Cheesefare Week, except Wednesday and Friday, recite the Idiomela Stichera of the day that are said at Small Vespers, even as was directed; and the Tri-ode which was written for Cheesefare Sunday, we sing on that same day at Compline. Likewise, when the Meeting of the Lord falls on Thursday of Cheesefare, then we sing the Thursday Tri-ode on this Monday of Cheesefare at Compline; but on the other days it is directed in its proper place. But if an All-Night Vigil does not take place anywhere, and Small Vespers is not chanted: then we sing at Vespers in the evening with refrains; and as for the present: Glory... in the Triodion, the Idiomelon of the day; Both now... of the Feast. At the Praises, after saying 4 Stichera of the Feast: Glory... in the Triodion, the Idiomelon of the day; Both now... of the Feast. Great Doxology. At the Hours and at the Liturgy, the entire service is of the Feast; but the daily Epistle and Gospel are read beforehand, or on that day at the Hours.*

*If during the Afterfeast of the Meeting: in Cheesefare Week:*

*In the evening: Stichera of the Feast, 3; and to the Saint of the day, 3. Glory... to the Saint; Both now... of the Feast. If not: Glory... Both now... of the Feast. At the Aposticha: The Idiomelon of the day, twice. Glory... to the Saint; Both now... of the Feast. If he has none: Glory... Both now... of the Feast. Troparion to the Saint, if he has one; Glory... Both now... of the Feast. Litany, and Dismissal.*

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord": Troparion of the Feast, twice; Glory... to the Saint, if there be one; Both now... of the Feast. After the Kathismata: Sessional hymn of the Feast. Canon of the Feast with the Heirmos on 8, and in the Menaion on 4. But where the Tri-ode occurs, Canon of the Feast with the Heirmos on 6, and in the Menaion on 4, and in the Triodion on 4. After Ode 3: Kontakion of the Feast and Ikos, if the Saint has one; however, that which is written in the Triodion we sing after the Kathismata; Glory... to the Saint; Both now... of the Feast. After Ode 6: Kontakion and Ikos to the Saint; if he has none, then here the Kontakion and Ikos of the Feast. Sessional hymn of the Feast; Glory... to the Saint; Both now... of the Feast; if he has no Stichera, then of the Feast, twice. At the Praises: Stichera of the Feast on 4; Glory... Both now... of the Feast. At the Aposticha: The Idiomelon of the day, twice; Glory... to the Saint; Both now... of the Feast; if he has no Stichera: Glory... Both now... of the Feast. Troparion to the Saint; Glory... Both now... of the Feast; if he has no Stichera, of the Feast only. Litany, and Dismissal; and the First Hour.*

*Monday of Cheesefare: Typikon Directives.*

*If the Finding of the Precious Head of John the Forerunner falls on Monday, or on Tuesday, or on Thursday of Cheesefare Week:*[^250]

*In the evening, Kathisma 140 [Ps. 140 / "Lord, I have cried"]. At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Forerunner on 6. Glory... of the Forerunner; Both now... Theotokion in the same Tone. Prokeimenon of the day, and the Old Testament Readings of the Forerunner. At the Aposticha: The Idiomelon of the day, twice. Glory... of the Forerunner, Tone 2: A divine mind... Both now... Theotokion. Troparion of the Forerunner; Glory... Both now... Resurrectional Theotokion. Litany, and Dismissal. And the customary exit into the Narthex, and the final Dismissal.*

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord": Troparion of the Forerunner, twice; Glory... Both now... Theotokion in the same Tone. The customary Psalter reading. After the 1st Kathisma: Sessional hymn in the Triodion, and Reading. After the 2nd Kathisma: Sessional hymns of the Forerunner, both; and reading concerning the Forerunner. Polyeleos, and Sessional hymn of the Forerunner, twice; Glory... Both now... Theotokion; and reading concerning the Forerunner. Sticheron, 1st Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon, Tone 4: There will I make the horn of David to bud: I have prepared a lamp for Mine Anointed! (Ps. 131:17 [LXX 131:17 / MT 132:17]). Verse: Lord, remember David, and all his meekness! (Ps. 131:1 [LXX 131:1 / MT 132:1]). "Let everything that hath breath..." Gospel according to Luke, Pericope 31 (Luke 7:17–35): At that time, this rumor went forth concerning Jesus... Psalm 50. Sticheron of the Forerunner. And the Biblical Odes in the Psalter we do not recite. Then we sing: Canon of the Mother of God with the Heirmos on 6, and of the Forerunner on 8. Katavasia: "I shall open my mouth..." Where the Tri-ode occurs, we sing: Canon of the Mother of God with the Heirmos on 4, and in the Triodion on 4, and of the Forerunner on 6. After Ode 3: Sessional hymn of the Forerunner, twice; Glory... Both now... Theotokion; and reading concerning the Forerunner. After Ode 6: Kontakion of the Forerunner, and Ikos; and we read in the Prologue. After Ode 9: Sessional hymn [Exapostilarion] of the Forerunner, twice; Glory... Both now... its Theotokion. At the Praises: Stichera of the Forerunner, Prosomoia on 4; Glory... of the Forerunner, Tone 6: The all-honorable head... Both now... Theotokion. At the Aposticha: The Idiomelon of the day, twice; Glory... of the Forerunner, Tone 6: He that formerly upon a platter... Both now... Theotokion. And the rest as usual, and the Dismissal, and the First Hour. At the Hours: Troparion and Kontakion of the Forerunner. Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom. Thereat, the Beatitudes of the Forerunner, Odes 3 and 6. Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, Gospel, and Communion Hymn: first of the day, then of the Forerunner.*

*If there occurs a Great Saint, or the Saint of the Temple, or a Saint commemorated on these days of Cheesefare Week: whether there be a Vigil or not, the rest follows after Festal Vespers. At Compline we sing the Tri-ode or Canon if it be Wednesday or Friday; and the Idiomelon of the day which is appointed at Matins at the Aposticha, we sing in the Temple of the Saint. At the Praises: Both now... in the Triodion. Before the Feast: in the evening, Sticheron [of the Saint] or of the Temple Saint. For this cause have we set forth these directives, that nothing written in the Triodion may be left aside. Know also: throughout this entire Cheesefare Week at Matins chant two Kathismata from the Psalter; and the third, the regular one in course, we sing at Vespers, which provides relief for the brethren. Even as it is necessary or customary to be brief, so it is throughout all of Cheesefare Week except Wednesday and Friday, concerning which the directive is given in its proper place. And concerning the other directives, (see fol. 24)[^251].*

## Monday of Cheesefare

*At "God is the Lord," Troparion to the Saint, twice. Glory... Both now... Theotokion according to the Tone. At the 1st Psalter reading, Sessional hymn in the Octoechos. And we read the Homily of Basil the Great, whose beginning is: "Hear ye the words of the Apostle..." At the 2nd Psalter reading, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "When the stone had been sealed" (Ка́мени знамена́нꙋ):*

The portal of divine repentance is opened! Let us draw near with fervor, having cleansed our bodies, making an putting away of foods and passions, as obedient followers of Christ Who called the world into the Heavenly Kingdom, offering the tithes of the entire year unto the King of all, that we may also behold His Resurrection with love!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

Thy divine hands, wherewith thou didst bear the Creator incarnate in His Godhead, O Most-pure Virgin, stretch forth and entreat Him: that we may be delivered from temptations, passions, and tribulations, who praise thee with love and cry aloud unto thee: Glory to Him Who dwelt within thee! Glory to Him Who came forth from thee! Glory to Him Who hath set us free through thy childbirth!

*And we read the Homily of Saint Basil the Great: "On giving heed to thyself," whose beginning is: "God Who created us gave us the need of speech..." And the Instruction of the same Basil the Great: "On the renunciation of worldly life and on spiritual perfection," whose beginning is: "Come unto Me, all ye that labor..." What remains we read at the Trapeza.*[^252]

*The Canons we sing as is customary: in the Octoechos up to Ode 6, on 6; and in the Menaion to the Saint, on 4. But in whichever Odes the Tri-ode occurs, we leave aside those Odes of the second Canon of the Octoechos altogether; and we sing in the Octoechos the 1st Canon with the Heirmos, on 6; and in the Menaion to the Saint on 4; and in the Triodion on 4.*

*Tri-ode [Canon], the Work of Lord Joseph [the Hymnographer], Tone 2.*[^253]

*Ode 1. Heirmos: A song of victory...*

*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee!*

*Monday of Cheesefare.*

*Troparion:* Radiant are the forefeasts of abstinence, luminous are the heraldings of the Fast today! Therefore let us run together in hope, brethren, and with great diligence!

*Troparion:* Having emulated our forefather Adam in transgression, I was driven out from delight, wretched that I am! Therefore I fall down before Thee in repentance and weep: "O Lord, save me!" (Gen. 3:23–24).

*Troparion:* The beginning of compunction and repentance is alienation from evil things and abstinence from passions: hasten therefore to make a cutting-off of wicked deeds!

*Theotokion:* Darkened by the night of transgressions, unto thee who didst bear Christ, the Sun of Righteousness, for those in darkness, I now diligently flee: O Sovereign Lady, save me! (Isa. 9:2; Mal. 4:2).

*Another Tri-ode [Canon, by St. Theodore the Studite]. Same Heirmos, and Ode of Tone 1.*

*Troparion:* Behold, the door of repentance is opened beforehand, O lovers of God! Come therefore, let us hasten to enter thereby, before Christ shuts it against us as unworthy! (Matt. 25:10–12).

*Troparion:* Presaging the spiritual springtime, this all-honorable Week of surpassing purification is now drawn near, which enlightens the bodies and souls of all with the sacred fasts!

*Glory... Triadikon:* O Unconfused, Uncreated, Holy Trinity, All-good Unity: Father, Son, and Consubstantial Spirit, ever save those who worship Thee in faith!

*Both now... Theotokion:* The laws of nature were overcome in thee, O all-pure Virgin: for having given birth without a man unto God, thou remainest pure, uncorrupted even as before thy childbirth!

*And again, the Heirmos:* Let us all sing a song of victory unto God, Who hath wrought marvelous wonders with an high arm, and saved Israel: for He is glorified! (Exod. 15:1ff).

*After Ode 3: Sessional hymn; Glory... Both now... Theotokion. After Ode 6: Kontakion to the Saint, and Ikos.*

*Ode 8. Heirmos: Him at Whom the Angels are amazed...*

*Troparion:* I have prodigally spent all my life, laboring in bitter and wicked servitude to the citizens of this world; yet when I desire to turn now unto Thy compassions, O Christ, do not despise me! (Luke 15:13–15).

*Troparion:* The shameful and grievous manner of my life cries out for great mercy and bountiful compassion, O Savior, Lover of mankind: grant me, who ask Thee and praise Thee with love, the season of repentance!

*Troparion:* I have emulated the accursed life of Cain, truly having slain the dignity of the mind, and I tremble before Thy judgment, O Benefactor: condemn me not with him unto the ages! (Gen. 4:8–12).

*Theotokion:* O all-praised Virgin, sole steadfast help toward God for the faithful who are in the world: deliver me from darkness and from the torment prepared for those who live wickedly!

*Another Tri-ode [Canon, by St. Theodore the Studite]. Heirmos: We offer a song unto Thee...*

*Troparion:* Behold, the time of repentance is come! Therefore, brethren, let us begin in a god-fearing mind, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord! (Dan. 3:57 [LXX]).

*Troparion:* Having set ourselves today against foods, let us strive by deeds also worthily to repent of our transgressions: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord! (Dan. 3:57 [LXX]).

*Bless we the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord!* (Dan. 3:56 [LXX]).

*Triadikon:* Glory to Thee, O invisible Holy Trinity, worshipped in Unity, Whom we faithfully glorify: Save Thy servants from every harm!

*Both now... Theotokion:* Rejoice, all-immaculate, pure Virgin! Rejoice, rod and Mother of the Lord! Rejoice, O protection of the world, Mother of God, blessed Mary, all-radiant!

*We praise, we bless, we worship the Lord, singing and supreme-exalting Him unto all ages!*

*Heirmos:* We offer a song unto Thee, as do the bodiless hosts, even as did the children in the furnace; and singing, we say: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord! (Dan. 3:57 [LXX]).

*Ode 9. Heirmos: Radiant...*

*Troparion:* In peaceful disposition and in pure love, let us faithful receive the most sacred season of the Holy Fast, asking...

*Troparion (continued):* ...forgiveness of those things wherein each hath sinned, that we may attain the joys there!

*Troparion:* Behold, the season of repentance, this pre-festal fast-entrance! Arise, O my soul, cleave warmly unto God the Benefactor, that thou mayest truly escape that dread and terrible judgment!

*Troparion:* Let us not wickedly mock all the entrances and portals of the Fast through lack of restraint and drunkenness; but in purity of thoughts let us enter fervently, receiving crowns of incorruption and the worthy fruits of our labors!

*Theotokion:* Cease not to pray for us unto the Good One, Who alone knows our weakness: to deliver from famine, destruction, earthquake, and every affliction the city that honors thee, O Mother of God, Hope of the earthborn!

*Another Tri-ode [Canon, by St. Theodore the Studite]. Heirmos: Thee, the gate beheld...*

*Troparion:* The season of repentance hath been brought in, O my soul: neglect it not! Give bread to the hungry; pray unto God every day, and night, and hour, that He may save thee! (Isa. 58:7).

*Troparion:* Even as we have made an estrangement from meats and other foods, so also let us flee all enmity toward our neighbors, and likewise fornication, lying, and every wickedness!

*Glory... Triadikon:* I worship the beginningless Godhead, one in nature; and in three Persons I glorify in the Trinity the undivided and co-enthroned Unity!

*Both now... Theotokion:* Thou art the boast, the hope, and the protectress of the faithful, O Virgin: with songs of praises we unceasingly glorify thee, O Mary, Mother of God: save Thy servants!

*Heirmos:* Thee, the gate beheld by the Prophet Ezekiel, through which none hath passed save God alone, O Virgin Mother of God, do we magnify in hymns! (Ezek. 44:2).

*Exapostilarion in the Octoechos; to the Saint, Sessional hymn; Exapostilarion to the Saint, if there be one. But if not, Sessional hymn and Kontakion. After the Doxology, four prostrations. At the Aposticha...*

*At the Aposticha, Idiomelon Sticheron, Tone 3:* At all times Fasting is profitable unto those who choose it and practice it: for neither does demonic temptation dare attack him that fasts, but the Angels, the guardians of our life, abide with us more diligently when we are cleansed by fasting!

*Verse: We have been filled in the morning with Thy mercy, O Lord, and we have rejoiced and been glad! In all our days let us be glad: for the days wherein Thou didst humble us, for the years wherein we have seen evils! And look upon Thy servants, and upon Thy works, and guide their children! (Ps. 89:14–16 [LXX 89:14–16 / MT 90:14–16]).*

*And again, the same Sticheron.*

*Second Verse: And let the brightness of the Lord our God be upon us, and prosper Thou the works of our hands upon us, yea, the work of our hands do Thou prosper! (Ps. 89:17 [LXX 89:17 / MT 90:17]).*

*For the Martyrs:* Come, all ye peoples, let us honor the memory of the holy Passion-bearers: for having become a spectacle unto Angels and unto men, they received crowns of victory from Christ, and they pray for our souls! (1 Cor. 4:9).

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:* O holy, most-pure boast of the heavenly ranks, song of the Apostles and fulfillment of the Prophets: O Sovereign Lady, accept our prayers!

*Be it known that on Cheesefare Monday the Idiomela are sung twice on this day.*

## Monday of Cheesefare at Vespers

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera as usual in the Octoechos, 3; and in the Menaion to the Saint, 3. Glory... to the Saint, if there be one. But if not, Glory... Both now... Theotokion in the same Tone and Podoben.*

*At the Aposticha, Idiomelon Sticheron, Tone 8:* Let us strive to cleanse ourselves by fasting from the defilement of our transgressions, and by mercy and loving-kindness toward the poor to enter into the bridal chamber of Christ the Bridegroom, Who grants us great mercy!

*Verse: Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes, unto Thee that dwellest in heaven! (Ps. 122:1 [LXX 122:1 / MT 123:1]).*

*And again, the same Sticheron.*

*Verse: Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us: for we are exceedingly filled with contempt! (Ps. 122:3 [LXX 122:3 / MT 123:3]).*

*For the Martyrs:* O Martyrs of the Lord, pray unto our God, and ask for our souls...

*For the Martyrs (continued):* ...a multitude of compassions, and cleansing from our many offenses, as ye make supplication!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:* The most glorious and all-pure Mother of God, true Mother of God, let us piously hymn, saying unto her "Rejoice!" as did the Angel: Rejoice, holy Mother of the incarnate Son of God! Rejoice, dwelling of the Holy Spirit, unceasingly praying for us that our souls may be saved! (Luke 1:28).

*Troparion to the Saint. Glory... Both now... Theotokion.*

*On the Apodosis of the Meeting of the Lord, Chapter 46:*[^254]

*(see fol. 24)[^255] Know: If the Feast of the Meeting of the Lord fell on Wednesday, or on Thursday, or on Friday, or on Saturday of Meatfare: its Apodosis is celebrated on Cheesefare Tuesday, and it is sung thus:*

*In the evening: At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Feast on 6. Glory... Both now... of the Feast. At the Aposticha: The Idiomelon of the day, twice; and For the Martyrs, with its verses and the usual prayers. Glory... Both now... of the Feast. Troparion of the Feast. To the Saint of the day we sing at Compline, or as the Superior wills. Concerning this, (see fol. 3 and fol. 30)[^256].*

*At Matins: At "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Feast, thrice. After the Kathismata: Sessional hymns of the Feast. Canon of the Feast with the Heirmos on 14. But where the Tri-ode occurs, we sing the Canon of the Feast on 10, and in the Triodion on 4. After Ode 3: Sessional hymn in the Triodion, which was sung after the 2nd Kathisma; Glory... Both now... of the Feast. After Ode 6: Kontakion and Ikos of the Feast. At Ode 9: We sing the "More honorable." Exapostilarion of the Feast, twice. At the Praises: Stichera of the Feast on 4. Glory... Both now... of the Feast. At the Aposticha: The Idiomelon of the day, twice; and For the Martyrs. Glory... Both now... of the Feast. Troparion of the Feast. Litany, and Dismissal.*

*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Feast, Ode 4, on 6. After the Little Entrance: Troparion of the Feast; Glory... Both now... Kontakion of the Feast. Prokeimenon, Alleluia, and Communion Hymn of the Feast; Epistle and Gospel of the day.*

## Tuesday of Cheesefare at Matins

*At the 1st Stichology, Sessional hymn in the Octoechos. At the 2nd Stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom and the Word" (Премꙋ́дрость и҆ Сло́во):*[^257]

O all-good Word, Who with the Father and the Spirit didst create things visible together with things invisible by Thine ineffable wisdom: be pleased in Thy compassion that we pass through the season of the radiant Fast in profound peace, dissolving the delusion of bitter sin, granting compunction, tears of healing, and forgiveness of offenses...

...forgiveness of offenses: that with an undoubting soul, singing in a spirit of love, we may praise Thy sovereign power with the Angels!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

Having ineffably conceived Wisdom and the Word in thy womb, O Mother of God, thou didst give birth unto Him Who holds the world, and in thine arms didst hold Him Who embraces all, the Nourisher of all and Creator and Lord; therefore I pray thee, O all-pure Virgin, and in faith I glorify thee, that I may be delivered from my transgressions when I am to stand before the face of my Creator, O Sovereign Lady, pure Virgin; do thou grant me thy help then: for thou canst do whatsoever thou wilt, O All-immaculate One!

*And we read the Homily of Saint Basil the Great on Thanksgiving, or on Fasting: "Pray, pray, O my people," which is appointed on Wednesday, (see fol. 84)[^258].*

*We sing the Canons of the Octoechos and the Menaion as was indicated on Monday, (see fol. 75)[^259]; likewise follow throughout the rest.*

*If the Meeting of the Lord falls on Cheesefare Tuesday: then we sing the Tuesday Tri-ode on Cheesefare Wednesday at Compline. Everything else is as directed on Monday, (see fol. 73)[^260].*

*Tri-ode [Canon], the Work of Lord Joseph [the Hymnographer], Tone 3.*[^261]

*Ode 2. Heirmos:* As the rain upon the grass, and as the frost upon the hay, so let my words come down upon the earth! (Deut. 32:2).

*Troparion:* As the dawn hath the ray of repentance shone forth unto us: the portals of bright abstinence!

*Troparion:* Deliver me from the outer darkness, O Christ, by the multitude of Thy mercy, as Thou alone art plenteous in mercy! (Matt. 22:13).

*Glory... Triadikon:* Snatch me from the fire, O Savior, and from the sleepless worm, cleansed by diligence in repentance! (Mark 9:44).

*Both now... Theotokion:* O all-praised Virgin, be thou unto us the way of repentance, leading unto the portals of salvation!

*Another Tri-ode [Canon, by St. Theodore the Studite]. Heirmos, Tone 2: Give heed, O my people...*

*Troparion:* The season of repentance is arrived, O Lord! Let us strive, lovers of Christ, to cleanse ourselves from all transgressions, and to appear blessed before the Master!

*Troparion:* Let us strive to accomplish now unceasingly a pure, sinless fast, that we may find remission of transgressions!

*Troparion:* Thou alone, O Christ, art plenteous in mercy, O Good One, ever receiving all in repentance: therefore we all ever glorify Thee!

*Glory... Triadikon:* Let us glorify the Trinity in Unity: unsevered, unconfused, undivided in hypostasis, yet divided as God in Persons!

*Both now... Theotokion:* O pure Virgin Mother, entreat the Word Whom thou didst enflesh as man, O Virgin, that He may save Thy servants from tribulations!

*Heirmos:* Give heed, O My people, unto My law; incline your ear unto the words of My mouth: for I have called upon Thy Name, O Lord! (Ps. 77:1 [LXX 77:1 / MT 78:1]; Deut. 32:1).

*Ode 8. Heirmos:* Him Who was begotten of the beginningless Father before the ages as God, and in these latter times was clothed in flesh from the Virgin as perfect man and true God: praise, bless, and supreme-exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:* The time of remorse is come, to show fruits of repentance worthy of God! Strive, O my soul: arise and cry aloud diligently in fasting and prayers: Praise, bless, and supreme-exalt Him unto all ages! (Matt. 3:8; Luke 3:8).

*Troparion:* Purify the senses of the soul, brethren, and cleave unto the Lord in patterns of compunction, crying purely, honoring Him with repentance: Praise, bless, and supreme-exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:* Let us strive by diligent prayer to put to death the enemy who ensnared us through belly-pleasing memories and lack of restraint in petty lusts that burn the senses, singing, blessing, and supreme-exalting Him unto all ages!

*Both now... Theotokion:* O Maiden, who didst make heavenly our earthly mingled nature: show forth the furrow of my frozen and corrupted soul fruitful with virtues by the rain of thy compassions, crying and supreme-exalting Christ unto the ages!

*Another Tri-ode [Canon, by St. Theodore the Studite]. Heirmos: In the bush unto Moses...*

*Troparion:* Let us now thoroughly purify ourselves, not only with foods, but also with deeds; and let us faithful begin with fervent intent, that we may appear joyful unto the Creator unto the ages!

*Troparion:* Thus it behooves us to fast: not in enmity and strife, not in envy and contention, not in vainglory and hidden deceit; but even as Christ did, in chastity! (Rom. 13:13; 1 Cor. 3:3).

*Troparion:* They that show mercy unto the poor, it is said, wisely lend unto the Savior, turning away from unfitting pleasures: for He gives richly the recompense of good things unto all ages! (Prov. 19:17).

*Bless we the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord!* (Dan. 3:56 [LXX]).

*Triadikon:* Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit: for God is One in Three. With the Angels unceasingly we men hymn Thee, O all-holy Trinity, unto all ages!

*Both now... Theotokion:* Rejoice, O pure Virgin, Mother of God Mary, propitiation for the world, horn of the Orthodox: pray that they who glorify thee may be delivered from the unquenchable fire unto all ages!

*We praise, we bless, we worship the Lord!*

*Heirmos:* Him Who prefigured of old upon Mount Sinai the wonder of the Virgin in the bush unto Moses: sing, bless, and supreme-exalt Him unto all ages! (Exod. 3:2).

*Ode 9. Heirmos:* Thee, the unburnt Bush and holy Virgin, Mother of the Light and Mother of God, the hope of us all, do we magnify! (Exod. 3:2).

*Troparion:* Open unto us the most pure bowels of Thy mercy, O Thou Who hast granted repentance unto men for salvation, O only Savior, plenteous in mercy!

*Troparion:* Alas, what a dread, inexorable throne is that, before which we must give an account of those evil things which we have committed in life! (Rom. 14:12; 2 Cor. 5:10).

*Troparion:* Rain down upon us a drop of Thy tender compassion, O Benefactor, drowning all our defilement, granting us occasions of repentance!

*Troparion:* Having brightly entered with a fervent heart into the portals of the Holy Fast, let us bring thanksgiving songs unto Christ!

*Theotokion:* Hymning thee piously as our mighty protectress and sure hope, we pray: Entreat Thy Son, O Virgin, that we may be saved!

*Another Tri-ode [Canon, by St. Theodore the Studite]. Heirmos: Thy virginity...*

*Troparion:* Let us now thoroughly purify ourselves in fasting and in tears, and in great amendments of life, that we may find great mercy!

*Troparion:* Let us now emulate the wise virgins, O lovers of Christ, and strive to meet Christ with bright lamps! (Matt. 25:1–13).

*Troparion:* Let us now emulate Elijah who fasted and fled from the wicked Jezebel, that we also may be lifted up from the earth! (3 Kings 19:1–8 [MT 1 Kings 19:1–8]; 4 Kings 2:11 [MT 2 Kings 2:11]).

*Glory... Triadikon:* Holy, Holy, Holy, thrice-holy Father Almighty, and Son consubstantial with the Spirit: One Godhead! (Isa. 6:3; Rev. 4:8).

*Both now... Theotokion:* In thee do we boast, and unto thee do we flee, O pure and venerable ever-virgin: deliver us all from torment!

*Heirmos:* Thy virginity, O undefiled Mother of God, which the fire of the Godhead did not consume, do we ever magnify! Then: Glory to God in the highest...

*At the Aposticha, we sing the Idiomelon Sticheron, Tone 3:* Fondly, O ye peoples, let us embrace the Fast: for the beginning of spiritual struggles is arrived! Let us leave aside bodily luxury; let us cause the gifts of the soul to grow; let us suffer together as servants of Christ, that we may also be glorified together...

*Idiomelon Sticheron (continued):* ...as children of God, and that the Holy Spirit may dwell within us and enlighten our souls! (Rom. 8:17).

*Verse: We have been filled in the morning with Thy mercy... (Ps. 89:14 [LXX]).*

*And again, the same Sticheron.*

*Verse: And let the brightness of the Lord our God be upon us... (Ps. 89:17 [LXX]).*

*For the Martyrs:* Having cast aside the fear of kings and tyrants, the soldiers of Christ boldly and valiantly confessed Him, the Lord, God, and King of all; and they pray for our souls!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:* O Mother of God, protectress of all who pray to thee: in thee do we hope, and in thee do we boast, in thee is all our hope! Entreat Him Who was born of thee for thine unprofitable servants!

*Then: "It is good to give thanks unto the Lord..." (Ps. 91:2 [LXX 91:2 / MT 92:1]). Trisagion, 30. And the Dismissal.*

## Tuesday of Cheesefare at Vespers

*From the beginning of Vespers, we begin the Prostrations. The usual Kathisma in order.*

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera in the Octoechos: of Repentance, 3; and in the Menaion to the Saint, 3, as usual. Glory... to the Saint. But if he have none, Glory... Both now... Stavrotheotokion in the Octoechos. Prokeimenon of the day. "Vouchsafe, O Lord." The usual litany.*

*Then, at the Aposticha, Tone 1:*

Joyfully let us faithful receive the God-inspired covenant of the Fast, even as of old the Ninevites, and again the harlot and the publicans, which is the preaching of repentance from John! By abstinence let us prepare ourselves for migration unto Sion of the Master's sacred ministry; by tears let us thoroughly purify ourselves therein in the divine washing; let us pray to behold the fulfillment of the Pascha prefigured here, and the manifestation of truth; let us prepare ourselves for the veneration of the Cross and the Resurrection of Christ God, crying unto Him: Put us not to shame in our expectation, O Lover of mankind! (Jonah 3:5; Matt. 21:31–32; Luke 7:37–50; 18:13).

*And the Verses as yesterday, (see fol. 72)[^262].*

*For the Martyrs:* O goodly was your merchandise, O Saints: for ye gave blood, and inherited the heavens; and having been tried for a brief time, ye rejoice eternally! Truly goodly was your merchandise: for having left behind corruptible things, ye received incorruptible things; and rejoicing with the Angels, ye unceasingly hymn the Consubstantial Trinity!

*Glory... Both now... Stavrotheotokion, Tone 1, Podoben: "O all-lauded martyrs" (Прехва́лныи мч҃ннци):*[^263]

Beholding Him upon the Tree of old Whom she had borne from a seedless womb, the undefiled Ewe, unable to bear the wounding of her maternal heart, tearing her hair, cried aloud: O Thou Who holdest all creation by Thy nod, how art Thou lifted up upon the Cross as one condemned, willing in every way to save mankind? (John 19:25).

*Now dismissest Thou Thy servant in peace... After the Trisagion: Troparion: "O Virgin Mother of God, rejoice..." One prostration. Glory... "O Baptist of Christ..." One prostration. Both now... "Supplicate for us..." One prostration. "Beneath thy compassion..." Without prostration. "Lord, have mercy," 40, and the Prayer. Another Priest: "Lord, have mercy," thrice. Glory... Both now... "More honorable than the Cherubim..." In the Name of the Lord [bless, father].*

**Priest:** God be compassionate unto us, and bless us...

*And three great prostrations with the Prayer of Saint Ephrem: "O Lord and Master of my life..." and the other 12 [metanias]. At the end, all together: "O Lord and Master..." Trisagion; after "Our Father": "Lord, have mercy," 12. Glory... Both now... "Lord, have mercy," twice; "Lord, bless." And the Dismissal.*

*GREAT COMPLINE [METHIMON]: On this evening Great Compline is chanted with prostrations.*[^264]

*When we awake: We chant the Trisagion. The Lenten Troparia which are said on Tuesday evening: We chant the Trisagion Troparia: "Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us..." After "It is truly meet": The Troparia of the Day and of the Temple. Small Dismissal.*

*Likewise do we also on Thursday evening [before Friday].*

*If the Meeting of the Lord falls on Cheesefare Friday: then we chant the Friday Canon together with the Tri-ode on this Cheesefare Tuesday at Compline.*

*On the Forefeast of the Meeting of the Lord (Chapter 44):*[^265]

*If the Forefeast of the Meeting of the Lord falls on Wednesday or on Friday of Cheesefare Week:*

*On Tuesday or on Thursday in the evening: At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Forefeast, 3; and to the Saint, 3. Glory... Both now... of the Forefeast. At the Aposticha, Sticheron in the Triodion, twice; and For the Martyrs. Glory... Both now... of the Forefeast. Litany, and three great prostrations, and the Dismissal; and at the end, 3 prostrations. At Matins...*

*Wednesday of Cheesefare.*

*At Matins: At "God is the Lord": Troparion of the Forefeast, twice; Glory... to the Saint; Both now... of the Forefeast.*

*After the 1st Kathisma: Sessional hymns in the Octoechos in the Tone. After the 2nd Kathisma: Sessional hymns in the Triodion, twice; Glory... Both now... Theotokion.*

*Canon of the Forefeast with the Heirmos, on 6; and in the Menaion to the Saint, on 4; and in the Triodion, on 4.*

*When the Tri-ode arrives: We sing the Canon of the Forefeast with the Heirmos, on 4; and to the Saint in the Menaion, on 2; and in the Triodion, on 8.*

*After Ode 3: Kontakion to the Saint, and Sessional hymn, twice; Glory... to the Saint; Both now... of the Forefeast.*

*After Ode 6: Kontakion of the Forefeast. After Ode 9: Sessional hymn to the Saint; Glory... Both now... of the Forefeast.*

*At the Aposticha: Sticheron in the Triodion, Idiomelon of the day, twice; and For the Martyrs. Glory... Both now... of the Forefeast. Litany, and 3 great prostrations. And the First Hour.*

*At the First Hour: Troparion of the Forefeast; Glory... to the Saint; Both now... Theotokion. After the Trisagion: Kontakion of the Forefeast; "Lord, have mercy," 40; and the Prayer: "Thou Who at every season..." "Lord, have mercy," thrice; Glory... Both now... "More honorable than the Cherubim..." and 3 great prostrations. Prayer: "O Christ, the True Light..." and the Dismissal, and 3 prostrations. Likewise on the other Hours we sing the Troparion of the Forefeast; after the Trisagion, Kontakion of the Forefeast; and at every Hour, after "More honorable," 3 great prostrations.*

*If the Feast of the Meeting of the Lord falls on Cheesefare Wednesday or on Friday:*

*At Small Vespers, and at Great [Vespers], and at Matins, the entire service is performed without change, even as was indicated on Cheesefare Friday, (see fol. 73)[^266].*

*And the Wednesday Canon and the Tri-ode we sing on Cheesefare Thursday at Compline.*

*And the Idiomelon of the day, which is at the Aposticha of Matins, we sing with the Feast at the Praises with Glory. And at the end of Vespers, and of Matins, and of every Hour and service, there are 3 great prostrations only. At the Hours: Troparion and Kontakion of the Feast. At the Sixth Hour: Troparion of the Prophecy is said, and the Prophecy is read.*

*And the Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom is celebrated in its own time.*

*And there is eating once in the day on Cheesefare Wednesday and Friday, even if this Feast [or another of the great feasts] should fall upon them.*

*And Vespers is celebrated according to the day, in its own time. After the customary Psalm: Kathisma 11 [Ps. 77–84]. But if an All-Night Vigil was celebrated anywhere, then the Kathisma is not said, but after the customary Psalm: At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Feast, 3; and to the Saint who is appointed, 3. Glory... Both now... of the Feast; if he have none: Glory... Both now... of the Feast. Prokimenon of the day, and the Prophecy. Then Prokimenon, Tone 6: "Let Israel hope in the Lord" (Ps. 129:7 [LXX / MT 130:7]). At the Aposticha: In the Triodion, Idiomelon of the day, twice; and For the Martyrs. Glory... to the Saint; Both now... of the Feast; if he have none: Glory... Both now... of the Feast. Troparion to the Saint, if he have one; Glory... Both now... of the Feast, and the Dismissal.*

*On the Afterfeast of the Meeting of the Lord:*

*If the Afterfeast of the Meeting of the Lord falls on Cheesefare Wednesday or on Friday:*

*(see fol. 73)[^267] The entire service is sung without change, even as was indicated on Cheesefare Monday.*

*At Matins: After the 9th Kathisma [or After the Kathismata], [Canon of the Feast] with the Heirmos, on 6; and in the Menaion, on 4; and in the Triodion, on 4.*

*When the Tri-ode arrives: We sing the Canon of the Feast, on 4; and in the Menaion, on 4; and in the Triodion...*

*...on 8. And the rest as on Friday. And at the end of every singing and Hour: At the end of Vespers, at Compline, at Matins, at the end of the First Hour, and at the other Hours, and at every singing, only at the end as was said: three great prostrations (see fol. 84)[^268].*

*And on Wednesday at Vespers: Stichera to the Feast, and in the Menaion as usual. Glory... Both now... of the Feast. Then the Prokimenon and Prophecy of the day. At the Aposticha: In the Triodion, Idiomelon of the day, twice; and For the Martyrs. Glory... Both now... to the Feast.*

*On the Finding of the Head of the Forerunner, Chapter 54, in the middle:*[^269]

*If the Finding of the Precious Head of John the Forerunner falls on Cheesefare Wednesday or on Friday:*

*(see fol. 74)[^270] His service is sung even as was indicated on Cheesefare Friday, only in the evening: The Prokimenon and Prophecy of the day; then Prokimenon, Tone 6: "Let Israel hope in the Lord" (Ps. 129:7 [LXX / MT 130:7]); then the Prophecies of the Forerunner. At the end of Vespers: 3 great prostrations, and the Dismissal, and the customary procession into the Narthex.*

*Likewise also, at Compline, at the Midnight Office, and at Matins, at the Hours, and at the end of every service: three great prostrations [as aforesaid]. At Matins: Canon of the Mother of God with the Heirmos, on 4; and in the Triodion, on 4; and to the Forerunner, on 6. Katavasia: "I shall open my mouth."*

*When the Tri-ode arrives, we omit the Canon of the Mother of God, and we sing the Canon of the Forerunner with the Heirmos, on 6, and in the Triodion in its own order (see fol. 74)[^271].*

*And all the rest as on Friday. At the end of every singing and Hour: 3 great prostrations, even as was aforesaid. At the Hours: Troparion and Kontakion of the Forerunner. At the Sixth Hour: Troparion of the Prophecy, and the Prophecy of the day is read. The Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom. Beatitudes of the Forerunner, Odes 3 and 6. Prokimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, Gospel, and Communion Verse of the Forerunner.*

## Wednesday of Cheesefare

*The Midnight Office with 30 prostrations.*

*At Matins: We sing "Alleluia" in the current Tone of the Octoechos, and the Trinity Hymns [Triadika] of the Tone, and the customary two Kathismata.*

*Know: That on Wednesday and Friday of Cheesefare Week, when we sing "Alleluia," we also make prostrations in the evening and in the morning, even as was aforesaid, and we observe the Fast according to the rule. But we do not sing the Inter-Hours [Mesoria], even if any Saint should occur who has a Troparion, except only for the Feast of the Meeting of the Lord, or the Finding of the Precious Head of John the Forerunner...*

*...or the Temple of any Saint: Then we sing the Troparion, and we do not make prostrations, except only 3 great prostrations, even as hath many times been said, (see fol. 78)[^272].*

*If there be none of these three feasts:*

*At the 1st Stichology: Sessional hymns in the Octoechos.*

*At the 2nd Stichology: Sessional Hymn, Tone 2:*

Grant that we, Thy humble servants, may in peace pass through this season of the fast, an exceeding purification, O Christ God, Who lovest mankind; cast down the wiles of the enemy, and by Thy Cross save all, O Thou Who alone knowest the secrets of the heart! (Ps. 43:22 [LXX 43:22 / MT 44:21]).

*Glory... the same. Both now... Stavrotheotokion:*

Standing by Thy Cross, she that bare Thee without seed, unable to endure beholding Thee unjustly suffering, wept aloud with lamentation and cried unto Thee: How sufferest Thou, Who art by nature impassible, O sweetest Son? I praise Thine extreme loving-kindness! (John 19:25).

*And we read the Homily of Saint Basil the Great on Fasting, whose beginning is: "Be comforted, be comforted" (Isa. 40:1); or: "Pray, pray, O my people."*[^273]

*Then: Psalm 50. We do not say the Canticles.*

*CANON: In the Octoechos, to the Cross with the Heirmos, on 6; and in the Menaion to the Saint, on 4; and in the Triodion, on 4.*

*The second Canon to the Mother of God in the Octoechos is omitted entirely.*

*In whichever Odes the Triodion is sung, it omits those same Odes in the Octoechos of the Cross.*

*And we sing first the Canon to the Saint in the Menaion with the Heirmos, on 6; and in the Triodion, on 8.*

*And at the end, the Heirmos that is in the Triodion. But since the Tri-ode has two Heirmoi, in the Menaion also we omit those same Odes, and sing them off in the preceding Odes, omitting also there the Odes in the Octoechos of the Cross.*

*Canon, Tone 4. Ode 1. The Work of Lord Andrew of Crete:*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:* Sing we unto the Lord my God, Who led forth the people from the bondage of Egypt, and covered the chariots of Pharaoh and his power! (Exod. 15:1–4, 19).

*Troparion:* He gave Himself unto those that hunger for our instruction, overcoming the tempter, showing forth our victory, and establishing the bounds of our labor![^274] (Matt. 4:2–4).

*Troparion:* Blessed Moses on Sinai, through abstinence was accounted worthy to speak with God face to face: let us faithful emulate him! (Exod. 33:11; 34:28).

*Troparion:* Purify Thy people, O Lord, and having looked down as Good with Thy merciful eye, visit all through the multitude of Thy mercy!

*Theotokion:* Unto thee do we all flee, O Virgin Mother of God, as unto a steadfast champion, moving thee unto prayer to deliver thy flock from every affliction!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:* My heart is established in the Lord my God; therefore the weak are girded with strength! (1 Kings 2:1, 4 [MT 1 Sam. 2:1, 4]).

*Troparion:* Marvelous Enoch by abstinence was translated from the earth: emulating his example, let us translate our life from corruption! (Gen. 5:24; Heb. 11:5).

*Troparion:* By prayers and by fasts let us entreat the Redeemer: for the Creator rejoices at the repentance of His creatures! (Luke 15:7, 10).

*Troparion:* Make ready, O my soul, and purify thyself before the Passion of Christ, that by the Resurrection thou mayest spiritually celebrate with Him! (Rom. 6:4–5).

*Theotokion:* As having given birth unto God, pray unceasingly for us: for unto thee, O Mother of God, do we sinners flee!

*Then the Tri-ode. Ode 3, Tone 2.*

*Heirmos: Upon the rock of faith... By Joseph.*

*Troparion:* Thou didst stretch out Thy hands upon Thy Cross; Thou didst by Thy deadness slay the curse; Thou didst by Thy Passion make mankind alive: therefore we sing Thy divine Crucifixion, O Jesus, Lover of mankind, our God!

*Troparion:* Slain by the eating of the tree, we have been made alive by the Tree of Thy Cross; therefore we offer it unto Thee in prayer: Send down grace and mercy upon us, O Benefactor and Master, plenteous in mercy!

*Troparion:* The portals of fasting have been opened; the stadium of abstinence draws near: with fervent zeal let us arise...

*Troparion (continued):* ...receiving the God-given gift that quenches the thirst of transgressions!

*Troparion:* The blessed season of the Fast, shedding forth the ray of repentance, let us vigilantly approach with eager love, driving away the deepest darkness of slothfulness, all of us rejoicing!

*Troparion:* Let us proclaim with wisdom the hallowing of the Fast, the abstinence from passions; let us cry aloud in tears unto the Master: Grant us, O Lord, grace cooperating with Thy desires, O Thou Who art plenteous in mercy!

*Troparion:* Having received the gift of the Fast, let us glorify Him Who gave it for our salvation, and let us fulfill this laboriously, that we may receive remission of our transgressions from the hand of the Creator!

*Theotokion:* Calm the tempest of my passions; heal the wounds of my soul; raise me up from the sleep of despondency by thy mediation and protection, O all-pure Sovereign Lady, Virgin Mother!

*Another Heirmos, in the same Tone: My barren mind...*

*Troparion:* When Thou, O Savior, wast willingly lifted up, all earthly creation was shaken, and the veil of the Temple was rent in twain! (Matt. 27:51).

*Troparion:* Thou wast wounded upon the Cross, O my Jesus, for my sake, and pierced in Thy side, O Good One, therefore in faith I worship Thy dominion! (John 19:34).

*Triadikon:* Bending the knee upon the earth, I worship the Father, I glorify the Son, and I praise the Spirit: the One-willed and Three-hypostatic Beginning!

*Theotokion:* Incomprehensible is the mystery of thy birthgiving, O pure one: for without knowing man thou dost give birth unto God, O Mary, and dost remain incorrupt!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*

*Troparion:* Having hallowed the season of the Fast by Thy power, O Christ, deliver us all who worship Thee from the snares of the enemy!

*Heirmos:* Show my barren mind to be fruitful in virtues, O good Cultivator and Planter of good things, by Thy compassion!

*Sessional hymn, in the Menaion to the Saint. Glory... Both now... Stavrotheotokion.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:* I heard Thy report and was afraid; I considered Thy works and was amazed, O Lord! (Hab. 3:2 [LXX]).

*Troparion:* Abraham, by virtues and hospitality, received the Trinity when the Angels came! (Gen. 18:1–8).

*Troparion:* An unstealable wealth is the gift of abstinence: and they that enrich themselves therewith shall be enriched with the things of God! (Matt. 6:19–20).

*Glory... Troparion:* Come, let us weep together in word and crying out in faith: O God, cleanse us who have greatly sinned!

*Both now... Theotokion:* O Mother without blemish, blessed and pure Virgin: pray unto Thy Son and our God for the whole world!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:* Thou Who didst make light to dawn, and didst illumine the morning, and didst display the day: Glory to Thee, glory to Thee, O Jesus, Son of God!

*Troparion:* Moses of old, having fasted upon Mount Sinai, was shown to be a beholder of God; and Elijah was taken up upon a fiery chariot! (Exod. 34:28; 4 Kings 2:11 [MT 2 Kings 2:11]).

*Troparion:* Through fasting of old the wise Isaiah tasted of the live coal with the tongs, when it touched his lips! (Isa. 6:6–7).

*Glory... Troparion:* Daniel, having fasted of old, and the Three Children together, broke the jaws of the lions and extinguished the furnace of burning flame! (Dan. 6:22; 3:25 [LXX]).

*Both now... Theotokion:* O pure Birthgiver of God, who didst bear God incorruptibly in thy womb: preserve thy flock and keep it uncondemned!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:* As...

*Heirmos (continued):* ...Thou didst deliver the Prophet Jonah, O Christ God, so also lead me up from the depth of transgressions, and save me, O Thou Who alone lovest mankind! (Jonah 2:1–10).

*Troparia:*

*Troparion:* Honorable fasting once saved Jonah in the whale, O Savior: let us therefore fast from the heart, and flee that corruption which is in Gehenna!

*Troparion:* The Ninevites again turned aside the wrath of God by fervent repentance and love: therefore let us all now lovingly emulate them! (Jonah 3:5–10).

*Glory... Troparion:* The present Fast calls us unto repentance: let us therefore all run with love, and understand what the gift of abstinence is!

*Both now... Theotokion:* O thou who didst ineffably give birth unto the Savior of the world, Christ our God: cease not to entreat Him to save the whole race of those that hymn thee!

*Kontakion of the Martyrs, Tone 2:* O Thou Who from the net...[^275]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:* O God of the fathers, put us not to shame, we pray Thee, O Lover of mankind; but deal with us in Thy meekness unto ages! (Dan. 3:34, 42 [LXX]).

*Troparion:* Let us also be as that Daniel of old, who by fasting once tamed the roaring lions in the den! (Dan. 6:16–22).

*Troparion:* Let us emulate the three youths with fear, that we may escape the fire of Gehenna, even as they escaped the Babylonian furnace! (Dan. 3:20–27 [LXX]).

*Glory... Troparion:* Let us fast also with a pure heart, and cleanse the body, and in all things hallow the spirit unto God! (2 Cor. 7:1; 1 Thess. 5:23).

*Both now... Theotokion:* O Virgin, pure Mother, cease not to pray unto the Pre-eternal Word incarnate of thee, that our souls may be saved!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:* Christ the King, Whom the captive youths confessed in the furnace, saying with a great voice: All ye works of the Lord, sing the Lord, and supreme-exalt Him unto ages! (Dan. 3:57 [LXX]).

*Troparion:* Joseph, having fasted first, fled from mingling with the lawless woman and attained the kingdom: therefore let us also quench by fasting the inflamed darts of the enemy Belial! (Gen. 39:7–12; Eph. 6:16).

*Troparion:* By fasting David established victory over the alien, and found the kingdom: therefore let us also by abstinence receive victories over enemies, and be crowned in the Lord! (1 Kings 17:40–51 [MT 1 Sam. 17:40–51]; Ps. 108:24 [LXX / MT 109:24]).

*Glory... Troparion:* Let us also preserve their virtues: the fortitude of Job, the guilelessness of Jacob, the faith of Abraham, the chastity of Joseph, and the valor of David! (Job 1:21–22; Gen. 25:27; 15:6; 39:9; 1 Kings 17).

*Both now... Theotokion:* Christ the King, Whom the Virgin Mary bore for us, and after childbirth remained a pure virgin: all ye works of the Lord, sing the Lord, and supreme-exalt Him unto ages!

*Tri-ode. Another Heirmos:* Unto God, Who descended into the fiery furnace unto the Hebrew children, and turned the flame into dew: Sing, ye works, and supreme-exalt Him unto all ages! (Dan. 3:49–50, 57 [LXX]).

*Troparion:* Cross-revealingly, Moses, lifting up his hands upon the mountain, conquered the enemies; but Thou, O Savior, having stretched out Thy hands upon the Cross, didst slay the all-destructive torment of Hades! (Exod. 17:11–12).

*Troparion:* Fastened with nails, O Christ, upon the Cross, and having opened Thy side, O Christ my Savior, Thou didst deliver the earthborn from the curse, and didst show them to be partakers of incomprehensible joy: therefore we bless Thy love toward mankind! (John 19:34; Gal. 3:13).

*Troparion:* The season of abstinence that has shone forth mentally enlightens the spiritual senses and drives away the gloom of passions: therefore let us embrace it from the heart, supreme-exalting Christ unto ages!

*Troparion:* All-destructive passions of the soul and deadly pleasures the fast slays, and truly makes stable the inclinations and motions of the heart: let us therefore lovingly receive it in faith!

*Troparion:* Not the beginning of the fast do the faithful know this present holy day to be...

*Troparion (continued):* ...honoring it with pure intent, but we call it the entrance and arrival unto the threshold of the fast!

*Theotokion:* Open unto us that knock and call upon thy mercy, O speedy refuge of men, and steadfast helper in afflictions, O Virgin, and certain protectress of those in sorrows!

*Another Heirmos: In the Bush unto Moses...*

*Troparion:* Lifted up upon the Cross, O Master of all, Thou didst raise up Adam and Eve again, and didst lead them into Paradise, O Christ the Savior, who praise Thee unto all ages!

*Troparion:* When Thou, O Christ, wast willingly lifted up upon the Cross, the sun's rays were darkened through fear, and the day went down; therefore the thief confessed Thee to be God unto all ages! (Luke 23:40–45).

*Bless we the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord!* (Dan. 3:56 [LXX]).

*Triadikon:* Equal to the Father have we believed the Son and the Holy Spirit to be, the Holy Trinity in one Godhead, Whom we worship in faith, ever trampling upon the teaching of Arius!

*Both now... Theotokion:* How shall I hymn thee as is meet, O Virgin Mother of God, darkened as I am now by foul sins? Yet, O all-pure one, forgive my boldness, my labor-loving songs!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*

*Troparion:* O Cross, hope of the faithful, weapon of kings, glory of priests, strength of monastics: by thy power save all that glorify thee unto ages!

*We praise, we bless, we worship the Lord, singing and supreme-exalting Him unto all ages!*

*Heirmos:* In the Bush unto Moses the wonder of the Virgin on Mount Sinai Who prefigured of old: Sing, bless, and supreme-exalt Him unto all ages! (Exod. 3:2).

*And we sing the "More honorable." Ode 9. Heirmos:* We all magnify Thy love toward mankind, O Christ our Savior, the glory of Thy servants and crown of the faithful, Who hast magnified the memory of her that bare Thee!

*Troparia:*

*Troparion:* Moses in Egypt parted the sea, and led the people through, and fed them in the impassable wilderness, working wonders by fasting! (Exod. 14:21–22; 16:4–35).

*Troparion:* Joshua the son of Nun through abstinence sanctified the people, and inherited the Promised Land, having first crossed the Jordan! (Josh. 3:5, 17; 4:1).

*Troparion:* Gideon of old, only with the three hundred men who lapped, conquered the aliens through abstinence and prayer: emulating him, let us also be made like unto him! (Judg. 7:5–7, 21).

*Theotokion:* Rejoice, all-honorable, pure boast of virginity, foundation of Angels and helper of men, joy of the world, Mary and Mother and handmaid of our God!

*Then the Tri-ode. Heirmos:* Unto God the Word, Who by ineffable wisdom came to renew Adam who had fallen grievously through the food of corruption, and for our sake was ineffably incarnate of the Holy Virgin: With one mind, ye faithful, let us magnify in songs!

*Troparia:*

*Troparion:* Having stretched out Thy divine hands upon the Cross, Thou didst gather together those that were scattered before, O Redeemer; and having mediated for that which was condemned unto the Father, Thou didst offer as a gift the substance of earthly beings: therefore we sing Thy most-pure Crucifixion! (John 11:52; Eph. 2:14–16).

*Troparion:* The grace of the holy fast that hath shone forth sends out rays that purify us, and again the flow of thoughts, driving away the gloom from our pupils: therefore, ye faithful, let us lovingly receive it!

*Troparion:* With the nails of Thy divine hands, Thou, O Compassionate One, didst nail our sins to the Cross; and with the spear in Thy side Thou didst tear asunder the wicked handwriting of our transgressions and grievous falls: therefore we sing Thy most-pure Crucifixion! (Col. 2:14; John 19:34).

*Troparion:* Radiant fasting, having drawn for us a chalice of divine gifts...

*Troparion (continued):* ...having drawn for us a cup of divine gifts, today sets it forth before all: let us who desire to partake thereof for the profit of the soul pray to the Master of all to work together with us!

*Troparion:* The most excellent path of virtues is opened, the stadium of divine fasts, behold, is made ready for all! Ye who desire to contend lawfully, ask Christ to grant unto us a peaceful season of swift course! (2 Tim. 2:5).

*Theotokion:* By thy speedy prayer and vigilant protection, by thy sovereign aid, O pure Lady, preserve now thy faithful servants from every adversary assault, and deceit, and transgressions, and save us from afflictions!

*Tri-ode. Another Heirmos:* More than nature in the flesh...

*Troparion:* Nailed willingly upon the Tree of the Cross, Thou didst deliver us all from the curse of the Law (Gal. 3:13): therefore as is due we magnify Thee, O Christ!

*Troparion:* All of us worship Thy Sufferings, O Savior, which Thou didst willingly accept, that Thou mightest deliver the human race from the bondage of the enemy!

*Triadikon:* Co-eternal is the Father, and He that non-sourcefully begat the Son, and the Spirit of one essence with the Father and the Son: an unmeasurable Trinity!

*Theotokion:* Pray to the Savior incarnate of thee, O Mary all-praised, to grant remission of transgressions unto us sinners!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*

*Troparion:* Kissing thee, O Cross, by thy power vouchsafe us to pass through the season of the fast in peace, and deliver us from the bondage of the enemy!

*Heirmos:* Her who beyond nature conceived in the womb in the flesh the Word Who shone forth timelessly from the Father, the only Theotokos, we the faithful magnify!

*Exapostilarion in the Octoechos: Trinity Hymns, Tone 3. After the Doxology ("Glory to God in the highest"):*

*At the Aposticha: Sticheron, Idiomelon, Tone 4:* While fasting from foods, O my soul, and of passions...

*Sticheron (continued):* ...not being cleansed, dost thou rejoice vainly in non-eating? For if this acceptable season be not unto thee for amendment, as deceitful shalt thou be abhorred of God, and shalt become like unto the evil demons who never eat! Nor yet, sinning, cast the fast away; but remain unshaken against the impulses of passions,[^276] deeming thyself to stand before the crucified Savior: nay rather, be crucified with Him Who was crucified for thy sake, crying unto Him: Remember me, O Lord, when Thou comest into Thy Kingdom! (Luke 23:42).

*Verse: We were filled in the morning with Thy mercy, O Lord, and we rejoiced and were glad: (Ps. 89:14 [LXX]). Again the same Sticheron.*

*Another Verse: And let the brightness of the Lord our God be upon us: (Ps. 89:17 [LXX]). Then, Martyrikon:*

*Martyrikon:* Neither tribulation, nor distress, nor famine, nor persecution, nor peril, nor the fury of wild beasts, nor sword, nor threatening fire were able to separate you from God, O all-praised martyrs! (Rom. 8:35). But rather by love toward Him, having contended as though in alien bodies, ye forgot nature and despised death: therefore according to the desert of your sufferings ye have received the reward, and have become heirs of the Heavenly Kingdom. Pray ye for our souls!

*Glory... Both now... Stavrotheotokion, the same Tone. Podoben: "O all-praised martyrs" (Prekhvalnii muchenitsy):*

A sword has pierced, O Son, said the Virgin, when on the Tree she beheld Christ hanging, and it tears my heart, O Master, even as Simeon foretold unto me of old (Luke 2:35). But arise and co-glorify, O Immortal One, Thy Mother and handmaid, who prays unto Thee!

*Then: "It is good to give praise" (Ps. 91:2 [LXX]). Trisagion. After the "Our Father": Troparion: "Standing in the temple of Thy glory..." "Lord, have mercy," 40, in a gentle voice. Then: "Bless, Father."*

**Priest:** Blessed and glorified is Christ our God, always, now and ever, and unto ages of ages.

**Choir:** Amen. "O Heavenly King..."

*And we make 3 great prostrations with the Prayer of Saint Ephraim:* **"O Lord and Master of my life..."** *And the remaining 12 prostrations lightly, for the sake of weariness...*

*Rubric (continued):* ...saying each to himself: "O God, cleanse me a sinner"; and again the same prayer:* **"O Lord and Master of my life..."** *entire, as one; and the sixteenth prostration.*

*And we join also the First Hour. The Abbot, or the Ecclesiarch, or the presiding Monk begins:*

**Reader:** "Come, let us worship," thrice, with 3 prostrations. At the First Hour, during which we also read the Catecheses of Theodore the Studite.[^277] At the First Hour, Troparion: "In the morning hear my voice..." (Ps. 5:4 [LXX]); "Make haste to anticipate..." "Order my steps..." (Ps. 118:133 [LXX]); and "Let my mouth be filled..." (Ps. 70:8 [LXX]). After the Trisagion, Lenten Troparia, according to their order: Tone 4: "Make haste to anticipate..." Glory... Tone 6: "O Teacher of wisdom..." Both now... "Pre-glorious Mother of God..." "Lord, have mercy," 40; and the Prayer: "Thou Who at every season..." "Lord, have mercy," thrice; Glory... Both now... "More honorable than the Cherubim..." In the Name of the Lord... and the common prostrations. Trisagion. "Lord, have mercy," 12. "O Christ, the True Light..." And the Dismissal, and the procession into the Narthex, and the Litiya for the Departed.*

*On the same Wednesday of Cheesefare:*

*We sing the Hours: the Third and the Sixth, with prostrations and with Lenten Troparia.*

*At the Sixth Hour, Troparion, Tone 3:*

O Holy King, All-powerful, Whom all creation fears and trembles before: save us, for Thou art able to forgive sins, as Thou art merciful! (Mark 2:7).

*Glory... And we chant the end of the Troparion:* For Thou art able to forgive sins, as Thou art merciful. *Both now... Again we chant the same Troparion entire.*

*First Prokimenon, Tone 6: O Lord, save Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance! (Ps. 27:9 [LXX]).*

*Verse: Unto Thee, O Lord, have I cried, O my God, be not silent unto me: (Ps. 27:1 [LXX]).*

*The Reading from the Prophecy of Joel (Joel 2:12–26):*

Thus saith the Lord: Turn ye unto Me with all your heart, and in fasting, and in sackcloth, and in weeping, and in mourning; and rend your hearts, and not your garments, and turn unto the Lord your God: for He is merciful and compassionate, long-suffering and plenteous in mercy, and repents of evil (Joel 2:12–13 [LXX]). Who knows if He will turn...

*Prophecy (continued):* ...and repent, and leave behind Him a blessing, even a sacrifice and a drink-offering unto the Lord our God? Sound the trumpet in Zion, sanctify a fast, proclaim a solemn assembly, gather the people, sanctify the congregation, select the elders, assemble the babes that suck the breasts; let the bridegroom go forth from his chamber, and the bride from her bridal closet! (Joel 2:14–16 [LXX]). Between the porch and the Altar shall the priests, the ministers of the Lord, weep, and say: Spare Thy people, O Lord, and give not Thine inheritance to reproach, that the heathen should rule over them, lest they should say among the nations: Where is their God? (Joel 2:17 [LXX]). And the Lord was jealous for His land, and spared His people. And the Lord answered and said unto His people: Behold, I send you corn, and wine, and oil, and ye shall be satisfied therewith, and I will no more make you a reproach among the nations (Joel 2:18–19 [LXX]). And him that is from the north will I drive far away from you, and will cast him out into a dry land; and I will destroy his face in the former sea, and his rear parts in the latter sea; and his rot shall come up, and his stench shall come up, because his deeds have been magnified! (Joel 2:20 [LXX]). Be of good courage, O earth, rejoice and be glad, for the Lord has magnified His doing! Be of good courage, ye beasts of the field, for the pastures of the wilderness have sprung up, for the tree has brought forth its fruit, the vine and the fig tree have yielded their strength! (Joel 2:21–22 [LXX]). And ye children of Zion, rejoice and be glad in the Lord your God, for He has given you food in righteousness, and He will rain down upon you the early and the latter rain, even as before. And the threshing floors shall be full of wheat, and the presses shall overflow... (Joel 2:23–24a [LXX]).

*Prophecy (continued):* ...with wine and oil. And I will restore unto you the years that the locust, and the caterpillar, and the rust, and the cankerworm have eaten, My great army which I sent among you. And eating ye shall eat and be satisfied, and shall praise the Name of the Lord your God, Who has dealt marvelously with you; and My people shall never be put to shame unto ages! (Joel 2:24b–26 [LXX]).

*Second Prokimenon, Tone 7: The Lord will give strength unto His people, and will bless His people with peace! (Ps. 28:11 [LXX]).*

*Verse: Bring unto the Lord, ye sons of God, bring unto the Lord young rams: (Ps. 28:1 [LXX]).*

*Be it known unto the Ecclesiarch, that he instruct the Paraecclesiarch, or the Canonarch, how to read the Prokimenon before the Paroimiai:[^278]*

*When the Canonarch chants the Troparion at the Sixth Hour, when the Singers finish the Troparion, the Canonarch chants "Glory...", and the Singers chant the end of the Troparion. The Reader chants "Both now...", and the Singers chant the Troparion entire, which the Canonarch announced unto them.*

*When thou chantest the Prokimenon for the Paroimiai: Do not say, "At Vespers, the Prokimenon..."; but only proclaim with a loud voice the Tone: whether Tone 1, or Tone 6, or whichever other Tone it be. Say not, "The Evening Prokimenon," but only whichever Tone it be.*

*Then: "O let Thy tender mercies speedily anticipate us..." (Ps. 78:8 [LXX]). Trisagion. And the rest of the customary Lenten office. And the Prayer: "O God and Lord of powers..." (of Saint Basil the Great). And together we sing also the Ninth Hour, and the Prayer: "O Master, Lord Jesus Christ our God..." We sing the Typika [Obednitsa]: At the Beatitudes, "In Thy Kingdom..." "Speedily hear..." Glory... Both now... "Remember us, O Lord..." and 3 prostrations. "The heavenly choir..." and the common prostrations; and after the "Our Father": Kontakion of the Temple, and to the Saint. Glory... Kontakion: "With the Saints give rest..." Both now... Kontakion: "O Protectress of Christians..." "Lord, have mercy," 40; Glory... Both now... "More honorable than the Cherubim..." 3 great common prostrations, and 12 other [light prostrations].*

*We sing: The second Choir begins Vespers.*

*Three prostrations. Psalm 103: "Bless the Lord, O my soul..." "Alleluia," twice; the 3rd "Alleluia" the Clerics sing with chant. Priest: Litany: "In peace let us pray to the Lord..." Choir: "Lord, have mercy."*

*On the same Wednesday of Cheesefare, at Vespers:*

*Stichera as usual in the Octoechos and in the Menaion. Then the Prokimenon:*

*[The Reader only announces the Tone, and does not say the word "Prokimenon," even as was said above.]*

*Tone 4: O God, in Thy Name save me, and in Thy power judge me! (Ps. 53:3 [LXX / MT 53:1]).*

*Verse: O God, hear my prayer, give ear to the words of my mouth: (Ps. 53:4 [LXX / MT 53:2]).*

*The Reading from the Prophecy of Joel (Joel 3:12–21 [LXX]):*

Thus saith the Lord: Let all the nations awake and go up to the Valley of Jehoshaphat: for there will I sit to judge all the heathen round about! Put ye in the sickles, for the harvest is ripe; come, tread, for the press is full, the vats overflow, for their wickedness is multiplied! (Joel 3:12–13 [LXX]). Voices resounded in the valley of judgment, for the day of the Lord is near in the valley of judgment! The sun and the moon shall be darkened, and the stars shall withdraw their shining (Joel 3:14–15 [LXX]). And the Lord shall cry out of Zion, and utter His voice from Jerusalem, and heaven and earth shall be shaken; but the Lord will spare His people, and strengthen the sons of Israel (Joel 3:16 [LXX]). And ye shall know that I am the Lord your God, Who dwell in Zion, My holy mountain: then shall Jerusalem be holy, and strangers shall no more pass through her (Joel 3:17 [LXX]). And it shall come to pass in that day, that the mountains shall drop down sweetness, and the hills shall flow with milk, and all the streams of Judah shall pour forth waters; and a fountain shall go forth from the house of the Lord, and water the valley of earthly measures (Joel 3:18 [LXX]). Egypt shall be a desolation, and Edom shall be a wilderness of destruction, for the injustices against the sons of Judah, because they shed righteous blood in their land (Joel 3:19 [LXX]). But Judah shall be inhabited unto ages, and Jerusalem from generation to generation (Joel 3:20 [LXX]). And I will avenge their blood, and will not leave it unpunished; and the Lord shall dwell in Zion! (Joel 3:21 [LXX]).

*Then we sing with melody, instead of "Let my prayer be set forth," the Prokimenon, Tone 6: Let Israel hope in the Lord, from henceforth and unto the age! (Ps. 130:3 [LXX / MT 131:3]).*

*Verse: Lord, my heart was not exalted, neither were mine eyes lifted up: (Ps. 130:1 [LXX / MT 131:1]).*

*Likewise do we also on Cheesefare Friday, even as was indicated on Wednesday. Then: "Vouchsafe, O Lord..."*

*At the Aposticha: Sticheron, Idiomelon, Tone 3:* The Lenten spring has shone forth, and the flower of repentance! Let us therefore cleanse ourselves, brethren, from every defilement, and crying out unto the Giver of Light let us say: Glory to Thee, O only Lover of mankind!

*Verse: Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes, O Thou that dwellest in heaven: (Ps. 122:1 [LXX]). Again the same Sticheron.*

*Then Verse: Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us: (Ps. 122:3 [LXX]). Martyrikon:*

*Martyrikon:* Thy Martyrs, O Lord, strengthened by faith, confirmed by hope, spiritually united in love of Thy Cross, dissolved the enemy's tyranny; and having obtained crowns, they pray with the Bodiless powers for our souls!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:* O holy Theotokos among women, unwedded Mother: pray unto the King and God Whom thou didst bear, that He may save us, as He loves mankind!

*"Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart..." After the Trisagion: Troparion to the Saint. Glory... Both now... His Kontakion. If there be none, then Troparion of the day; Glory... Both now... Kontakion according to the tone of the Troparion. Litany: "Have mercy on us, O God..." Three great prostrations. "Blessed be the Name of the Lord..." "I will bless the Lord..." "It is truly meet..." And the Dismissal. And the procession into the Narthex, and the customary Litiya for the Departed.*

*And be it understood: That in Palestine, which is in the land of Jerusalem, we did not receive from the Holy Fathers to celebrate the complete Liturgy on Wednesday and Friday of Cheesefare Week, nor yet the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts.[^279] On these two days we sing "Alleluia" with prostrations, at Vespers and at Matins, even as was said above, (see fol. 86 and 88)[^280].*

*If, however, the Feast of the Meeting of the Lord, or the Forerunner's [Finding], or the Temple [feast] of any Saint should fall upon these two days: on these three Feasts the Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom is celebrated. And also at the end of Matins, and at the end of the Hours, there are three great prostrations only. At the Sixth Hour, the Troparion of the Paroimia is said, and the Paroimia is read.*

*Rubric (continued):* ...And we join also the Ninth Hour. Vespers also takes place in its own time without Liturgy, for the sake of the Feast. At Vespers: Prokimenon of the day, and the Reading of the Feast; then the Readings of the day; and again the Prokimenon with singing:* "Let Israel hope in the Lord..." *Eating takes place once in the day on these two days, even if a Feast should occur upon them, (see fol. 86)[^281].*

*If there be none of the three feasts aforesaid: We make the customary prostrations, which on Tuesday at the [Ninth] Hour [were begun]; at Vespers from the beginning. We sing the great Ambon-Psalm [Ps. 103]. At Matins on both these days, and at the Hours, we perform the prostrations and the Fast.*

*After the dismissal of Vespers, we enter the Trapeza, and we eat at the fourth hour [of the afternoon] cheese and eggs, once in the day. We also drink wine, thanking God.*

*For this was instituted by the decree of the Holy Council concerning Wednesday and Friday of Cheesefare Week: On these two days, at the time of the fourth hour, to sing the Hours with Vespers, and thus to eat cheese and eggs, and things like unto them.[^282] This was instituted on account of the heretics, the Andusanites and the Sabellians, and many other heretics whom we anathematize; as also the past week, of Artzivur, in which the cursed Armenians fast.*

*Unto us Christians, however, relaxation is given by the Canons of the Holy Fathers, and we eat during that week of Artzivur on Wednesday and Friday cheese, eggs, milk, and butter; and the laity meat; that we may anathematize the Armenian heresy, (see fol. 37)[^283].*

*Likewise do we also on this week of Cheesefare on Wednesday and Friday: keeping the Canon of our Father among the Saints, Nicephorus, Patriarch of Constantinople, the Confessor, who says thus:* "It is appointed for Monks to fast on Wednesday and Friday of Cheesefare Week; and after Vespers to eat cheese and eggs, wherever they may be found." *And this Canon overturns the decree of the Jacobites and the Tetradite heresy.[^284]*

*On Cheesefare Wednesday evening, we sing Small Compline. And we sing the Canon that is in the Menaion in order, of the Saint who is to occur on Saturday.*

*If the Meeting of the Lord was on Cheesefare Tuesday, then we sing the Tuesday Tri-ode on this Cheesefare Wednesday at Compline with the Canon of Cheesefare Saturday.*

*Know: If the Feast of the Three Holy Hierarchs, January 30, falls on one of the days of Cheesefare Week up until Cheesefare Friday, their service is sung with the Triodion, even as that of the Forerunner...*

*Rubric (continued):* ...Likewise also in the preceding weeks: of the Publican and Pharisee, and of the Prodigal Son, and on Meatfare Week.

*Only at Matins: The 17th Kathisma [Neporochny] with its own Troparia, and the Hypakoe of the Tone, and the customary reading. Then: "Praise ye the Name of the Lord..." The 17th Kathisma with the Hierarchs' refrains [Pripivy]. And at each "Glory" at the end we sing: Glory... Both now... "Alleluia," thrice, with the Refrain. Sessional hymns to the Saints and Theotokion, and reading of the Hierarchs. And the rest in order together according to custom.*

*On the Sunday of the Prodigal Son and Meatfare Sunday, we sing: "By the waters of Babylon" (Ps. 136 [LXX]). But Glory... Both now... "Alleluia" is not sung; but we sing the 17th Kathisma with the Hierarchs' refrains. The Resurrection Canon with the Heirmos, on 4; and in the Triodion, on 6; and to the Hierarchs, on 4, (see fol. 55)[^285]. Katavasia: "The sun once shone upon the dry land..."*

*If the Three Holy Hierarchs fall on the Saturday before Meatfare: Then at Matins, after the customary two Stichologies, we sing: "Praise ye the Name of the Lord..." and the 17th Kathisma with the Hierarchs' refrains.*

*It is necessary to know: That if the Feast of the Meeting was on Meatfare Sunday or Cheesefare Monday, its Apodosis is on this Cheesefare Thursday. And it is sung as was indicated on Cheesefare Tuesday, (see fol. 80)[^286].*

*If the Meeting of the Lord falls on Cheesefare Thursday, or on Friday, or on Saturday: its Apodosis is on Cheesefare Sunday.*
