*If the Finding of the Honorable Head [of the Forerunner] falls on this Cheesefare Thursday: see the rubric, (see fol. 74)[^287].*

## Thursday of Cheesefare

*At Matins:*

*The entire service is even as on Cheesefare Monday, (see fol. 75)[^288].*

*At the 1st Stichology: Sessional hymn in the Octoechos.*

*At the 2nd Stichology: Sessional Hymn, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up" (Voznesyisya):*

Even as Moses led Israel out of bondage through the Red Sea (Exod. 14), having drowned Pharaoh in the deep with Thy supreme right hand: so in wonders, O disciples of Mary, Thy disciples opened the sea of grievous godlessness, and guided all unto Thee, O Word without beginning, O only Lover of mankind!

*Glory...*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:* O refuge of those in distress, reconciliation unto God for sinners, all-hymned Lady: save us from every assault and malice of man, and fearful torment, and the deceit of dishonor, who with undoubting faith ever call upon thee, O all-hymned Virgin!

*And we read in the Lenten Collectanea [Sobornik], the Homily of Saint Anastasius, Abbot of Mount Sinai, on the Sixth Psalm.[^289]*

*Canons: We sing the customary [canons] in the Octoechos and in the Menaion, (see fol. 86)[^290]; in whichever Odes we sing the Triodion, the Odes of the Canon in the Octoechos and in the Menaion are omitted, because the Tri-ode has two Heirmoi.*

*The creation of Lord Joseph.*

*Tri-ode, Tone 4. Ode 4. Heirmos:* The Prophet heard of Thy coming, O Christ, and was afraid; he considered Thy works and was amazed: Glory to Thy power, O Lord! (Hab. 3:2 [LXX]).

*Troparion:* The disciples of Christ shone forth in the world as the radiance of the knowledge of God, O Lord, and destroyed the deceit of impiety, dispelling the gloom: by their prayers show mercy unto those that hymn Thee!

*Troparion:* The vestibules of the fast, crowned with the fruitful virtues of ascetic contests, receive those who run with vigilance; with songs and mystical hymns, let us all, the faithful, eagerly run together!

*Troparion:* Having received garments of mortification through the boldness of intemperance, I, wretched one, have clothed myself therein: but do Thou clothe me, O Son of God, in the radiant garment of regeneration! (Gen. 3:21; Gal. 3:27; Titus 3:5).

*Troparion:* My life has been consumed in craftiness and every uncleanness, yet unto Thee, the All-compassionate, do I flee: anticipate me therefore and save me, O Christ God, by Thy grace!

*Theotokion:* Beyond word didst thou conceive, and beyond nature didst thou bring forth, O Parent of God, Him Who rules over creation: Him do thou ceaselessly entreat to deliver from all wrath us that call thee blessed!

*Heirmos:* The Prophet heard of Thy coming, O Christ, and was afraid; he considered Thy works and was amazed: Glory to Thy power, glory, O Christ, to Thy descent! (Hab. 3:2 [LXX]).

*Another Heirmos, Tone 2: Thou hast come from the Virgin:*[^291]

*Troparion:* Having dispersed the mist of intemperance with the radiance of your teachings, O all-praised Apostles, enlighten through abstinence all sinners and righteous men!

*Troparion:* Even as luminous pearls of the Word, O Apostles, ye adorn all the ends of the earth by abstinence, truly making noble now the dignity of our souls!

*Troparion:* Having quenched the furnace of passions with the dew of the Fast, O Apostles, teach all men to have this great haven and dwelling-place of salvation!

*Glory...*

*Troparion:* I have sinned above all men, wretched that I am, and have not repented; like Manasseh have I sinned (2 Chron. 33): fashion in me the ways of repentance before death dissolves me![^292]

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:* We, all the faithful, know thee, O Lady Theotokos, as a haven of salvation and an indestructible wall: for by thy prayers thou dost deliver our souls from tribulations!

*Heirmos:* Thou hast come from the Virgin, neither an ambassador nor an angel, but the Lord Himself incarnate, and hast saved me, the whole man: therefore I cry aloud unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lord! (Isa. 63:9; Hab. 3:2 [LXX]).

*Ode 8. Heirmos:* Thou hast established all things by Thy wisdom, O Master, and again Thou hast founded the earth, as Thou knowest its foundation, setting its course upon the untouchable waters: therefore we all cry aloud, singing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord! (Prov. 3:19; Ps. 103:5, 135:6 [LXX]; Song of the Three Holy Children 35).

*Troparion:* All the earth is full of Thy praise, O Lord (Hab. 3:3 [LXX]): for the divine utterance of Thy divine Apostles traversed it entirely, converting from the depth of ignorance unto understanding those who sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto the ages! (Ps. 18:5 [LXX]).

*Troparion:* The divine grace of repentance hath appeared, bringing salvation unto those who enjoy its sufferings and labors...

...of fasts and afflictions: therefore run, O my soul, unto thy Master, seeking to receive forgiveness for thy many transgressions!

*Troparion:* The stadium of God-given abstinence hath been opened; let us joyfully go forth to meet it, we who are in need of mercy! For the Compassionate One awaits our salvation, to grant forgiveness; He stretches forth His hand unto those who seek Him diligently and serve Him with love!

*Troparion:* Grown old in fasting through worldly thinking, nourish the Lord now, O my soul, with the food of well-doing and virtue, offering sweet-smelling sacrifices unto Him, and crying unceasingly: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Both now... Theotokion:* O Lady reverenced by all, receiving the entreaties of thy servants, deliver me from darkness and unquenchable fire, cleansing the multitude of my evils through thy mediation and thy divine prayer!

*Another Heirmos: He was afraid of the holy children:*[^293]

*Troparion:* Christ, having given you as light unto the ends of the whole inhabited earth, O Apostles, said: "Go forth and teach among the nations (Matt. 28:19), that seeing Me in the flesh, living in abstinence, trampling down all the power of the enemy, and showing unto men the upright way..."

*Troparion:* Thou hast shown forth, O Christ, the coming abstinence for the purification of men and the separation from passions: for by this Thy disciples were well-pleasing unto Thee, and appeared as bright luminaries upon the earth, preaching the Lord in three Persons, yet in one essence, unto those who hymn Him!

*Troparion:* Ye were sent as preachers unto the Gentiles...

...from the Master Christ, O disciples, to enlighten their thoughts with divine teachings: to restrain themselves from passions and meats, to desire abstinence, and to know Him as Lord, Creator, Maker of all, and Benefactor!

*Glory...*

*Troparion:* As once Cain the murderer, I have not offered blameless sacrifices of my soul unto Thee, O Christ, being held fast by an unclean mind: do not abhor me who draw near unto Thee through fasting, O all-good Savior, but look upon the gifts which with love I bring unto Thee, my God, in the time of the Fast![^294]

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:* Blessed was the womb of the Mother of God, for it received the Word, Who in the density of flesh manifested Himself to men in form according to hypostasis;[^295] and she appeared as the city of God, in which the Most High, both Lord and God, was well-pleased to dwell: therefore we cry aloud: Rejoice, thou highly favored and pure Theotokos! (Luke 1:28, 48; Ps. 45:5, 86:3 [LXX]).

*We praise, we bless, and we worship the Lord:*

*Heirmos:* The unceasing fire, fed upon measureless fuel, retreated, fearful before the holy children's undefiled body that shared the soul's glory; and when the perpetual flame had withered, an everlasting song was sung: Hymn the Lord, all ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages! (Dan. 3:57).

*Ode 9. Heirmos: For He that is mighty hath magnified me:* (Luke 1:49).

*Troparion:* As a flame void of deceit were ye sent into the world, delivering it from the depth of sins: with joy we hymn Him Who strengthened you as His Apostles!

*Troparion:* When the world had become foolish of old, Christ sent you as salt into the world, O Apostles, to bring it to its senses unto the knowledge of Him: you do we faithfully praise! (Matt. 5:13).

*Troparion:* Since the time for repentance and prayer hath arrived, yet I, slothful one, grow despondent, with compunction I cry unto Thee: Save me freely, O God, as Thou art merciful!

*Troparion:* Because a multitude of grievous sins holds me fast, in tears I fall down before Thee, O Christ, asking forgiveness, that Thou mayest grant me a favorable season in the time of the Fast!

*Troparion:* As one bound with the indissoluble bonds of my passions, I groan and cry aloud unto Thee, O Savior: Deliver me, that with exceeding joy I may hymn Thy tender compassion!

*Both now... Theotokion:* Since God hath shown thee forth, O pure Virgin, as a helper unto the race of men, cease not to pray diligently unto Him for us, the faithful!

*Heirmos:* For He that is mighty hath magnified me, and holy is His Name, and His mercy is on them that fear Him from generation to generation! (Luke 1:49-50).

*Another Heirmos: Him Whom nothing can contain:*[^296]

*Troparion:* The choir of the Apostles showed forth the Fast, shining upon the entire inhabited earth, and with divine splendor enlightening the world!

*Troparion:* The choir of Thy disciples, O Jesus, Lover of mankind, taught the nations of the Gentiles to lay up in store the wealth of abstinence!

*Troparion:* The twelve-stringed utterance, the company of Thy disciples, bestows upon men the wealth of abstinence and the fountain of salvation!

*Glory...*

*Troparion:* With the voice of the Prodigal I cry aloud unto Thee, O Lord: Father, I have sinned! Save me, O Compassionate One, and separate me not from Thy glory! (Luke 15:18, 21).

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:* Having borne in thy womb God as man, and having poured forth Life unto the world, we hymn thee, O all-pure Virgin!

*Heirmos:* Having contained in thy womb the uncontainable God, and having brought forth joy unto the world, we hymn thee, O all-pure Virgin!

*Exapostilarion, Tone... and: Glory to God in the highest:*[^297]

*At the Aposticha: Stichera, Tone 3. Idiomela:*

The comeliness of abstinence hath shone forth, driving away the deep gloom; the honor of the fast hath arrived, bringing healing unto the passions of the soul. This of old fortified Daniel...

...and protected the Children in Babylon: the one indeed muzzled the mouths of the lions, and the others quenched the flame of the furnace (Dan. 6:22; Dan. 3:50). Through these do Thou also save us from the same, O Christ God, as Thou art the Lover of mankind!

*Verse:* We were filled in the morning with Thy mercy... (Ps. 89:14 [LXX]).

*And we chant the same Sticheron again.*

*Verse:* And let the brightness of the Lord our God be upon us... (Ps. 89:17 [LXX]).

*Martyrikon:* As luminaries in the world do ye shine even after death, O holy martyrs, having fought the good fight (Phil. 2:15; 2 Tim. 4:7): having boldness, entreat Christ that our souls may be shown mercy!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:* O holy Theotokos among women, unwedded Mother, pray unto Him Whom thou didst bear, our King and God, that He may save us, as He is the Lover of mankind!

*Then: It is good to give thanks unto the Lord:* (Ps. 91:2 [LXX]).

*We also join the First Hour according to custom, and the Dismissal.*

*On that same Thursday of Cheesefare, at Vespers:*

*From the beginning we commence the prostrations, and all the rest of the service is even as was indicated above for Cheesefare Tuesday, (see fol. 88)[^298].*

*At the Aposticha: Stichera, Tone 2. Idiomela:*

The Lord's Cross, unto those who undeviatingly bow down before it, is the bridle of every pleasure and the law of abstinence! For they who gaze upon Him Who was nailed to the tree without stumbling crucify the flesh with its passions and desires (Gal. 5:24): among whom let us also strive to be! By a pure fast let us unite ourselves unto Him Who in His love for mankind united Himself unto us by His Passion, and Who by His impassibility changed our nature, as He hath great mercy!

*Verse:* Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes... (Ps. 122:1 [LXX]). *And we chant the same Sticheron again.*

*Verse 2:* Have mercy upon us, O Lord, have mercy upon us... (Ps. 122:3 [LXX]).

*Martyrikon:* Having had no desire for earthly delights, the passion-bearers...

...were accounted worthy of heavenly good things, and became fellow-citizens with the angels: O Lord, by their prayers have mercy on us and save us!

*Glory... Both now... Stavrotheotokion, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Egda ot dreva):*

When thou didst behold hanging upon the Tree the ripe Cluster, Whom untilled thou didst bear in thy womb, O pure one, lamenting and weeping thou didst cry aloud: "O Child, Benefactor, pour forth wine whereby all drunkenness of passions may be quenched, for my sake who bare Thee, with divine consolations, as Thou art tenderhearted!"

*Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart... (Luke 2:29). Theotokos Virgin, rejoice... Prostrations. And the rest, (see fol. 86)[^299]. And the Dismissal.*

*If the Meeting of the Lord falls on this Cheesefare Friday, or the Finding of the Honorable Head of the Forerunner: Then see the appointed rubric for Cheesefare Wednesday, (see fol. 78)[^300].*

*After this: Great Compline, and the prostrations: When they strike [the klepalo], and the rest.*

*If the Meeting had fallen on Cheesefare Wednesday, then we sing the Canon of the Meeting and the Tri-odes on this Cheesefare Thursday at Compline.*

## Friday of Cheesefare

*At Matins:*

*After the Six Psalms: We sing "Alleluia" in the Tone, and the Trinity Hymns [Triadika] of the Tone.*

*At the 1st Stichology: Sessional hymn in the Octoechos.*

*At the 2nd Stichology: Sessional Hymn from the Triodion, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having placed all their hope" (Vse upovanie):*

Exalt the horn of the Church, O precious Cross! Cast down heretical arrogance by thy power, and make glad the Orthodox assemblies! Grant that we all may anticipate thy Forefeast, and bow down before thee, the footstool of Christ: for in thee do we boast, O blessed Tree! (Ps. 74:11, 98:5 [LXX]; Gal. 6:14).

*Glory... Both now... Stavrotheotokion:*

The only undefiled Ewe-lamb, the Theotokos and Virgin, beholding Him Who was incarnate of her pure blood voluntarily hanging upon the Cross, cried aloud...

...O Maiden, with bitter tears: "O my gentle Son, I hymn Thy fearful and whole dispensation!"

*Then we read the Homily of Basil the Great on Fasting, whose beginning is: "Blow the trumpet at the new moon" (Ps. 80:4 [LXX]), in 3 parts; and on the monastic life by the same author: "It behooves the monk first of all to have an unacquisitive life."[^301] Then Psalm 50.*

*Canons: In the Octoechos, and in the Menaion, and in the Triodion; in whichever Odes we sing the Tri-ode, we omit these Odes of the Octoechos and in the Menaion: because the Tri-odes have two Heirmoi.[^302]*

*The Canon in the Triodion. The creation of Lord Joseph.*

*Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:* Having passed through the water as upon dry land, and having escaped the evil of Egypt, the Israelite cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God! (Exod. 14:22, 29).

*Troparion:* The divine season of abstinence hath shone forth upon all, the illumination of repentance taking away the darkness of transgressions: let us receive this with a fervent heart!

*Troparion:* Behold, the beauty of repentance enlargs souls by the introduction of fasting; having entered into this with sobriety, let us, the faithful, receive the absolution of our sins!

*Glory...*

*Troparion:* Principalities, Powers, Cherubim, and all ye Heavenly Hosts, pray that we may accomplish the season of the Fast in repentance and all purity! (Col. 1:16).

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:* O holy Virgin, sole protection of the faithful, grant thy favorable prayer unto all during the time of the Fast, unto us who know how to honor thee as the Mother of God!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:* O Builder of the vault of heaven and Founder of the Church, do Thou establish me in Thy love, O Summit of desires, Firmness of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:* Let us flee all-destructive passions of the soul; by the practice of the fast and compunction, let us show forth fruits, we who previously embittered the merciful God by our sins! (Matt. 3:8; Luke 3:8).

*Troparion:* Let us all eagerly strive now to kindle our lamps with the oil of good deeds: let us prepare ourselves to enter with the wise virgins rejoicing into the radiant bridal chamber! (Matt. 25:1-13).

*Glory...*

*Troparion:* God-proclaiming Prophets, God-beholding Martyrs, and divine Disciples of the Savior, pray unto Him, we all entreat you, that we may begin well and finish in a manner well-pleasing unto God the stadium of the Fast!

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:* As the cause of all good things, O Lady, we all diligently entreat thee: that through thy mediation we may enter into the ascetical contest of the fast, and that its saving end may be granted unto us!

*Heirmos:* O Builder of the vault of heaven and Founder of the Church, do Thou establish me in Thy love, O Summit of desires, Firmness of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind!

*Sessional hymn in the Menaion. Glory... Both now... Stavrotheotokion.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:* I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation, I considered Thy works, and glorified Thy Divinity! (Hab. 3:2 [LXX]).

*Troparion:* As the dawn dawning upon us, the grace of fasting is now set forth: the season of repentance, dissolving for us the darkness of debts!

*Troparion:* Let us feed the poor, receiving mercy through mercy (Matt. 5:7); and with the divine water of fasting, let us wash away the defilements of the soul!

*Glory...*

*Troparion:* Heavenly Angels, entreat the Giver of God that He may receive our compunctionate repentance now through His boundless tender compassions!

*Both now...*

*Theotokion:* Pure mercy-seat for sinners, O holy Lady, tear asunder the handwriting of my transgressions by thy mediation! (Col. 2:14).

*Heirmos:* I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation, I considered Thy works, and glorified Thy Divinity! (Hab. 3:2 [LXX]).

*Ode 5. Heirmos:* Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy presence, O Light unwaning, and an alien darkness hath covered me, wretched one? But convert me, and direct my ways unto the light of Thy commandments, I pray Thee![^303] (Ps. 87:15; Isa. 26:9 [LXX]).

*Troparion:* Making this present day the beginning of an honorable life, fortify yourselves diligently for the contests of the fast...

...let us prepare ourselves, O faithful, offering the afflictions of the flesh and the good fruitfulness of the soul unto the Master of all!

*Troparion:* Fasting showed Moses forth as a partaker of divine vision of God (Exod. 24:18, 34:28): zealously imitating him, therefore, O my soul, receive the fast, having arranged in thyself divine ascents, that thou mayest behold the splendor of God![^304] (Ps. 83:6 [LXX]).

*Troparion:* By the prayers of the holy Apostles and of the holy-confessing Martyrs, account us worthy, O compassionate Jesus, to pass through the season of fasting in repentance and in every putting away of sin, we beseech Thee!

*Both now... Theotokion:* Desiring to enter the door of the Fast, we entreat thee, the Door of God, to enter in with thy servants, O Lady, and to enlarge in every way our thoughts and hearts for the keeping of saving commandments! (Ezek. 44:2-3).

*Then the Tri-ode of Lord Joseph, Tone 6.*

*Ode 5. Heirmos:* With Thy divine light, O Good One, illumine, I pray Thee, the souls of those who lovingly rise early unto Thee, that they may know Thee, O Word of God, as the true God, calling them back from the darkness of sins! (Isa. 26:9).

*Troparion:* Having nailed Thy hands to the Cross, and being pierced with a spear in Thy ribs, O Lover of mankind, Thou didst tear asunder the handwriting against fallen Adam: therefore with hymns we glorify Thee, O Giver of life! (Col. 2:14; John 19:34).

*Troparion:* The all-wicked serpent deceptively cast upon me the passions of the soul, and cast me out of Paradise (Gen. 3): but Thou, having nailed Thy hands upon the Cross, didst draw me unto the height of incorruption, O Savior!

*Troparion:* The divine season of the fast, purifying the passions of the soul and washing the wounds of the soul, hath come now, it is at hand: let us run together, ye faithful, and receive it with a philosophic mind!

*Troparion:* A good season hath appeared, clothing every person in the divine chastity of the soul: come, let us joyfully receive it, purifying ourselves exceedingly by fasting and prayer!

*Troparion:* Let us offer fasting, tears, prayer, and a humble disposition unto Him Who humbled Himself for our sake (Phil. 2:8): that today through abstinence...

...He may grant forgiveness for the sins committed by us!

*Both now... Theotokion:* By the light-beaming rays of thy brightness, O gracious Lady unto the world, drive away the gloom of my soul and the night of my sin, that with fervor I may hymn and glorify thee!

*Another Heirmos, Tone 8: Why hast Thou cast me off:*

*Troparion:* I venerate Thy Cross, O Lord, Lover of mankind, whereby Thou hast saved me, and I hymn Thy saving and divine sufferings, O Master, by which I have been delivered from my painful passions, O Word, passing over into an uninjured life!

*Troparion:* Putting our trust in the Cross, we turn away from the deceit of the adversaries; established therein, we are acknowledged as faithful unto God, like chosen newborn lambs, and we have drunk milk void of deceit! (1 Pet. 2:2).

*Glory... Trinity Hymn [Triadikon]:* All of us glorify the Father, and the Word, and the Holy Spirit in one essence: setting forth radiant knowledge, worshiping with both [piety and understanding], and distinguishing the Persons, serving without confusion and without change![^305]

*Both now... Theotokion:* Hope of the ends of the earth, joy unto thy servants art thou, O pure Virgin! Preserve those who lovingly revere thine icon, O all-pure one, and by thy prayers deliver all of us from the alien one, as thou art most tenderhearted!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee:*

*Troparion:* Make straight Thy upright way, O Lord, for all Thy servants who lovingly revere the stadium of the fast: establish them, and by Thy holy fastenings bind them unto that which is better, and make them worthy of the Kingdom!

*Heirmos: Why hast Thou cast me off:*

*Ode 6. Heirmos:* Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities; and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee: for unto Thee have I cried, and do Thou give ear unto me, O God of my salvation! (Jonah 2:3; Ps. 87:2 [LXX]).

*Troparion:* The Deliverer hath appointed for us this present season of fasting...

...appointed for us the cleansing of spiritual defilements: with a fervent heart, let us draw near, O faithful, that we may receive forgiveness!

*Troparion:* Having accepted the sighing of the publican, O Christ, and the weeping offered from the soul of the harlot (Luke 18:13, 7:38): do Thou accept our supplications, as the Lover of mankind, granting us cleansing!

*Glory... Troparion:* By the contrition of Thy preachers, and Prophets, Martyrs, and Apostles, and of Thy Venerables and Hierarchs, and of all Thy Righteous ones, O Christ: send down upon our souls the cleansing of transgressions!

*Both now... Theotokion:* O pure Lady, who alone didst establish the infirmity of human nature by thy divine childbearing: show thyself my helper, as I enter upon the divine struggle of the fast!

*Heirmos:* Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities, and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee: for unto Thee have I cried, and do Thou give ear unto me, O God of my salvation! (Jonah 2:3; Ps. 87:2 [LXX]).

*Then the Martyrion, Sessional Hymn, Tone 6.*[^306]

*Ode 7. Heirmos: "The Hebrew children in the furnace" (Дѣ́ти є҆вре́йскїѧ въ пе́щи):*

*Troparion:* Of old Elijah, borne aloft upon a fiery chariot, was illuminated by fasting (4 Kgs. 2:11 [LXX] / 2 Kings 2:11):[^307] behold, O my soul, imitating him, put to death the carnal intemperances through temperance!

*Troparion:* Behold the season of temperance, showing forth unto thee the light of salvation! Be not negligent, O soul, while God shows longsuffering; but cry out earnestly: O Good One, have compassion on me!

*Glory... Troparion:* Fasting, having protected the Children, preserved them unconsumed by fire (Dan. 3): O Jesus, by their prayers deliver me from eternal fire, in Thine abundant tenderheartedness!

*Both now... Theotokion:* O only Helper of men, be a help unto us in the season of temperance: that, well-pleasing [God] through repentance, we may receive the Heavenly Kingdom!

*Heirmos:* The Hebrew children boldly trampled upon the flame in the furnace, and changed the fire into dew, crying: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages! (Dan. 3:49–50).

*Ode 8. Heirmos:* Sevenfold the Chaldean tormentor furiously kindled the furnace for the godly ones; but seeing them saved by a higher power, he cried out to the Creator and Redeemer: Children, bless; priests, hymn; people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages! (Dan. 3:19–57).

*Troparion:* Having zealously kindled the furnace of repentance, burn therein all bodily pleasures, and that we may not receive the forthcoming fire of the rich man (Luke 16:24), crying out: Remember us in mercy: Priests, bless; people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^308]

*Troparion:* May the incoming season of the fast be for us the cause of the putting away of every sin! Let us not be slothful, therefore, nor run despondently, that in brief and small days we may wash away many defilements through contrition of heart, singing unto our one God!

*Glory... Troparion:* The angelic ranks, the assembly of the Martyrs, the holy choir of the divine Apostles, the congregation of the Venerables, Hierarchs, and Prophets entreat Thee, O Good One and Lover of mankind: grant sincere repentance unto Thy servants in the season of temperance now being introduced!

*Both now... Theotokion:* Pure Mother of God, unto thee we flee: have compassion upon us by thy good prayer, and grant Thy Son and Lord to be well-reconciled unto all of us in the season of fasting now being introduced, unto the salvation of the faithful who hymn thee unto all ages!

*Then the Tri-ode of Lord Joseph, Tone 6. Heirmos: "According to the paternal law" (По зако́нꙋ ѻ҆́тчꙋ):*[^309]

*Troparion:* Not having preserved the Father's inheritance, we were enslaved unto sinful laws (Luke 15:11–14): but having stretched out Thy hands upon the divine Cross, Thou hast granted freedom unto all...

...for the sake thereof! Therefore have mercy in prayer upon those who with faith bring [abstinence] unto Thee in these holy days, supremely exalting Thee unto all ages!

*Troparion:* Bitterly having eaten the enticing food of old, the first-formed man was rejected, and condemned, was driven out from delight (Gen. 3): but nailed to the Tree with nails, Thou didst nail up the evil handwriting of his sin (Col. 2:14). Therefore we hymn Thine abundant tenderheartedness unto all ages!

*Troparion:* Having broken down by Thy Cross the separation and the middle wall of the partition, and renewed rich peace unto the ends [of the earth] (Eph. 2:14):[^310] grant us, living in peace, O Christ, to pass through the coming fasting with steadfastness, unceasingly singing the Lord and supremely exalting Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:* The grace of the fast today offers solar rays unto all, thoroughly purifying from sin the darkness of those held by diverse passions: having passed over in joy, let us philosophically receive the gift, crying: Sing the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:* Having rejected the law of the fast, we were submerged in the depth of sin, and we stood in need of the fast now being introduced: unto us who pass through it, O Compassionate One, send down grace from heaven and rich peace, crying: Works, hymn the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Both now... Theotokion:* Restoration for the falling, intercessor for sinners, refreshment for strangers, consolation for the sorrowful, O all-pure Virgin: dissolve the grief of my soul, O pure one, and pray that consolation from above from God be granted unto me, as I earnestly sing: Works, praise the Lord!

*Another Heirmos, Tone 8: "Angels and the heavens" (А҆́гг҃лы и҆ нб҃са̀):*

*Troparion:* Seeing Thee nailed in the flesh upon the Cross, O Compassionate One, the nature of creation was altered: the light of day turned into darkness, the earth was shaken, and all things bowed down! (Matt. 27:45, 51).

*Troparion:* Having exalted human nature unto the height of the Cross, and from Thy divine rib gushed forth a wealth of salvation (John 19:34) unto those who with faith, O Savior, worship Thy most pure passion!

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord.*

*Trinity Hymn [Triadikon]:* God indeed is one Trinity: the Father having neither departed into the Son, nor the Son having changed into the procession [of the Spirit], but proper [to each Person] and both [unconfused], Light, God, I glorify the Three unto all ages![^311]

*Both now... Theotokion:* How hast thou given birth unto the Son, Who agelessly shone forth from the Father, and is hymned with the Holy Spirit? Even as He Himself knows, Who willed to be born of thee, unto the ages!

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*

*Troparion:* Receiving our repentance, since Thou art good by nature, deliver us from the snares of the enemy, O Lord, that with faith and love we may now hymn Thy holy dominion!

*We praise, we bless, and we worship the Lord.*

*Heirmos:* Him Who sits upon the Throne of glory, and as God is unceasingly glorified: Angels and the heavens, bless, sing, and supremely exalt unto all ages! (Dan. 3:56–59).

*And the Magnificat ("More honorable than the Cherubim") is chanted.*

*Ode 9. Heirmos:* Heaven was amazed thereat, and the ends of the earth were struck with wonder, that God appeared unto men in the flesh, and thy womb became more spacious than the heavens: therefore, O Mother of God, the hierarchies of Angels and of men magnify thee!

*Troparion:* Behold, the light-bearing time hath arrived, the holy day hath broken forth today: shake off the dark darkness of thy passions, O my soul, and receive the beams that guide thee unto the light; drinking contrition as wine, rejoice, and hate the drunkenness of pleasures!

*Troparion:* As truly most blessed is the time of fasting, O Christ, which Thou hast given unto all the faithful for the overcoming of sins, and saving cleansing, and the receptacle of gifts: wherein we pray Thee, O Savior, make us partakers of Thy blessed good things!

*Troparion:* Pouring out customarily upon us Thy rich mercies, O Compassionate One, grant us tears that cleanse defilements and thoughts, pure faith and love toward Thee, abundant repentance, and perfect appropriation unto Thee, the only Most-Merciful One!

*Glory... Troparion:* Divine multitude of all the Angels, and praiseworthy choirs of the Saints, pray now diligently unto the Good One: that He may strengthen us to run without stumbling this divine stadium of present fasting, and make us victorious!

*Both now... Theotokion:* O good-loving Virgin, who didst give birth unto the good Savior, bless all of us who are afflicted by immeasurable passions and thoughts, O Lady, and weighed down with heavy burdens of sins: that worthily, as the Mother of God, we may magnify thee!

*Then the Tri-ode of Lord Joseph, Tone 6. Heirmos: "Every tongue is at a loss" (Недоꙋмѣ́етъ всѧ́къ ꙗ҆зы́къ):*[^312]

*Troparion:* Every nature of both men and Angels is at a loss to thank Thy tenderheartedness, O Benefactor: that for our sake, willing in the flesh, Thou didst become poor (2 Cor. 8:9), and didst suffer on the Tree for us, having become a curse, that Thou mightest take away the primal curse of mankind! (Gal. 3:13).

*Troparion:* The radiant day of temperance hath arrived; with radiant countenance come, O my soul, let us meet the Master, asking that grace be sent down upon us from above, and that we may find correction of many stumbling-blocks: that...

...we may not experience the most terrible Gehenna there!

*Troparion:* Willingly stretching out Thy hands upon the Tree, Thou didst gather together all things that had been sundered, O Giver of life; and opening Thy ribs with a spear, O Longsuffering One, Thou didst correct for us the defeat that had budded forth from the rib [of Adam]:[^313] therefore thanksgivingly we hymn Thy tenderheartedness!

*Troparion:* For those held fast in the darkness of sins, the sacred entrances of repentance have now arrived, illuminating the souls of all! Therefore, O my soul, turn away from the darkening satiety of passions, fasting that thou mayest delight in eternal sweetness there!

*Glory... Troparion:* The light-bearing fast now coming upon us sets forth a table of struggles, prepared for this purpose: from this, soberly, all of us faithful, let us receive the honorable cup of purifying tears, that we may not experience incurable tears there!

*Both now... Theotokion:* Protector of all who hope in thee, vigilant helper in afflictions, O Mother of God our patroness: deliver us from everlasting Gehenna and the bitter torments that lie upon us, that worthily we may hymn thy majesties!

*Heirmos:* Every tongue is at a loss to praise thee worthily, and an otherworldly mind is bewildered to hymn thee, O Mother of God: yet, being good, accept our faith, for thou knowest our divine love; for thou art the protectress of Christians: thee do we magnify!

*Then the Trinity Exapostilarion [Photogogikon], Tone 3: Praise the Lord from the heavens (Ps. 148:1).*

*To Thee glory is due, O Lord our God, and to Thee glory... Glory to God in the highest.*

*At the Praises [Ainoi]: Idiomelon Sticheron, Tone 6:* Before the saving Cross, when sin reigned and impiety had dominion (Rom. 5:21), bodily food was deemed blessed by men, and few were negligent of carnal desires: but since the mystery of the Cross was wrought, demonic...

...tyranny hath been extinguished through the knowledge of God, and heavenly virtue abides upon earth! Therefore fasting is honored, temperance shines forth, and prayer is set aright; and a witness is this present time, given unto us by Christ God Who was crucified, unto the salvation of our souls!

*Verse:* We were filled in the morning with Thy mercy, O Lord, and we rejoiced and were glad (Ps. 89:14 [LXX]). *And we repeat the same Idiomelon.*

*Verse 2:* And let the brightness of the Lord our God be upon us (Ps. 89:17 [LXX]).

*Martyrion:* O Lord, in the memory of Thy Saints all creation keeps festival: the heavens rejoice with the Angels, and the earth is glad with men; by their prayers have mercy on us!

*Glory... Both now... Stavrotheotokion:* When the All-immaculate Lady beheld Thee suspended upon the Cross, weeping maternally she cried aloud: O my Son and my God, my sweetest Child, how dost Thou endure this most reproachful passion?

*Then: "It is good to give praise..." (Ps. 91:2 [LXX]). Trisagion. Troparion: "Standing in the temple of thy glory..." Lord, have mercy (40 times). Heavenly King... etc. And we make three great prostrations, saying secretly the Prayer of Saint Ephrem the Syrian: "O Lord and Master of my life..." etc., with the intermediate [twelve reverences]. We also join the First Hour with the customary prostrations. We read the Catechesis of our Venerable Father Theodore the Studite. Then the Dismissal.*

*ON THE SAME CHEESEFARE FRIDAY. We chant the Hours with prostrations, even as was indicated for Wednesday: the Third and Sixth Hours.*[^314]

*At the Sixth Hour, Troparion, Tone 8:* Grant us help from affliction, O Lord, and save us, Thou Who wast born of the Virgin, O Lover of mankind! (Ps. 59:13 [LXX]).

*The Paraecclesiarch says:* Glory... *And the Singers chant the ending of the Troparion:* Save us, Thou Who wast born of the Virgin, O Lover of mankind! *The Reader:* Both now... *And the Singers chant the whole Troparion:* Grant us help... *Then the Prokeimenon, Tone 4:* Let Thy mercy, O Lord, be upon us, even as we have hoped in Thee (Ps. 32:22 [LXX]). *Verse:* Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous: praise is seemly for the upright (Ps. 32:1 [LXX]).

*Reading from the Prophecy of Zechariah (Chapter 8:7–17):*[^315]

Thus saith the Lord Almighty: Behold, I will save My people from the east country, and from the west country; and I will bring them in, and will dwell in the midst of Jerusalem; and they shall be My people, and I will be their God, in truth and in righteousness (Zech. 8:7–8).

Thus saith the Lord Almighty: Let your hands be strong, ye that hear in these days these words from the mouth of the Prophets, from the day that the house of the Lord Almighty was founded, and from the time that the temple was built (Zech. 8:9).

For before those days, the wages of men profited not, and there were no wages for cattle, and to him that went out and to him that came in there was no peace from tribulation: and I sent all men, every one against his neighbor (Zech. 8:10).

And now, not according to the former days will I do in these days unto the remnant of this people, saith the Lord Almighty (Zech. 8:11).

For I will show peace: the vine shall give her fruit, and the earth shall give her increase, and the heavens shall give their dew; and I will cause the remnant of My people to inherit all these things by lot (Zech. 8:12).

And it shall come to pass, that as ye were a curse among the nations, O house of Judah, and house of Jerusalem; so will I save you, and ye shall be a blessing: be of good courage, and let your hands be strong! (Zech. 8:13).

For thus saith the Lord Almighty: As I thought to afflict you, when your fathers provoked Me to wrath, saith the Lord Almighty, and I repented not (Zech. 8:14):

So have I purposed and thought in these days to do good unto Jerusalem, and unto the house of Judah: be of good courage! (Zech. 8:15).

These are the words that ye shall do: Speak ye the truth every man...

...every man to his neighbor; judge truth and the judgment of peace and justice in your gates: and let none of you imagine evil in your hearts against his neighbor; and love not a false oath: for all these things I hate, saith the Lord Almighty! (Zech. 8:16–17).

*Prokeimenon, Tone 3:* Sing praises to our God, sing praises: sing praises unto our King! (Ps. 46:6 [LXX]). *Verse:* All ye nations, clap your hands (Ps. 46:1 [LXX]).

*And the rest is performed with prostrations according to custom. Together we also chant the Ninth Hour with all its order, and the Beatitudes rapidly without chanting. And all the rest is even as was indicated for Cheesefare Wednesday.*[^316]

*Concerning the Saints of the day: (see fol. 30 and 104);[^317] except on the Forefeast and Feast of the Meeting of the Lord, or the Saint whose temple it is, (see fol. 30 and 10).*[^318]

*[Narrative Woodcut Illustration: Synaxis of all our Venerable and God-bearing Fathers who shone forth in ascetic fasting and spiritual struggle. Across an architectural and monastic landscape are depicted the great desert fathers and founders of monasticism: Saint Paul of Thebes, Saint Anthony the Great, Saint Agathon, Saint Pachomius the Great, Saint Nicetas of the Caves, Saint Anthony of the Caves, and Saint Theodosius of the Caves holding the Kyiv-Pechersk Lavra monastery.]*[^319]

## Cheesefare Saturday

*We perform the commemoration of all our Venerable and God-bearing Fathers who shone forth in fasting and ascetic struggle.*[^320]

*ON FRIDAY EVENING. We chant "Blessed is the man..." (Ps. 1): the first Kathisma.*[^321]

*At "Lord, I have cried": Stichera on 6. Tone 8:*

*Sticheron 1, Tone 8, Idiomelon:* Come, all ye faithful, let us hymn the choirs of the Venerable Fathers: Anthony the foremost, the radiant Euthymius, and each one, and all together; and their...

...citizenship spiritually traversing like another paradise of delight, beautifully let us cry out: These are the trees which our God planted! These, blossoming with the fruits of incorruptible life, offered them unto Christ, nourishing our souls; unto whom let us cry aloud: O God-bearing blessed ones, pray that our souls may be saved! (Twice).

*Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*

*Sticheron 2, Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):* Rejoice, faithful Egypt! Rejoice, venerable Libya! Rejoice, chosen Thebaid![^322] Rejoice, every place, city, and land that hast reared citizens of the Heavenly Kingdom, and brought them up in temperance and toils, and shown them forth unto God as men of perfected desires! These were revealed as beacons unto our souls: these, by the beam of wonders and the signs of deeds, spiritually shone forth unto all ends [of the earth]; unto whom let us cry aloud: O all-blessed Fathers, pray that we may be saved! (Twice).

*Sticheron 3, Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):* Who among the earthborn shall declare your marvelous lives, O universal Fathers? What tongue shall speak of your sacred daily struggles and your sweats, the streams of your virtues, the mortifications of the flesh, the battles against passions, in vigils, in prayers, and in tears? Living in the world, ye were truly revealed as Angels: ye utterly cast down demonic powers, having wrought marvelous and miraculous signs! Therefore pray for us, O most blessed ones, that we may obtain unending joy! (Twice).

*Glory... Tone 6, Idiomelon:* Having preserved that which is according to the image unharmed, appointing the mind as master over the ruinous passions through asceticism, ye advanced mightily unto that which is according to the likeness:[^323] courageously...

...for having courageously compelled nature, ye strove to subjugate the worse unto the better, and to enslave the flesh unto the spirit! Therefore ye were revealed as the summit of monastics, citizens of the desert, anointers of those who run well, the most precise rule of virtues! And now in the heavens, the mirrors being dissolved,[^324] O all-patient ones, ye gaze purely upon the Holy Trinity, praying directly for those who honor you with faith and love!

*Both now... Theotokion of the day in the Tone of the Octoechos: for Saturday, quiet.*[^325]

*Prokeimenon, Tone 7:* Thou art my protector, O God, and Thy mercy shall go before me! (Ps. 58:9, 10 [LXX 58:9, 10 / MT 59:9, 10]). *Verse:* Deliver me from mine enemies, O God, and from them that rise up against me redeem me! (Ps. 58:1 [LXX 58:1 / MT 59:1]).

*Reading from the Prophecy of Zechariah (Zech. 8:19–23):*

Thus saith the Lord Almighty: The fast of the fourth month, and the fast of the fifth, and the fast of the seventh, and the fast of the tenth, shall be unto the house of Judah for joy, and for gladness, and for good feasts; and ye shall rejoice, and love truth and peace![^326] Thus saith the Lord Almighty: Yet shall there come many peoples, and the inhabitants of many cities! And the inhabitants of five cities shall come together into one city, saying: Let us go diligently to pray before the face of the Lord, and to seek the face of the Lord Almighty; I will go also! And many peoples and many nations shall come and seek the face of the Lord Almighty in Jerusalem, to entreat the face of the Lord Almighty! Thus saith the Lord Almighty: In those days, when ten men from all languages of the nations shall take hold, and shall take hold of the hem of the garment of a man that is a Jew...

...that is a Jew, saying: We will go with thee, for we have heard that God is with you! (Zech. 8:23).

*Prokeimenon, Tone 6:* Let Israel hope in the Lord, from henceforth and unto the age! (Ps. 130:3 [LXX 130:3 / MT 131:3]). *Verse:* Lord, my heart is not exalted, nor are mine eyes become haughty! (Ps. 130:1 [LXX 130:1 / MT 131:1]).

*At the Aposticha: Sticheron Idiomelon, Tone 2:* Let us cleanse ourselves, brethren, from all defilement of flesh and spirit: let us kindle the lamps of our souls through love of the poor, not devouring one another through slander! (2 Cor. 7:1; Gal. 5:15). For the time is at hand when the Bridegroom shall come to reward all according to their deeds! (Matt. 25:1–13; Rev. 22:12). Let us enter with Christ together with the wise virgins, crying aloud unto Him with that voice of the thief: Remember us, O Lord, when Thou comest into Thy Kingdom! (Luke 23:42).

*Repeat the same Sticheron. Verse:* Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes, unto Thee that dwellest in heaven! (Ps. 122:1 [LXX 122:1 / MT 123:1]).

*Second Verse:* Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us: for we are exceedingly filled with contempt! (Ps. 122:3 [LXX 122:3 / MT 123:3]).

*For the Martyrs:* When the holy Martyrs pray for us and hymn Christ, every delusion ceases, and the human race is saved through faith!

*Glory... Tone 8, Idiomelon:* Multitudes of monastics, we honor you today as our instructors, O venerable fathers: for through you have we learned truly to tread the upright path! Blessed are ye who labored for Christ and laid low the power of the enemy! Companions of the Angels, fellow-dwellers with the righteous and the saints: with them pray ye unto the Lord, that our souls may find mercy!

*Both now... Theotokion, Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ пресла́вное чꙋ́до):* Rejoice, treasury of purity! Rejoice, pure dwelling of the immaterial Light! Rejoice, headpiece of our salvation! Rejoice, proclamation of the Apostles! Rejoice, boast of the Martyrs! Rejoice...

...fulfillment of the Prophets, O all-immaculate one! Rejoice, adornment of ascetics and monastics, and salvation of the faithful!

*Then, "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart..." (Luke 2:29–32). Troparion, Tone 4:* O God of our Fathers, Who dealest ever with us according to Thy gentleness, remove not Thy mercy from us; but by their prayers direct our life in peace!

*Glory... the same. Both now... Dismissal Theotokion, Tone 4:* The mystery hidden from all ages and unknown to Angels, through thee, O Theotokos, was revealed unto those upon the earth: God being incarnate in unconfused union, and having willingly accepted the Cross for our sake, whereby having resurrected the first-formed, He saved our souls from death!

*[The Ladder.]* O Holy Mother of the ineffable Light, with angelic hymns we piously magnify thee![^327] *[The same also at "God is the Lord": and at the end: (see fol. 104)].*[^328]

*After this, Litany: "Have mercy on us, O God..." 16 great prostrations.*[^329] *3 times: "Blessed be the name of the Lord..." (Ps. 112:2). Psalm 33: "I will bless the Lord at all times..." "It is truly meet..." And Dismissal.*

*There is no customary communal meal, even as was directed for Cheesefare Wednesday (see fol. 104).*[^330]

*At the refectory, we read the Discourse of Saint Dorotheus on the Holy Fast.*[^331]

*At Small Compline, we sing the Canon for the Departed in the Tone of the Octoechos, which is in turn [in the Tomb-chapel].*[^332]

*Concerning the Feast of the Meeting of the Lord, Chapter 46:*[^333]
*If it occur that on this Cheesefare Saturday falls the Feast of the Meeting of the Lord:*
*On Friday evening, at Small Vespers: At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Feast on 4. Glory... Both now... of the Feast. Prokeimenon of the day, and the Old Testament Reading (Paroemia) of the day, and after it the Prokeimenon: "Let Israel hope in the Lord..." (Ps. 130:3). At the Aposticha: Idiomelon of the day, and for the Martyrs. Glory... Both now... of the Feast. Troparion of the Feast. And Dismissal.*

*At Great Vespers: "Blessed is the man..." (Ps. 1): the first Kathisma. At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera on 10: of the Feast, 6; and of the Fathers, 4. Glory... of the Fathers. Both now... of the Feast. Little Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day. Readings of the Feast: 3. At the Litiya: all of the Feast. At the Aposticha: of the Feast. Glory... of the Fathers. Both now... of the Feast. At the Blessing of the Loaves, Troparion of the Feast (Twice), and of the Fathers (Once). And Readings concerning the Feast.*

*If, however, an All-Night Vigil is not celebrated: then at Great Vespers all is performed according to that which was written above. And the Reading of the day and of the Feast. At the Aposticha: of the Feast; then Verse of the Fathers: "Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His saints" (Ps. 115:6 [LXX 115:6 / MT 116:15]), and we sing the Idiomelon, Tone 8: "Multitudes of monastics..."; then Glory... Idiomelon in the Triodion; Both now... of the Feast.*

*At Matins: At "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Feast (Twice); Glory... of the Fathers; Both now... of the Feast. After the Kathismata: Sessional hymns of the Feast, and Reading of the Feast. Polyeleos, and Megalynarion of the Feast. Sessional hymns of the Feast, all together. Glory... Both now... Theotokion. And Reading of the Feast. Hymns of Ascent (Anavathmoi), the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon of the Feast: "Let every breath..." Gospel of the Feast. After Psalm 50: Sticheron of the Feast. Canon of the Feast with its Heirmoi on 8, and of the Fathers on 6...*

...and of the Fathers on 6. Katavasia of the Feast. After Ode 3: Kontakion and Ikos of the Fathers, and Sessional hymns of the Fathers (twice); Glory... Both now... Sessional hymn of the Feast. After Ode 6: Kontakion and Ikos of the Feast. At Ode 9: we sing "More honorable than the Cherubim..." Exapostilarion of the Feast; Glory... of the Fathers; Both now... of the Feast. At the Praises: Stichera of the Feast, 3; and of the Fathers, 3. Glory... of the Fathers; Both now... of the Feast. Great Doxology. Troparion of the Fathers; Glory... Both now... of the Feast. Litany; and the Catechesis of Saint Theodore the Studite; and Dismissal; and the customary exit into the narthex; and the First Hour. At the First Hour: Troparion of the Feast; Glory... of the Fathers; Both now... Theotokion of the Hour. After the "Our Father": Kontakion of the Feast.

*AT THE LITURGY. The Beatitudes of the Feast: Ode 3, on 4; and of the Fathers: Ode 6, on 4. Entrance Hymn of the Feast: "The Lord hath made known His salvation before the nations" (Ps. 97:2 [LXX]). Troparion of the Feast and of the Fathers. Glory... Kontakion of the Fathers. Both now... Kontakion of the Feast. Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, Gospel, and Communion Hymn: first of the Feast, and then of the Fathers.*

*If the Meeting of the Lord occur on Cheesefare Saturday, its Apodosis is transferred to Cheesefare Sunday.*

*Concerning the Apodosis of the Meeting of the Lord, Chapter 47:*[^334]
*If the Feast of the Meeting of the Lord fell on Cheesefare Tuesday or Wednesday, its Apodosis takes place on this Cheesefare Saturday, and it is sung thus:*

*On Friday of Cheesefare at Vespers: At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Feast, 3; and of the Fathers, 3. Glory... of the Fathers. Both now... of the Feast. Prokeimenon of the day, and the Old Testament Reading (Paroemia), and after it the Prokeimenon: "Let Israel hope in the Lord..." (Ps. 130:3). At the Aposticha: in the Triodion, the Idiomelon of the day (twice), and for the Martyrs. Glory... of the Fathers. Both now... of the Feast. Troparion of the Fathers; Glory... Both now... of the Feast.*

*AT MATINS. At "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Feast (twice); Glory... of the Fathers; Both now... of the Feast. After the Kathismata: Sessional hymns of the Feast. Canon of the Feast with the Heirmos on 8, and of the Fathers on 6. Katavasia of the Feast. After Ode 3: Kontakion and Ikos of the Fathers, and their Sessional hymns; Glory... Both now... of the Feast. After Ode 6: Kontakion and Ikos of the Feast. At Ode 9: we sing "More honorable than the Cherubim..." Exapostilarion of the Feast; Glory... of the Fathers; Both now... of the Feast. At the Praises: Stichera of the Feast, 3; and of the Fathers, 3. Glory... of the Fathers; Both now... of the Feast. Great Doxology. Troparion of the Fathers; Glory... Both now... of the Feast. And the customary exit into the narthex; and the First Hour; and final Dismissal.*

*AT THE LITURGY. The Beatitudes of the Feast: Ode 4, on 4; and of the Fathers: Ode 6, on 4. After the Entrance: Troparion of the Feast, and of the Fathers. Glory... Kontakion of the Fathers. Both now... Kontakion of the Feast. Prokeimenon of the Feast, and of the Fathers. Epistle of the day, and of the Fathers. Alleluia of the Feast, and of the Fathers. Gospel of the day, and of the Fathers. Communion Hymn of the Feast, and of the Fathers.*

*Concerning the Finding of the Head of the Forerunner, Chapter 48:*[^335]
*If it occur that on this Cheesefare Saturday falls the Finding of the Precious Head of Saint John the Forerunner (February 24):*

*On Friday evening: Regular Kathisma [According to others, we sing "Blessed is the man..." (Ps. 1): the first Antiphon]. At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Forerunner, Prosomoia, 3; and of the Fathers, 3. Glory... of the Forerunner; Both now... Theotokion: the 1st in the Tone. Little Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day, and the Old Testament Reading (Paroemia), and after it the Prokeimenon, Tone 6: "Let Israel hope in the Lord..." (Ps. 130:3), with singing. Then the Old Testament Readings (Paroemias) of the Forerunner: 3. "Vouchsafe, O Lord, to keep us this evening..." And the Litany: "Let us complete our evening prayer unto the Lord..." At the Aposticha: Idiomelon of the day (twice), and for the Martyrs. Then Verse: "Be glad in the Lord, and rejoice, ye righteous" (Ps. 31:11 [LXX]), and we sing the Sticheron of the Forerunner, Idiomelon, Tone 2: "That which was formerly hidden in an earthen vessel..." Glory... of the Fathers, Tone 8: "Multitudes of monastics..."; Both now... Theotokion: the 1st in the same Tone. Troparion of the Forerunner; Glory... of the Fathers; Both now... Dismissal Theotokion: "The mystery hidden from all ages..." After this, Litany: "Have mercy on us, O God..."; and 3 great prostrations. Then: "Blessed be the name of the Lord..." (3 times). And Psalm 33: "I will bless the Lord at all times..." And Dismissal.*

*After Vespers, we sing in the narthex the Canon for the Departed in the Tone of the Octoechos, which is in turn; or at Compline.*

*AT MATINS. At "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Forerunner (twice); Glory... of the Fathers; Both now... Dismissal Theotokion: "The mystery hidden from all ages..." After the Kathismata: Sessional hymns of the Fathers, and their Theotokia; and Reading of the Forerunner, and of the Fathers. Polyeleos, and Sessional hymns of the Forerunner, all together; Glory... Both now... Theotokion; and Reading of the Forerunner. Hymns of Ascent (Anavathmoi), the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon: "Let every breath..." and Gospel of the Forerunner. Psalm 50. Sticheron of the Forerunner. But the Biblical Odes in the Psalter we do not say, but we sing "Unto the Lord..." Canon of the Forerunner with the Heirmos on 6, and of the Fathers on 8. Katavasia: Heirmos of the Triodion. Stichology of the Biblical Odes is taken according to the Triodion, but that which is the Canticle in the Psalter is not recited. After Ode 3: Kontakion and Ikos of the Forerunner; then his Sessional hymn; Glory... of the Fathers; Both now... Theotokion; and Reading of the Forerunner. After Ode 6: Kontakion and Ikos of the Fathers; and we read the Prologue. After Ode 9: Exapostilarion of the Forerunner; Glory... of the Fathers; Both now... Theotokion. At the Praises: Stichera of the Forerunner, 3; and of the Fathers, 3. Glory... of the Fathers; Both now... Theotokion. Great Doxology. Troparion of the Forerunner; Glory... of the Fathers; Both now... Theotokion; and exit into the narthex; and the First Hour. At the Hours: Troparion of the Forerunner; Glory... of the Fathers; Both now... Theotokion of the Hours. After the "Our Father": Kontakion of the Forerunner; and at the other Hour, Kontakion of the Fathers: thus are the Troparia said at all the Hours, and the Kontakia alternate.*

*AT THE LITURGY. The Beatitudes of the Forerunner: Ode 3, on 4; and of the Fathers: Ode 6, on 4. After the Entrance: Troparion of the Temple, and of the Forerunner, and of the Fathers. Kontakion of the Temple, and of the Forerunner; Glory... Kontakion of the Fathers; Both now... Kontakion of the Mother of God: "Steadfast Protectress..."*

..."Steadfast Protectress of Christians..." But if the temple be dedicated to the Mother of God: Both now... Kontakion of the Temple. Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, Gospel, and Communion Hymn: first of the Forerunner, and then of the Fathers. The regular Epistle and Gospel of the day are read beforehand at the Hours, or at the Liturgy under the heading.[^336]

## Cheesefare Saturday at Matins

*At "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Fathers (twice); Glory... Both now... Dismissal Theotokion, Tone 4: "The mystery hidden from all ages..."*

*After the 1st Stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "That which was commanded mystically" (Повелѣ́нное та́йнѡ):*[^337] Illumined by the lightning of the fathers, entering as into a beautiful paradise today, let us delight in the torrent of sweetness! (Ps. 35:9 [LXX 35:9 / MT 36:8]). And gazing with awe upon their courage, let us emulate their virtues, crying aloud unto the Savior: By their prayers, O God, make us partakers of Thy Kingdom!

*Glory... Tone 8, Podoben: "That which was commanded mystically" (Повелѣ́нное та́йнѡ):* The leader of monastics, Paul, in concord with wise Anthony and Euthymius let us all hymn, and with the other fathers; and these let us entreat, that they may unceasingly pray unto Christ for us who celebrate their divine and all-radiant memory, glorifying the Savior and Lord in hymns!

*Both now... Theotokion, Tone 8, Podoben: "That which was commanded mystically" (Повелѣ́нное та́йнѡ):* We give thanks unto thee ever, O Virgin, and we magnify thee, O pure one, and we worship thee, hymning thy childbirth, O thou full of grace, crying unceasingly: Save us, O Virgin all-merciful, as thou art good; and snatch us from dread demonic disputation in the hour of trial, that we thy servants may not be put to shame!

*After the 2nd Stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "The pastoral reed-pipe" (Свирѣ́лей па́стырскихъ):*[^338] Anthony together with Euthymius let us all [hymn]...

...with the other God-bearing fathers, let us praise in hymns, celebrating their memory! For these pray unto the Lord for the whole world, that we may be delivered from the ancient curse, being freed from torment!

*Glory... the same. Both now... Theotokion, Tone 4:* By the prayers of Thine Angels, Prophets, Apostles, Martyrs, and holy Hierarchs, and of all the Venerable, O Savior: send down Thy mercy upon all, and grant peace unto the world, and tranquility unto the Churches, Thou Who willingly didst take flesh from the Virgin for our sake!

*Another Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "That which was commanded mystically" (Повелѣ́нное та́йнѡ):* As walking in a meadow blossoming with virtues, surrounding the God-bearing ascetics, we are filled with the sweet-smelling fragrance! For being pierced with the darts of temptations, they enslaved the body unto the spirit in subjection through temperance, having lived an angelic life upon earth; therefore were they also accounted worthy of glory!

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion, Tone 8, Podoben: "That which was commanded mystically" (Повелѣ́нное та́йнѡ):* Entreated by the prayers of Thine incorporeal hosts, O Christ, and of the Forerunner, the Disciples, and the Prophets, and the Martyrs, and of all the holy and Venerable, and of Thine immaculate unwedded Mother: grant us to walk in Thy light, and account us worthy to obtain Thy Kingdom, for the sake of the compassions of Thy mercy!

*And we read in Saint Ephrem, 3: Discourses on the Departed Fathers;[^339] and the Encomium, 5: of Anastasius of Sinai,[^340] whereof the beginning is: "What is this today, O beloved, with diligence..." and the rest.*

*We recite also the Biblical Odes which are in the Psalter: "Let us sing unto the Lord..."*[^341]

*CANON of the Saint to whom the Temple is dedicated, in the current Tone, with the Heirmos, on 6; and of the Holy Fathers, on 8.*

*Canon of the Holy Fathers. The work of Theodore the Studite. Tone 8.*[^342]

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:* Let us send up a song, ye peoples, unto our marvelous God, Who freed Israel from bondage, singing a song of victory and crying aloud: Let us sing unto Thee, the only Master! (Exod. 15:1).

*Troparion:* In spiritual songs, let us in concord hymn our divine fathers who shone forth in fasting, whom Egypt, the Thebaid, and Libya brought forth, every place, and city, and land![^343]

*Troparion:* Rejoice, first instructor of monastics, Anthony the all-glorious![^344] O God-bearing Amoun,[^345] glory of Nitria! Arsenius the Angel,[^346] bulwark of hesychia, and spiritual Ammonas![^347]

*Troparion:* Adorn thyself, truly a vessel of God, O Agathon of sacred soul![^348] Achillas,[^349] and Amoes,[^350] flowers of the desert; Anoub,[^351] and Alonius;[^352] Amonatha,[^353] and Indes,[^354] radiant pearls of virtues!

*Troparion:* Luminaries of discernment unto us, let Ares be hymned today,[^355] and great Apollos;[^356] and the lights of obedience, Adras[^357] and Acacius;[^358] with them also Abba Cyris shines forth,[^359] like unto a certain morning star!

*Troparion:* A high mountain of life was Auxentius revealed;[^360] and the champion of purity, Abraham the Great;[^361] with them Aphrodisius,[^362] a pillar of temperance, was revealed together with Athenodorus![^363]

*Troparion:* Ammonius shines like a star in heaven among the ascetics,[^364] and the blessed Aninas;[^365] and with them shines the great Antiochus,[^366] and fervent Agapitus,[^367] and whosoever else blazes forth with them!

*Troparion:* With sacred songs let us praise the great Athanasius of Mount Athos,[^368] who fasted radiantly, the great luminary of the whole universe, by whose prayers we are all saved!

*Troparion:* In your divinely-inspired lives truly revealed as the beauty of the Church, O most wise ones, all ye blessed fathers commemorated by name: make supplication unceasingly unto the Lord for us!

*For the Martyrs:* Come, ye faithful lovers of the martyrs, let us honor the martyrs with sacred songs and hymns, chanting and crying aloud in faith unto Christ our God: Let us sing unto Thee, the only Master!

*Glory... Triadikon:* I hymn the Trinity of one essence, hypostatically distinct: the unbegotten Father, the begotten Son, and the Holy Spirit: beginningless kingdom, dominion, and one Godhead!

*Both now... Theotokion:* Rejoice, most holy Temple, dew-covered fleece, sealed fountain of immortal streams! (Judg. 6:37–40; Song of Songs 4:12). Keep Thine own city unconquerable against all that wage war against it, O Sovereign Lady!

*Katavasia: Heirmos of the present Canon: "Let us send up a song, ye peoples..."*

*Ode 2. Which is sung without Stichology, but unto each Troparion the Refrain:*

*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee!*

*Here the Second Canticle of the Psalter is recited.*[^369]

*Ode 2. Heirmos:* See, see that I am your God, begotten of the Father before the ages, and born of the Virgin in the latter times without man, having dissolved the sin of our forefather Adam, as Lover of mankind! (Deut. 32:39).

*Troparion:* With sweet fragrance are we now filled, running as into another paradise, planted of God by the virtues of the ascetics: in which they blossomed through abstinences and tears, bearing varied fruit of life unto God, the most venerable!

*Troparion:* Great Bessarion, who lived like a bird as an Angel;[^370] the new Job again;[^371] steadfast Benjamin;[^372] but also Vitalis the luminary, saving harlots unto God;[^373] with them let divine Euthymius[^374] and glorious Babylas be hymned![^375]

*Troparion:* By height of life thou wast a heaven, O Benedict;[^376] and a house of simplicity, the Shepherd Julian;[^377] but also a certain Basil received the crown of obedience: for having been seen living in the tomb, he displays unto us the brightness of submission![^378]

*Troparion:* A song unto Gelasius, this ever-memorable king over passions;[^379] praise unto Gerasimus, whom the wild beast served: for it came unto him for the sake of the perfection of virtue;[^380] glory also unto Father Germanus, with wise Gaius, well-pleasing servants of Christ![^381]

*Troparion:* David, the boast and glory of Thessalonica;[^382] let God-accepted Daniel be praised;[^383] great in wonders in deed and word, Daniel of Scetis;[^384] and Dius and Dalmatius, leaders of monastics, the confirmation of the faith![^385]

*Troparion:* The dawn of obedience revealed them better than a multitude of stars, as two luminaries shining unto us: both Dometian and Dometius, wise in God;[^386] with them let Bezmenitius also be gloriously hymned![^387]

*For the Martyrs:* Having received the shrine of those who struggled for the Lord's sake as inexhaustible wealth for the faithful: come, ye faithful, let us piously hymn the martyrs, who through faith bring forth fruits of healings for souls and bodies!

*Glory... Triadikon:* O most perfect Unity, transcendent Three-hypostatic Divinity: unbegotten Father, and only-begotten Son, and Spirit Who proceedest from the Father and art revealed through the Son;[^388] one substance and nature, dominion and kingdom: save us all!

*Both now... Theotokion:* Thou alone didst bring a marvelous birth unto humanity; thou alone didst not succumb unto the corruption of nature, having received a seedless and incorrupt birth; therefore as Mother of God, as is meet, do we faithful gloriously praise thee, O pure one!

*Katavasia: "See, see that I am your God..."*

*Ode 3. Heirmos:* "There is none holy as the Lord, and none righteous as our God, Whom all creation hymns: there is none holy besides Thee, O Lord!" (1 Kings 2:2 [LXX 1 Reg. 2:2 / 1 Sam. 2:2]).

*Troparion:* Entering into another paradise of the virtues of the marvelous Fathers, let us taste of their ever-living gift, of animate delight, faithfully praising them!

*Troparion:* Let Eudocius, the most radiant star, be honored; Helladius the day-star; God-inspired Ephrem, with zealous Eulogius, who by deeds and wonders enlightened the ends of the earth![^389]

*Troparion:* Hymned with songs be marvelous Zosimas, and all-honorable Zacharias, and Zenon, and Zoilus; and let great Isaiah and glorious Elijah be honored with them![^390]

*Troparion:* I sing of Theodore of Pherme, all-glorious, and all-hymned Ennathas; with whom also Theodulus, with Theonas do I celebrate; praise marvelous and great Theodosius![^391]

*Troparion:* Be thou praised, O Theoctistus, all-excellent shepherd, and lofty Thalassius; and great Theodore, named the Sykeote, who was revealed on earth as lofty by signs and wonders![^392]

*Troparion:* Other stars shine forth again: most radiant John the Hut-dweller (Kalyvites), and he of the Ladder (Climacus) with three light-bearers, with very many others who shone forth gloriously![^393]

*For the Martyrs:* Let us honor with hymns and spiritual songs, having come together, O lovers of the feasts, the all-honorable and prompt memory of the Martyrs; for they ever pray unto Christ for our race!

*Glory... Triadikon:* O consubstantial Trinity and super-essential Unity, possessing union in division and one essence across the Persons:[^394] unite us into the one desire of Thy commandments!

*Both now... Theotokion:* Moses prefigured thee as the God-receiving Tabernacle...

*Both now... Theotokion (continued):* ...overshadowed by the Seraphim; and the Holy of Holies, O Virgin, prefigured thy pure birthgiving, for Christ to be depicted in the flesh!

*Katavasia: "There is none holy as the Lord, and none righteous as our God, Whom all creation hymns: there is none holy besides Thee, O Lord!"*

*Kontakion of the Temple, and Ikos.*

*Sessional Hymn of the Saints, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́ро предварѝ):* As rays of light, Christ the Sun of Righteousness sent you forth, enlightening the earth, O holy Fathers! Therefore illumine my soul, darkened in malice, by your divine prayers with the divine light of the knowledge of God, O God-blessed ones!

*Another Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom and the Word" (Премꙋ́дрости):* Anthony's meekness and tranquility; the greatness and wonder of Eudoxius; the quietude and hesychia of Paul and Arsenius; the glory of Theoctistus; and all the choirs of the other venerable ones: let us faithful glorify and jointly praise them in hymns, and with them let us sing of the virgin Euphrasia with all the God-wise women, crying aloud in harmony: Pray unto Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who celebrate your holy memory with love![^395]

*Glory... in the same Tone and Podoben:* Having conquered the passions, ye cleaved unto the love of good things, and were clothed in Christ with transcendent glory! Finding rest from your labors, having obtained the life on high through the labors of abstinence, therefore do ye worthily rejoice together with the celestial powers, O our God-bearing Fathers: ask remission of sins for those who celebrate your holy memory with love!

*Both now... Theotokion, in the same Tone and Podoben:* The heavenly gate and the ark, the most holy mountain, the radiant cloud let us hymn, the unburnt bush, the spiritual paradise, the restoration of Eve, the great vessel of the whole universe; for in her was wrought salvation for the world, and remission of ancient transgressions! Therefore we cry aloud unto her: Pray unto Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who piously venerate thy most pure birthgiving!

*If it be a Temple of Christ, or of the Mother of God: Glory... Both now... Sessional Hymn of the Temple.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos: "From the overshadowed mountain, O Word, the Prophet foresaw in divine contemplation the one Theotokos who was to give Thee flesh, and in trembling he glorified Thy power!" (Hab. 3:3).*

*Troparion:* A luminary in the world was revealed the divine Hilarion; a spiritual mountain, great Justin;[^396]

*Troparion (continued):* ...with them let Erasmus be honored; and excellent in glory Hesychius with Joseph!

*Troparion:* In hymns let Peter be praised, who fasted upon Mount Athos; and the Emperor who excellently made all things luminous by the radiant beams of his wonders![^397]

*Troparion:* Jeremiah shone forth by his life; and the divine strength Ischyrion was revealed; with them also shine Charis, Coprius, and Clistor; and together with them the most excellent Cassian![^398]

*Troparion:* Marvelous is the blessing of Callistus; praise the virtuous deeds of Laurence; glorify the benefactions of Longinus; honor the virtues of Leontius![^399]

*Troparion:* Sing of Leontius, the depth of theology; chant of Maximus, the abyss of doctrines; the adornment Marcian, and Mark the well-obedient and submissive unto God![^400]

*Troparion:* The mind of virtues, Macarius the Great; and the city-dweller, the possession of piety; with them is proclaimed Mark the zealous, and from Dalmatia, the eunuch Moses![^401]

*Troparion:* I magnify the courses and struggles of Martinian and Malchus for purity; the virtues of Marcellus, the shepherd of shepherds; and I honor Miles, the raiser of the dead![^402]

*For the Martyrs:* O Martyrs of Christ, offer unceasing prayers unto the Creator and Fashioner for the peace of the world, and for those who honor your memory in hymns!

*Glory... Triadikon:* In a marvelous manner, as one and three, is all the Godhead undivided in three Persons; for the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit are one nature to be adored!

*Both now... Theotokion:* Rejoice, spacious container of God! Rejoice...

*Both now... Theotokion (continued):* ...ark of the New Covenant; rejoice, jar whence the heavenly Manna was given unto all men! (Heb. 9:4).

*Katavasia: "From the overshadowed mountain, O Word, the Prophet foresaw in divine contemplation the one Theotokos who was to give Thee flesh, and in trembling he glorified Thy power!" (Hab. 3:3).*

*Ode 5. Heirmos: "From the darkness of passions, as from the deepest night, having delivered me, vouchsafe my spirit to awake early unto Thee, I pray, in the light of the day of Thy commandments, O Christ!"*

*Troparion:* Come, let us behold the ever-living flowers of Eden's paradise, the efforts of the Fathers cultivated by God, of which the Lord is the sole Husbandman!

*Troparion:* Every spiritual soul is watered by the verbal streams of Nilus; the life of the steersman Nicon shines forth; and with Nathanael, fair Nidersus![^403]

*Troparion:* Xenophon with his two sons, shining with virtues, enlightens us; and great Orsisius, and above all Onuphrius; and who among earthborn men can worthily praise Poemen?[^404]

*Troparion:* Let Pambo be worthily blessed both in deed and word as lofty; and Publius the all-blessed who drove away demons; and equally let radiant Pinnufrius be glorified![^405]

*Troparion:* In like manner let truly all-glorious Paphnutius be honored; Pisora, Patermuthius, Paul the Simple; great Pityrun, and the Exarch of the Fathers![^406]

*Troparion:* Luminously let Pachomius, the luminary of luminaries, be praised; Palamon the fellow-archer; divine Petronius; and with these, let Polychronius be chanted with divine voices![^407]

*Glory... Triadikon:* The Godhead is three in Hypostases, though one in essence, into Whom we were baptized, in Whom we believe: the Father, the Word, and the Co-enthroned Spirit!

*Both now... Theotokion:* Having by a word brought forth the Word beyond speech, O most holy one...

*Both now... Theotokion (continued):* ...do thou unceasingly pray unto Him, that thy flock may ever be delivered from perils!

*Katavasia: "From the darkness of passions, as from the deepest night, having delivered me, vouchsafe my spirit to awake early unto Thee, I pray, in the light of the day of Thy commandments, O Christ!"*

*Ode 6. Heirmos: "Receive me who am held fast by many sins, O Lover of mankind, falling down before Thy compassions, even as the Prophet Jonah, O Lord, and save me!" (Jonah 2:2).*

*Troparion:* Marvelous and all-honorable are our Fathers, whose are the divine dispensations, whose are the wonders, whose are the healings! For who more than these hath shown the power of signs?[^408]

*Troparion:* Let Rabula all-marvelous, and Rufus together, and Sioes likewise, equal unto the Angels, be praised; and with them the divine Sarapion, and Silvanus![^409]

*Troparion:* A four-lighted heaven was revealed upon earth: the twice-paired namesake band of Simeons—three who were upon pillars, and one fool for Christ's sake![^410]

*Troparion:* In the midst of the stars, as the sun of which he was leader, consecrated Sabbas shone forth; and with him Serapion of deeds and Silvanus give illumination![^411]

*Troparion:* Dalmatius and Timothy; Tithoes together with Hyperechius and Pharmuthius; Phocas, Chariton, Chaeremon; let Psoes and venerable Hor be chanted![^412]

*Troparion:* O holy and glorious multitude of the Fathers, remembered and unnamed: deliver from perils those who celebrate your memories with faith!

*Glory... Triadikon:* O Trinity beyond beginning, and divine Unity: Light and Lights do I hymn, Life and Lives, Mind and Word, and the Holy Spirit, the holiness of the one God!

*Both now... Theotokion:* A flower of life sprouted from thy root, O forefather Jesse...

*Both now... Theotokion (continued):* ...leap for joy! For Christ God, Who saves the world, is born of the pure Maiden! (Isa. 11:1).

*Katavasia: "Receive me who am held fast by many sins, O Lover of mankind, falling down before Thy compassions, even as the Prophet Jonah, O Lord, and save me!" (Jonah 2:2).*

*Tone 8, Podoben: "As first-fruits of nature" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ нача́тки):* As preachers of piety and muzzlers of impiety, Thou hast made radiant the choir of the God-bearers, illuminating the world under the sun; by their prayers preserve in perfect peace those who glorify and magnify Thee, singing unto Thee, O Lord: Alleluia![^413]

*Ikos:* I looked upon the good things of this life, seeing with my mind what comes to pass, and having measured the afflictions thereof, I lamented the life of men; but you did I exceedingly call blessed, who have chosen the better part (Luke 10:42), so as to desire Christ and abide with Him, and to chant ever with the Prophet David: Alleluia!

*And we read in the Prologue. And the Synaxarion.*

*Synaxarion on Cheesefare Saturday:*

*Verses:*
*Unto the souls of the righteous, whose memory abides forever,*
*I offer words as repentant oblations.*

On this day we perform the commemoration of all those who have shone forth in contemplation of God and flourishing in divine things, of holy men as well as women.[^414] For gently, through those feasts which have preceded, the God-bearing Fathers have trained us toward spiritual flourishing, and having made us ready and fit for the course of the Fast, and having drawn us away from indulgence and surfeiting, and having well and sufficiently instructed us in the fear of the future Judgment; and since now also throughout this Cheesefare Week they have properly and fittingly purified us as was meet, and have placed two fasts in the midst of it, that is to say of Wednesday and Friday, so that they might little by little awaken and prepare us for that Fast: so also unto this end do they set before us in our midst all those men and women together who have lived venerably and piously through many labors and toils. That through their memory they might make us more fit and ready for the stadium of ascetic struggles against the assaults of enemies, so that having their lives as a certain pattern and guide, and from them...

...drawing help and rescue in battle, we might sail toward spiritual struggles; having this with consideration before ourselves, that they also remained partakers of the same human nature with us. For even as Hetmans, having drawn up their regiments in battle array, before stepping to the head of the ranks, encourage and rouse their army by words and examples, and by the remembrance of ancient heroes who famously led as hetmans and fought valiantly,[^415] and thence having taken heart and strength, with hope of victory, go against the enemy and engage with their whole soul: so now also the God-bearing Fathers act wisely and prudently. For having fortified both male and female sex unto spiritual struggles through those persons who lived holily, they thus lead them into the course of the Fast. That looking upon the pattern and portrait (*counterfeit*)[^416] of their unslothful and unyielding life, we might practice many and diverse virtues, as each hath strength; and first of all and chiefest, love, and a prudent departure from unprofitable deeds and actions. Unto this also, that this fast be not of foods alone, but also of the tongue, of wrath, and of the eyes, and simply speaking, an emptying and departure from every evil!
