For this reason the Holy Fathers established the present commemoration of all the saints here, bringing into our midst those who through fasting and other good deeds pleased Christ God and were united unto Him, and stirring us up by their example unto the race of virtues, and arming ourselves bravely and valiantly from heaven against the passions; and giving us a certain impetus and means, that if we also apply care and diligence equal unto theirs, nothing shall be a hindrance to accomplish even as much as they accomplished, and to become worthy of the same honors, since they also were together of the same nature with us.

Concerning Cheesefare Week, some relate that the Emperor Heraclius instituted it, which previously was Meatfare.[^417] For having waged war against Khosrow and the Persians for six years, he promised God that if he should prevail against that foe, he would change this week and establish it in the midst of fasting and food, which he also did. And again I consider that even if that might indeed have happened by circumstance, nevertheless this week was devised by the Holy Fathers for a certain purification, that from meat and copious eating, we might not cast ourselves suddenly into the perfect and strict fast...

...and suffering harm from the sudden transition, find it burdensome and hard; likewise that we might not harm the constitution of our bodies, but gently and little by little depart from fat and luxurious foods; and refraining like unbridled horses, through meager and simple food or fodder, might receive and place upon ourselves the bridle of the fast. For even as on the side of the soul they achieved this through these parables, so likewise on the side of the body have they regulated it, cutting off little by little whatsoever hinders fasting. By the prayers of all Thy venerable ones, O Christ our God, have mercy on us, now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

*Ode 7. Heirmos: "Thou Who didst bedew the children in the furnace, and didst preserve the Virgin who bore Thee after childbirth: blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our Fathers!" (Dan. 3:26, 52).*

*Troparion:* Come, let us offer songs as is due unto the women who lived venerably and equally unto the Angels, by whose prayers let us cry aloud: O God, save us all![^418]

*Troparion:* Let Christ-bearing Bryene with divine Febronia be honored, and with Theomais and Hieria; and let Platonida also be chanted with them, and Melandrosia faithfully![^419]

*Troparion:* Praise unto the angel-like Euphrasias, with the two Theodoras, and the all-wealthy Anastasias; unceasing singing and glory unto those who marvelously served God![^420]

*Troparion:* Mary of Egypt is a light in the world; and she who was named Marinus appeared as a star unto the universe; Euphrosyne is a sun, shining with virtues![^421]

*Troparion:* Radiant like a flame was Theodula in life; and Theodota and Julitta give illumination in struggle; and all-wealthy Isidora walks alongside them in deeds![^422]

*Troparion:* Let heavenly-wise Marina now be honored with great Matrona; and let Syncletica and Sarah, with Justina, be praised in hymns as wise![^423]

*Troparion:* Pelagia, an Angel of the Lord; Thais, a lamp...

*Troparion (continued):* ...of repentance; with them let her be sung, and whatsoever other woman excelled in fasting![^424]

*Glory... Triadikon:* Unto the Father with the Son let us bow down, and unto the Holy Spirit in equal measure, glorifying and crying aloud faithfully: Glory to Thee, O Trinity, Unity, our God!

*Both now... Theotokion:* O Theotokos Virgin, who didst bear the Savior and Master for the world, O blessed one: pray ever unto Him to have mercy upon our souls!

*Katavasia: "Thou Who didst bedew the children in the furnace, and didst preserve the Virgin who bore Thee after childbirth: blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our Fathers!" (Dan. 3:26, 52).*

*Ode 8. Heirmos: "Him Who was glorified upon the holy mountain, and through fire in the bush made known unto Moses the mystery of the Ever-Virgin: hymn ye the Lord and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!" (Exod. 3:2).*

*Troparion:* The shepherd and wise teachers, the hierarchs of Christ's Church, together with the venerable ones let us all chant, hymning and supremely exalting the Lord unto all ages![^425]

*Troparion:* As is due, let Basil the Great and with him truly much-suffering Athanasius be chanted in hymns, together with Gregory, pre-eminent in theology unto the ages![^426]

*Troparion:* John the Golden-mouthed, with the Cyrils, the God-wise pillars, let us glorify; likewise also Hesychius the second Theologian, with God-proclaiming and divine Meletius![^427]

*Troparion:* Glory unto Gregory of Nyssa, with the two wonder-working fathers; and let all-wise divine Epiphanius, with Amphilochius, radiant luminaries, be chanted unto the ages![^428]

*Troparion:* Metrophanes, the glory of hierarchs, with Nectarius, Atticus, and Gennadius; and with them let us sing of Anatolius, living luminaries, together with Eusebius and all-wise Proclus![^429]

*Troparion:* Unto Nicholas the sacred preacher, unto Sophronius...

*Troparion (continued):* ...truly honey-tongued, with Eulalius I sing; and unto Diadochus, together with Eustathius and Juvenal, supreme fathers![^430]

*Refrain: Bless we the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord! (Dan. 3:56).*

*Triadikon:* As a Unity in essence do I hymn Thee, as a Trinity in Persons do I honor Thee, O Father, Son, and all-holy Spirit; the dominion without beginning of Thy kingdom do I glorify unto the ages!

*Both now... Theotokion:* Thou wast revealed as the mountain of God, O Theotokos, in whom Christ took up His dwelling, and made those who sing divine temples: "Hymn the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!"

*Refrain: We praise, we bless, we worship the Lord!*

*Katavasia: "Him Who was glorified upon the holy mountain, and through fire in the bush made known unto Moses the mystery of the Ever-Virgin: hymn ye the Lord and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!" (Exod. 3:2).*

*And the more honorable stichology [Magnificat, Luke 1:46–55].*

*Ode 9. Heirmos: "Of Him Who was manifested in the bush and on the mountain, and Who in these latter times was incarnate of the Virgin for our salvation, let us magnify Him, the Lover of mankind!"*

*Troparion:* Who shall tell of the boldness of Ambrose? Or how shall one utter the wisdom of Hierotheus? And the steadfastness according to faith of the God-bearing Alexanders?[^431]

*Troparion:* Let divine Phaedimus and God-bearing Spyridon, with Antipatros, Pambo, Palladius, and Nonnus, Jerome, and all-honorable Germanus, be chanted as divine luminaries![^432]

*Troparion:* Let Dionysius, great in divine things, be honored as a mystic of divine mysteries; much-suffering Clement; and Flavian, and great Paul, the first preachers of confession![^433]

*Troparion:* Let Michael of Synnada be sung with Tarasius; Nicephorus again with Theodore most zealously; and Theophanes, truly of sacred name, defenders of the Icons of Christ![^434]

*Troparion:* Let Peter and Ignatius, truly God-bearers, as Apostles and hieromartyrs of Christ, be hymned together with Polycarp and Cyprian, martyrs of Christ![^435]

*Troparion:* O venerable fathers and hieromonks of the Lord, with the hieromartyrs and holy women, all ye named and unnamed: pray that our souls may be saved!

*Glory... Triadikon:* O God, One in Trinity, glory be unto Thee unceasingly! For though Each is God, yet art Thou One by nature: Father, Son, and Holy Spirit, in tri-radiant personal properties!

*Both now... Theotokion:* Of old on Mount Sinai, Moses foresaw the bush burning with fire yet unconsumed: thy God-receiving womb, O Virgin, which received the immaterial Fire! (Exod. 3:2).

*Katavasia: "The birthgiving of the Ever-Virgin, manifested unto the Lawgiver on the mountain in fire and the bush for the salvation of us the faithful, let us magnify with unceasing hymns!" (Exod. 3:2).*

*Exapostilarion of the Fathers, Tone 2, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены ᲂу҆слы́шите):*[^436] Ye who renounced the world and took up the Cross, O multitude of venerable fathers, with the choirs of martyrs, the assembly of hierarchs, and the congregation of women: make us joyful, that we may worthily hymn your all-praised memory! (Matt. 16:24).

*Glory... Both now... Tone 2, Podoben: "Let us assemble with the disciples" (Съ ᲂу҆чн҃ки взы́иди):*[^437] Those who shone forth in fasting, the God-bearing fathers and hierarchs, together with the choirs of venerable women and hieromartyrs, let us praise with all manner of joy, that we may be sanctified, and through their supplications and the prayers of the Mother of God, may easily accomplish the course of the Fast!

*At the Praises: Every breath... Stichera of the Fathers on 4. Tone 8, Podoben: "In Eden of old" (Въ є҆де́мѣ ра́и):*[^438]

*Sticheron 1:* All ye faithful, let us magnify in hymns the whole multitude of the fathers who fasted in venerable manner, and let us praise with songs the hierarchs of Christ...

*Sticheron 1 (continued):* ...in God-pleasing harmony, O brethren! For they lived in abstinence and in pure fasting, and made known unto us the Gospel of Christ. With them let us also hymn the God-bearing and radiant women, zealously emulating their manner of life in our souls in God-like manner, that we may find there the forgiveness of our transgressions!

*Sticheron 2:* Those who shone forth joyfully in fasting and lived in venerable manner, let us gloriously praise, O brethren, as those who lived well and were piously translated in joy unto eternal life, having swiftly run by virtue and honor unto the imperishable and blessed inheritance which is there! These let us worthily honor, that through their intercession we may obtain mercy from God, and eternal glory and joy, and be delivered from the inexorable torments that are there!

*Sticheron 3:* O choir of all hierarchs, celebration of the venerable, assembly of the fasters and of venerable women who lived in piety: being besought by grace, pray ye unto the only Good and all-compassionate Lord to have compassion also upon us, that through your prayers we may be preserved in peace, and delivered from the everlasting condemnation of that place, and unceasingly enjoy the future inheritance unto ages of ages, rejoicing with you, and unceasingly sending up a song of praise unto the Giver of life!

*Sticheron 4:* A God-like festivity, O faithful, let us applaud abundantly today: of the hierarchs and fasters, the hieromartyrs, and the venerable and pious women! For they truly despised corruptible and transitory things as a spider's web, and counted them as refuse... (Phil. 3:8).

*Sticheron 4 (continued):* ...that they might gain Christ their King, and those divine things which eye hath not seen, nor ear ever heard! Through their prayers, O God, deliver our souls from corruption! (1 Cor. 2:9).

*Glory... Tone 6:* O venerable fathers, into all the earth went out the sound of your righteous deeds! Therefore in the heavens have ye found the reward of your labors, having routed the hosts of demons and attained unto the ranks of the Angels, whose blameless life ye zealously emulated! Having boldness unto the Lord, entreat peace for our souls! (LXX Ps. 18:5; Rom. 10:18).[^439]

*Both now... Theotokion: "O Theotokos, thou art the true vine..."*

*The Great Doxology. After the Trisagion, Troparion of the Fathers; Glory... Both now... Theotokion; the 4 litanies; and the dismissal. And the 1st Hour.*

*At the Hours, Troparion and Kontakion of the Fathers.*

*It should be known that from the Sunday of the Publican and the Pharisee we read on Saturdays the regular Epistle and Gospel of the cycle first, and then of the Saint, even unto the Sunday of All Saints.*[^440]

*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes from the Canon of the Holy Fathers, Odes 3 and 6. Prokeimenon of the Fathers alone: "Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones" (LXX Ps. 115:6). Verse: "What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He hath rendered unto me?" (LXX Ps. 115:3). Another Prokeimenon: "The venerable shall boast in glory, and shall rejoice upon their beds" (LXX Ps. 149:5). Verse: "Sing unto the Lord a new song" (LXX Ps. 149:1). Epistle of the day, to the Romans, pericope 115: "Brethren, let us pursue the things which make for peace..." (Rom. 14:19–23, 16:25–27). Another Epistle for the Venerable Fathers, to the Hebrews, pericope 335: "Brethren, obey your leaders..." (Heb. 13:17–21). Alleluia, Tone 2. Verse: "Planted in the house of the Lord, in the courts of our God they shall flourish" (LXX Ps. 91:14). Verse: "Be glad in the Lord, and rejoice, ye righteous" (LXX Ps. 31:11). Gospel of the day, from Matthew, pericope 16: "The Lord said: Take heed that ye do not your alms before men..." (Matt. 6:1–13). Another Gospel from Luke, for the Venerable ones, pericope 24: "At that time, Jesus stood on a level place..." (Luke 6:17–23). Communion Verse: "In everlasting memory shall the righteous be. Alleluia" (LXX Ps. 111:6).*[^441]

*[Iconography: Half-page woodcut engraving of the Fall and Expulsion of Adam and Eve from Paradise. At upper left: the Creation of Eve from Adam's side ("Созда́ні́е Еѵ҆́вы"). In the center: the Tree of the Knowledge of Good and Evil with the serpent coiled around its trunk offering the fruit to Eve, who gives it to Adam ("Престꙋпле́ні́е", The Transgression). At right: a Cherub with a fiery sword driving Adam and Eve forth from the gates of Eden ("И҆згна́ні́е и҆з̾ Ра́я", The Expulsion from Paradise). Below: Adam tilling the ground with a spade and Eve spinning wool, inscribed with the divine decree: "In the sweat of thy face shalt thou eat thy bread" (Gen. 3:19).]*

## CHEESEFARE SUNDAY: THE EXPULSION OF ADAM

*Vigil as usual.*

*On Saturday evening, at Little Vespers: Stichera of the Resurrection; and the Aposticha of the Theotokos according to custom.*

*At Great Vespers: "Blessed is the man..." (LXX Ps. 1); the entire 1st Kathisma.*

*At "Lord, I have cried": Stichera from the Octoechos: 3 of the Resurrection, and 3 Anatolian; and from the Triodion on 4. Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Всѐ ᲂу҆пова́ні́е возло́жше):*[^442]

*Sticheron 1:* My Creator and Lord, having taken dust from the earth, breathed into me the breath of life and honored me, creating me as ruler upon earth of all visible things, and companion of the Angels! But crafty Satan, using the serpent as his instrument, deceived me by food and separated me from the glory of God... (Gen. 2:7; 3:1–6).

*Sticheron 1 (continued):* ...and handed me over unto the nethermost death in the earth! But as Thou art the merciful Master, call me back again!

*Sticheron 2:* Wretched that I am, I was stripped of the garment woven by God, having transgressed Thy divine commandment, O Lord, by the counsel of the enemy; and I am now clothed with fig leaves and coats of skins, for I was condemned in the sweat of my brow to eat weary bread, and the earth was cursed to bring forth thorns and thistles unto me! But do Thou Who in these latter times wast incarnate of the Virgin, call me back and lead me again into Paradise! (Gen. 3:7, 17–21).[^443]

*Sticheron 3:* O all-venerable Paradise, most fair beauty, tabernacle fashioned by God, endless joy and delight, glory of the righteous, splendor of the prophets, and dwelling-place of the saints: by the rustling of thy leaves, pray unto the Creator and God to open unto me the gates which I closed by my transgression, and vouchsafe me to partake of the Tree of Life, and of that joy whereof I formerly delighted in thee! (Gen. 3:24).

*Sticheron 4, Idiomelon:* Adam was driven out of Paradise through disobedience, and cast out from delight, deceived by the words of a woman; and he sits naked opposite that place, weeping: "Woe is me!" Therefore let us all make haste to receive the time of the Fast, giving ear unto the traditions of the Gospel, that having become well-pleasing unto Christ through them, we may receive again the habitation of Paradise! (Gen. 3:6, 23–24).

*Glory... Tone 6:* Adam sat opposite Paradise, and weeping over his nakedness, he lamented: "Woe is me, who was persuaded and robbed by deceitful trickery, and estranged from glory! Woe is me, who was naked in simplicity, but am now perplexed in distress! But...

*Glory (continued):* ...O Paradise, no longer shall I delight in thy sweetness; no longer shall I behold the Lord my God and Creator! For I shall go into the earth from whence I was taken! (Gen. 3:19). O merciful and compassionate One, I cry unto Thee: Have mercy on me who am fallen!"[^444]

*Both now... Theotokion: the 1st of the Tone in the Octoechos in course.*

*At the Litiya: The usual Stichera: the Sticheron of the Saint whose Temple it is; and of the Theotokos, the Amorion hymns of the Tone. If it be the Afterfeast of the Meeting of the Lord, see above (see fol. 104)[^445].*

*Glory... Tone 6:* The sun hid its rays, and the moon with the stars turned into blood; the mountains were dismayed, and the hills trembled, when Paradise was shut! As Adam went forth, beating his hands upon his face, he said: "O merciful One, have mercy on me who am fallen!" (Joel 2:10, 31).[^446]

*Both now... Theotokion, in the same Tone:* Mystically we hymn thee, O Theotokos Mary! For thou hast been revealed as the throne of the Great King, the all-holy tabernacle wider than the heavens, the chariot of the Cherubim, higher than the Seraphim, the bridal chamber of glory; for from thee came forth God incarnate of all: entreat Him that our souls may be saved!

*At the Aposticha: Stichera in the Octoechos, and according to the alphabet: 3.*

*Glory... Tone 6:* Adam was driven out of Paradise because of food; therefore sitting opposite it, he lamented and groaned with a sorrowful voice, saying: "Woe is me! What have I suffered, wretch that I am? I transgressed a single commandment of the Master, and was deprived of all good things! O most holy Paradise, planted for my sake, and shut because of Eve...

*Glory (continued):* ...pray unto Him Who made thee, and Who fashioned me, that I may be filled with thy blossoms!" Therefore also the Savior answered unto him: "I desire not the destruction of My creature, but desire that he be saved, and come unto the knowledge of the truth; for him that comes unto Me, I will in no wise cast out!" (1 Tim. 2:4; John 6:37).[^447]

*Both now... in the same Tone: "The Creator and Deliverer..." (see fol. 20)[^448].*

*Then, "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart..." (Luke 2:29–32). After the Trisagion: "O Theotokos Virgin..." thrice, and the blessing of the loaves. And we read the Homily of John Chrysostom on the Expulsion of Adam. And the rest of the Vigil according to custom.*

*For the Saint of the day, we sing at Compline, as indicated on the preceding Friday (see fol. 30)[^449].*

*If the Forefeast of the Meeting falls on this Cheesefare Sunday, or on the preceding three Sundays wherein the Triodion is sung: then we sing the Forefeast together with the Sunday service and with the Triodion, as indicated above (see fol. 3)[^450]. Unto Saint Tryphon we sing when the Superior wills.*

*If the Feast itself of the Meeting falls on this Cheesefare Sunday, the rubric for it is written under the Sunday of the Publican and the Pharisee (see fol. 3)[^451]; with only certain modifications under Meatfare Sunday (see fol. 54)[^452], and so forth: it is celebrated for one day only, without an Afterfeast.*

*If the Feast of the Meeting was on Cheesefare Thursday, or on Friday, or on Cheesefare Saturday, its Apodosis is celebrated on this Cheesefare Sunday. And the service is sung for it from the Triodion, as on the Feast itself, as on the Sunday of the Publican, the Prodigal, and Meatfare Sunday, without Old Testament readings; even as on other Sundays with a Saint having an All-Night Vigil.*

*If the Finding of the Head of the Forerunner falls on this day, the rubric for it is written under Meatfare Sunday (see fol. 85)[^453].*

*If the Finding of the Precious Head of the Forerunner falls on Monday of the 1st Week of the Fast, the entire Feast of the Forerunner is sung on this Cheesefare Sunday.*

*On Cheesefare Sunday at Matins:*

*At "God is the Lord": Troparion of the Resurrection, twice; and its Theotokion. At the 1st and 2nd Psalter readings: Sessional Hymns of the Resurrection of the Tone. The Blameless [LXX Ps. 118] with their Troparia. Hypakoe of the Tone. And the Polyeleos, as indicated on the preceding Sunday, which is of the Prodigal Son (see fol. 20)[^454].*

*When they begin Psalm 136 [By the waters of Babylon], then the Priest censes the holy icons, the Superior, and the rest of the brethren. And we begin to read from the First Book of the Hexaemeron of Chrysostom. Then the Hymns of Ascents, and the Prokeimenon of the Tone. "Let every breath..." The Resurrection Gospel. "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ..." Psalm 50.*

*Then we sing in place of "Through the prayers of the Apostles...":*

*Glory... Penitential Sticheron, Tone 8:* Open unto me the doors of repentance, O Giver of life! For my spirit rises early toward Thy holy temple, bearing the temple of my body all defiled; but as Thou art compassionate, cleanse me by Thy tender mercy![^455]

*Both now... Theotokion:* Guide me onto the paths of salvation, O Theotokos! For with vile sins I have defiled my soul, and in sloth have spent my whole life; but by thine intercessions, deliver me from all uncleanness!

*Then in Tone 6: "Have mercy on me, O God, according to Thy great mercy..." (LXX Ps. 50:1). And instead of "Jesus, having risen from the dead...", the Sticheron: "When I ponder the multitude of evil deeds which I have done..." written under the Sunday of the Publican and the Pharisee (see fol. 5)[^456]. Then, "O God, save Thy people...", and "Lord, have mercy," 12 times.*

*Canons from the Octoechos, 3: of the Resurrection on 4; the Cross and Resurrection on 2; and the Theotokos on 2; and from the Triodion on 6. The composition of Christopher the Protoasekretis.*[^457]

*Ode 1, Tone 6. Heirmos: "Having traversed the deep on foot as if on dry land, and beholding their pursuer Pharaoh submerged, Israel cried aloud: Let us sing unto God a song of victory!" (Exod. 14:29–15:1).*

*Troparion 1:* Come, O my wretched soul, weep today over thine own deeds, remembering the first nakedness in Eden, whereby thou wast cast out from delight and unceasing joy!

*Troparion 2:* Out of the abundance of Thy mercy and compassions, O Maker of creation and Creator of all, having of old quickened me from the dust, Thou didst command me to hymn Thee with Thine holy Angels!

*Troparion 3:* For the sake of the wealth of Thy goodness, O Creator and Lord, Thou didst plant the sweetness of Paradise in Eden, commanding me to delight in sweet, fair, and unfading fruits!

*Glory... Troparion:* Woe is me, O my wretched soul! Authority was given thee by God to enjoy the things in Eden, yet thou wast commanded not to eat the fruit of knowledge: why didst thou transgress the law of God? (Gen. 2:16–17).

*Both now... Theotokion:* O Virgin Mother of God, as daughter of Adam by race, yet by grace the Mother of Christ God: call me back now, who have been driven out of Eden!

*And we sing as Katavasia the Heirmos of the present Canon:*
*"Having traversed the deep on foot as if on dry land, and beholding their pursuer Pharaoh submerged, Israel cried aloud: Let us sing unto God a song of victory!" (Exod. 14:29–15:1).*
*Or: "I shall open my mouth...", at the discretion of the ecclesiarch.*

*Ode 3. Heirmos: "There is none holy as Thou, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O good One, and established them upon the rock of Thy confession!" (1 Sam. / 1 Kgs. 2:2).*

*Troparion 1:* The crafty serpent, of old envying mine honor, whispered deceitfully into the ears of Eve, through whom I was deceived and driven out—woe is me!—from the choir of life! (Gen. 3:1–6).

*Troparion 2:* Stretching forth my hand audaciously, I tasted of the tree of knowledge, whereof God commanded me by no means to taste; and bitterly was I cast out from divine glory! (Gen. 3:6).

*Glory... Troparion:* Woe is me, O my passion-loving soul! How didst thou not recognize the deception? How didst thou not perceive the flattery and the envy of the enemy? But thou wast darkened in mind, and didst transgress the commandment of thy Creator!

*Both now... Theotokion:* O pure Lady, my hope and protection, who alone by thy childbearing didst cover of old the nakedness of fallen Adam: clothe me again with incorruption, O pure Virgin!

*Katavasia. Heirmos: "There is none holy as Thou, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O good One, and established them upon the rock of Thy confession!" (1 Sam. / 1 Kgs. 2:2).*

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph was amazed" (Ꙋди́вися І҆ѡ́сифъ):*[^458] Adam was driven out from the delight of Paradise through bitter food in intemperance, having failed to keep the commandment of the Master, and was condemned to work the earth from whence he was taken, and in much toil to eat his bread! Therefore let us love abstinence, that we may not weep outside Paradise like him, but may enter therein! (Gen. 3:17–19, 23).

*Another [Sessional Hymn], Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross" (Възнесы́йся):*[^459] Now is the season of virtues revealed, and the Judgment is at the doors; let us not sorrow, but come, as we fast let us offer tears, compunction, and almsgiving, crying aloud: "We have sinned more than the sand of the sea! But forgive all, O Deliverer of all, that we may receive the incorruptible crown!" (Jas. 5:9; Prayer of Manasseh 9).

*Both now... Theotokion, in the same Tone:* Never shall we, the unworthy, cease to speak of thy mighty acts, O Theotokos! For hadst thou not stood interceding for us, who would have delivered us from so many dangers? Who would have preserved us free until now? We shall not depart from thee, O Sovereign Lady, for thou ever savest thy servants from all evils!

*Then the Reading from the Theologian [Gregory of Nazianzus], on the Calamity of the City, whose beginning is: "Why do ye dissolve the praised order?"; and we make two stases.*[^460]

*Ode 4. Heirmos: "Christ is my power, my God and Lord, the venerable Church chants in God-pleasing manner, crying aloud from a pure mind, keeping festival in the Lord!" (Hab. 3:2).*

*Troparion 1:* I, passionate wretch, was accounted worthy of honor by Thee, the Master, in Eden! Woe is me, how was I deceived, and being envied by the devil, was cast out from before Thy face!

*Troparion 2:* Weep for me, ye ranks of Angels, ye beauties of Paradise and trees, and celestial splendor therein, who was wretchedly deceived and estranged from God!

*Troparion 3:* O blessed woods, trees planted by God, beauty of Paradise...

*Troparion 3 (continued):* ...O beauty, now shed tears over me, from your leaves as from eyes, over me who am naked and estranged from the glory of God!

*Glory... Troparion:* No longer do I behold thee, nor do I delight in thy most sweet and divine splendor, O all-honored Paradise! For naked was I cast down upon the earth, having provoked my Creator to anger!

*Both now... Theotokion:* O holy Lady, who didst open the gates of Paradise unto all the faithful, which Adam once locked by his transgression: open unto me the doors of mercy!

*Katavasia. Heirmos: "Christ is my power, God and Lord, the venerable Church chants in God-pleasing manner, crying aloud from a pure mind, keeping festival in the Lord!" (Hab. 3:2).*

*Ode 5. Heirmos: "By Thy divine light, O Good One, enlighten with love, I pray Thee, the souls of those who dawn early unto Thee, that they may behold Thee, O Word of God, the true God, Who recallest us from the gloom of sins!" (Isa. 26:9).*

*Troparion 1:* Envying my blessed life in Paradise of old, the enemy, the hater of mankind, tripped me up in the guise of a serpent, and showed me forth estranged from everlasting glory!

*Troparion 2:* I sob and weep in my soul, and seek to add a multitude of tears to mine eyes, when I look and perceive my nakedness, which I obtained through transgression!

*Glory... Troparion:* From the earth was I fashioned by the hand of God, and that I should return again unto the earth did I hear, passionate wretch that I am! Who will not bewail me, who was cast away from God, and exchanged Eden for Hades? (Gen. 2:7, 3:19).

*Both now... Theotokion:* As the mystical Bridal Chamber of glory do all we, the faithful, proclaim thee, O all-immaculate Mother of God! Therefore do I pray thee, O pure one: make me, who am fallen, an inheritor of the bridal chamber of Paradise!

*Katavasia. Heirmos: "By Thy divine light, O Good One, enlighten with love, I pray Thee, the souls of those who dawn early unto Thee, that they may behold Thee, O Word of God, the true God, Who recallest us from the gloom of sins!" (Isa. 26:9).*

*Ode 6. Heirmos: "The sea of life, surging with the tempest of temptations, beholding, and running unto Thy calm haven, I cry unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O Greatly-merciful One!" (Jonah 2:2–3).*

*Troparion 1:* With a God-woven garment didst Thou clothe me, O Savior, in Eden as compassionate; but I transgressed Thy commandment, believing the deceiver, and was seen naked, wretched that I am! (Gen. 3:7, 21).

*Troparion 2:* O my all-passionate soul, thou hast departed from God! Through thine inattention thou wast deprived of the delight of Paradise, parted from the Angels, and brought down into corruption. O the fall!

*Glory... Troparion:* Have mercy, take pity, O Almighty God, upon the work of Thy hands! Disdain me not, I pray Thee, O Good One, who have separated myself from the choir of Thine Angels!

*Both now... Theotokion:* O Mary called of God, Lady of all, who didst bear the Lord, the King of all and Deliverer: recall me, who am held captive, unto the glory of Paradise!

*Katavasia. Heirmos: "Beholding the sea of life surging with the tempest of temptations, running unto Thy calm haven, I cry unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O Greatly-merciful One!" (Jonah 2:2–3).*

*Kontakion, Idiomelon, Tone 6:*[^461] O Master, Guide of wisdom, Giver of prudence, Instructor of the foolish, and Defender of the poor: strengthen and give understanding unto my heart! Do Thou, the Word of the Father, give me speech, for behold, I shall not forbid my lips to cry unto Thee: "O Merciful One, have mercy upon me who am fallen!"

*Ikos:*[^462] Adam sat then and wept, opposite the delight of Paradise, beating his face with his hands, and saying: "O Merciful One, have mercy upon me who am fallen!"

Seeing the Angel who had cast him out and locked the door of the divine Paradise, Adam groaned heavily and said: "O Merciful One, have mercy upon me who am fallen!"

Suffer together with thy master who has become impoverished, O Paradise; by the rustling of thy leaves beseech the Creator that He lock thee not! "O Merciful One, have mercy upon me who am fallen!"

O Paradise all-virtuous, all-holy, all-rich, planted for the sake of Adam and locked for the sake of Eve, beseech God for him who is fallen: "O Merciful One, have mercy upon me who am fallen!"

*Then we read the Prologue, and the Synaxarion:*

*Synaxarion for Cheesefare Sunday: On the Expulsion of Adam:*[^463]

*Verses:*
Let the world bitterly lament together with the forebears of our race,
Which likewise fell together with them, when through sweet food they fell.

On this day we make commemoration of the fall from the delight of Paradise of Adam, who was created first, which our most divine and holy Fathers arranged and set before the Holy Forty Days—that is, before the Great Fast. As it were, they show forth through these matters how very profitable the medicine of fasting is unto bodily nature, and on the contrary, what shame and reproach come from gluttony and boorishness. Leaving aside, therefore, speaking of those matters which have been begun in the world—for they are well-nigh innumerable—they set forth Adam, who was created first, showing clearly what great evil he suffered because he would not fast in the least, and thence brought that same evil upon our nature. And that this was the first divine commandment among men—the good commandment of the Fast—which he did not keep, but became obedient unto his belly, and yet more unto the Deceiver, being caught through Eve; therefore not only did he fail to attain unto godhood, but drew upon himself and brought in death, and made the whole human race partakers of his ruin. Because of the transgression of food by the first Adam, the Lord fasted forty days and was obedient.[^464] For which cause also the present Holy Forty Days was discovered and instituted by the Holy Apostles, so that by keeping that which our forefather Adam did not keep and thereby lost incorruption, we, by keeping it, might seek out and receive that incorruption again. In another manner understanding it, as we said above, the aim and goal of the Holy Fathers is this: that all those works which have been wrought by God from the beginning even unto the very end should be briefly encompassed and embraced. And since the cause of all that has happened and happens unto us is transgression and Adam's fall from the delight of Paradise, according unto that meaning they set this before us, that when we bring it unto our remembrance, we might flee above all intemperance in all things, and emulate virtues.

On the sixth day, therefore, Adam was created by the hand of God, being honored by His image through the breathing of life; and receiving the commandment at once, he dwelt in Paradise until the sixth hour. Thereafter, having transgressed that commandment, he was cast out from thence. (Gen. 1:26–27; 2:7, 16–17; 3:6, 23–24).

Philo the Jew, indeed, says that Adam dwelt in Paradise a hundred years; others say seven days or seven years, out of honor for the sevenfold number.[^465] And that in the sixth hour he stretched forth his hands and touched the fruit, Christ the New Adam also manifested, Who in the sixth hour and on the sixth day stretched forth His hands upon the Cross, healing his fall and perdition.[^466]

He was created, moreover, midway between corruption and incorruption, so that unto whichever side he should incline by free will and through adoption, he should acquire that for himself. It was a possible thing, therefore, for God to make man sinless—that is, unable to sin; but He did not do so, in order that virtue might also proceed from his own free will. For this reason the Law gives him permission, indeed, to touch all the plants, but one—not so! That is to say, it was permissible to have and to ponder knowledge and understanding of the divine power which is revealed from all created things, but knowledge concerning the divine nature in nowise! Which also Gregory the Theologian says philosophizing: that the divine thoughts and concepts are trees, and the plants are contemplation of God.[^467] That is, God permitted and commanded Adam, he says, to consider diligently all other elements and qualities, and to distinguish them with his intellect, and from thence to glorify God—for this, indeed, is true delight and joy; and likewise concerning his own bodily nature. But concerning God, who He is according unto His nature, and in what place, and how He made all things from nothing and brought them into being, he was by no means to inquire. But he, that is, our Forefather Adam, leaving other things, inquired rather concerning God, and wishing clearly to know concerning His nature, investigated inquisitively. Whence, as one who was yet imperfect, most simple, and a child in such great matters, he fell, especially when Satan put into him a deceitful imagination and a false opinion that he should be God! (Gen. 3:5).

The great and divine Chrysostom, moreover, says that that tree in Paradise possesses a certain twofold power; he also says that Paradise is upon earth, and philosophizes that it is both...

...spiritual and sensible, even as was Adam also; and that both of these are midway between corruption and incorruption, at once preserving Scripture in its entirety and not adhering strictly to the literal text.[^468]

Some also say that that tree of disobedience was a fig tree; for having perceived their nakedness immediately, they made use of the leaves of that tree and covered themselves (Gen. 3:7). For this reason also Christ cursed the fig tree, as that which was the cause of so great a transgression (Matt. 21:19). For that tree has a certain resemblance unto sin: first it is sweetening, then sharp and stinging as to its leaves, and finally sticky because of its milk.

There are also some who understood that tree to be the intercourse and carnal knowledge of Adam with Eve, but not well.

The aforesaid Adam, therefore, having transgressed, and having clothed himself in a mortal body and received the curse, was cast out of Paradise, and the flaming sword was commanded to guard the gates of Paradise (Gen. 3:19, 24). And sitting before Paradise, he wept over how many good things he had lost and that he was naked, because he had not fasted for a little time; and all the race proceeding from him became partakers of woes equal unto his. Until He Who created us took pity upon our nature, when Satan had alienated it for a long time and was bringing it unto perdition: Jesus, I say, born of the Holy Virgin, having lived most excellently upon earth and shown us the way through virtues contrary to that one—that is, through Fasting and Humility—and having vanquished him who had deceived and cheated us, brought us back again unto our first dignity!

Wishing, therefore, to set all these things before our eyes, the God-bearing Fathers set forth for us the divine works throughout the whole Triodion. First, then, from the Old Testament, among which are the creation of all things and of man, and likewise Adam's fall from the delight of Paradise, whereof we now make remembrance. Thereafter also certain things from the words of Moses, the Prophets, and most of all David, and between them adding something from Grace, that is, Mercy. Then, proceeding in order, also from the New Testament, whereof the first is the Annunciation,[^469] which by ineffable divine arrangement is constantly found and celebrated well-nigh within the Holy Forty Days; as also concerning Lazarus,[^470] and concerning the branches spread by the children when Christ rode into Jerusalem,[^471] and concerning the Holy and Great Week, the holy Gospels are read through...

...and the very holy and saving Sufferings of Christ are tenderly and subtly chanted. Finally, when concerning the Resurrection and other mysteries even unto the Descent of the All-Holy Spirit, the sacred Acts of the Apostles are read—that is to say, how the Preaching took place and assembled all the holy ones. For the Acts confirm and corroborate the Resurrection through miracles.[^472]

Since therefore, because Adam did not fast even once, we suffered so many woes and evils, commemoration of this is now set forth at the entrance unto the Holy Forty Days. So that we, remembering what great evil that which is not fasting brought in, might strive and hasten to receive the Fast pleasantly and willingly, and to observe it, so that we might attain unto that which Adam lost—that is, Deification (*Theosis*)—through this Fast, weeping fervently, fasting, and humbling ourselves until God visit us. For without these means it is not easy to obtain that which we lost.

It is also proper to know that this Holy and Great Forty Days is the tithe of the whole year.[^473] For from seven weeks, taking away Saturdays and Sundays, there remain thirty-five days; adding thereto unto the Fast Holy Saturday and the half of the radiant Night, it becomes thirty-six and a half days, which is the tenth part of the three hundred and sixty-five days in the year. For because out of sloth we do not wish always to fast and to empty ourselves of evils, the Apostles and divine Fathers handed down this Forty Days as a kind of summer or harvest for souls. So that whatsoever improper and base things we have committed throughout the whole year, we might now, being contrite, and through fasting humbling and abasing ourselves, efface and wash away. Which also we ought to observe with great diligence: and not this one only, but also the other three—I mean the Fast of the Holy Apostles, of the All-Pure Mother of God [Dormition], and of the Nativity of Christ, besides this Holy Forty Days. For according unto the four seasons of the year, the divine Fathers also instituted and handed down the Fasts.

Yet especially and above all ought we to honor the Forty Days, because of the Holy Passions, and because Christ fasted this and was glorified.[^474] And Moses, having fasted forty days, received the Law.[^475] And Elijah likewise,[^476] and Daniel,[^477] and as many as were proven and praised before God. And what a good thing fasting is, Adam shows and teaches us from the contrary thing, that is, intemperance. For this reason, therefore, Adam's expulsion from Paradise was conceived and appointed in this place by the Holy Fathers.

By Thine ineffable compassion, O Christ our God, make us worthy of the delight of Paradise, and have mercy upon us, as Thou art alone good and the Lover of mankind. Unto Whom belong honor and glory, unto ages of ages. Amen.

*Ode 7. Heirmos: "The Angel made the furnace pour down dew for the holy Children, but the command of God consumed the Chaldeans, persuading the tyrant to cry: 'Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!'" (Dan. 3:49–50).*

*Troparion 1:* O Lord Who rulest over all the ages and didst fashion me by Thy will: disdain me not, O God, whom the deceitful serpent once envied and who provoked Thee to anger, O Savior, but call me back!

*Troparion 2:* Clothed in shameful garments—woe is me!—instead of radiant raiment, I bewail my perdition, O Savior, and in faith cry unto Thee: O Good One, disdain me not, O God, but call me back!

*Glory... Troparion:* With a wound the all-crafty serpent craftily wounded all my soul, and caused me to be driven out from the delight of Paradise. But O compassionate Savior, disdain me not as God, but call me back!

*Both now... Theotokion:* Receive my slavish supplication, O all-immaculate Lady, through thine intercession, and grant me forgiveness of transgressions, O pure one, as I cry aloud earnestly in lamentations: Disdain me not, O Good Lady, but call me back!

*Katavasia. Heirmos: "The Angel made the furnace pour down dew for the holy Children, but the command of God consumed the Chaldeans, persuading the tyrant to cry: 'Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!'" (Dan. 3:49–50).*

*Ode 8. Heirmos: "From the flame Thou didst pour forth dew for the holy ones, and the sacrifice of the righteous one Thou didst consume with water; for Thou doest all things, O Christ, whatsoever Thou willest: Thee do we exalt supremely unto all ages!" (Dan. 3:49–50; 3 Kgs. 18:38).*

*Troparion 1:* With manifold gifts of old didst Thou honor the work of Thy hands, O only Lover of mankind; but the fierce serpent deceived him by envy—woe is me!—stripping him of the hoped-for good things!

*Troparion 2:* Why didst thou listen unto bitter counsel? And why wast thou disobedient unto the divine command? Woe is me, humble soul that hast grieved God, who wast appointed to glorify Him unceasingly with the Angels!

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord.*

*Troparion 3:* Having been mistress over reptiles and beasts, how didst thou converse with the soul-corrupting reptile? Receiving the deceiver as an upright counselor! O thy deception, O my all-passionate soul!

*Both now... Theotokion:* As the radiant dwelling of God's incarnation do I hymn thee, O Mary graced of God; therefore enlighten me who am grievously darkened by passions with the light of mercy, O Hope of the hopeless!

*We praise, we bless, and we worship the Lord.*

*Katavasia. Heirmos: "From the flame Thou didst pour forth dew for the holy ones, and the sacrifice of the righteous one Thou didst consume with water; for Thou doest all things, O Christ, whatsoever Thou willest: Thee do we exalt supremely unto all ages!" (Dan. 3:49–50; 3 Kgs. 18:38).*

*Ode 9. Heirmos: "It is not possible for men to see God, upon Whom the ranks of Angels dare not gaze; but through thee, O all-pure one, the incarnate Word was seen of men, Whom magnifying, together with the heavenly hosts we call thee blessed!" (1 Tim. 6:16; Luke 1:28).*

*Troparion 1:* Sweet seemed unto me the fruit of knowledge in Eden, when I took my fill of food; but bitter gall was the end thereof! Woe is me, passionate soul: how intemperance has made thee estranged from the food of Paradise! (Gen. 2:17; 3:6).

*Troparion 2:* O God of all, Lord of mercy, look compassionately upon my lowliness, and settle me not far from divine Eden, so that beholding the beauty from whence I fell, I may strive with tears to receive again that which I lost!

*Troparion 3:* I sob, groan, and weep, beholding the Cherubim commanded to guard the entrance of Eden with the flaming sword (Gen. 3:24), inaccessible—woe is me!—unto all transgressors, unless Thou, O Savior, make it unobstructed for me!

*Glory... Troparion:* I dare to rely upon the multitude of Thy mercy, O Christ Savior, and upon the Blood from Thy divine side, wherewith Thou didst sanctify human nature (John 19:34); and Thou didst open the gates of Paradise, O Good One, unto those who serve Thee, which were previously locked unto Adam!

*Both now... Theotokion:* O impassable, spiritual gate of life, Virgin Mother of God unwedded: the gates of Paradise which were previously locked unto me, open by thy prayers, that after God I may glorify thee, my helper and mighty refuge!

*Katavasia. Heirmos: "It is not possible for men to see God, upon Whom the ranks of Angels dare not gaze; but through thee, O all-pure one, the incarnate Word was seen of men, Whom magnifying, together with the heavenly hosts we call thee blessed!" (1 Tim. 6:16; Luke 1:28).*

*Resurrectional Exapostilarion. And the present [Exapostilarion]. Tone 2, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Же́ны внꙋ́шите):*[^478]

*Glory...:* I transgressed Thy commandment, O Lord, wretched that I am, and being stripped of Thy glory, I was filled with shame—woe is me!—and cast down from the delight of Paradise, O Compassionate One. O Merciful One, have mercy upon him who has justly been deprived of Thy grace!

*Both now... Another [Exapostilarion], Tone 2, Podoben: "Let us assemble with the disciples" (Съ ᲂу҆чн҃ки и҆де́мъ):*[^479] Him who was cast out of Paradise of old, O Lord, because of the eating of the tree, Thou didst lead back again by Thy Cross and Passion, O my Savior and God. Therefore strengthen us to accomplish the Fast in holy manner, and to worship Thy divine Resurrection, the saving Pascha, through the prayers of her who bore Thee!

*At the Praises: Resurrectional Stichera, 4; and Anatolian, 1; and of the Cheesefare Triodion, 3, Idiomela, Tone 5:*[^480]

*Verse: Let every breath praise the Lord (Ps. 150:6).*

*Sticheron 1:* "Woe is me!" Adam cried out with weeping: "for the serpent and the woman have cast me out from divine boldness, and the eating of the tree has estranged me from the delight of Paradise! Woe is me! I can no longer bear reproach: I who was once king of all earthly creations of God am now shown forth a captive by one lawless counsel! And I who was once clothed in the glory of immortality, like one mortal now pitiably wear the coat of mortality! Woe is me! Whom shall I make a helper in my weeping? But do Thou, Lover of mankind, Who didst fashion me from the earth, clothing Thyself in tenderheartedness, deliver me from the bondage of the enemy and save me!" (Gen. 3:1–6, 21).

*Verse: I will give thanks unto Thee, O Lord, with my whole heart (Ps. 9:1).*

*Sticheron 2:* The stadium of virtues is opened! Ye who desire to compete, enter in, girding yourselves with the good contest of the Fast; for those who strive lawfully are justly crowned! And taking up the whole armor of the Cross, let us oppose the enemy, holding faith as an unbreakable wall, and prayer as a breastplate, and almsgiving as a helmet, and instead of a sword, Fasting, which cuts off all malice from the heart! (2 Tim. 2:5; Eph. 6:11–17). He that does these things shall receive the true crown from Christ the King of all on the day of judgment!

*Verse: I will be glad and rejoice in Thee (Ps. 9:2). Tone 6:*

*Sticheron 3:* Adam was driven out of Paradise, having partaken of food as a disobedient one; Moses became a beholder of God, having cleansed the eyes of his soul through Fasting (Gen. 3:23–24; Exod. 24:18). Therefore, desiring to be inhabitants of Paradise, let us turn away from unprofitable food, and loving to behold God, let us fast the Mosaic four decades of days (Exod. 34:28), purely persevering in prayer and supplication! Let us quell the passions of the soul, let us drive away the lusts of the belly, and lightened let us pass over unto the upward journey, where the angelic choirs with unceasing voices hymn the undivided Trinity, to behold the incomprehensible beauty of the Master! There account us worthy, O Son of God, Giver of life, who hope in Thee, to rejoice together with the angelic hosts, through the prayers of Thy Mother who bore Thee, O Christ, and of the Apostles, the Martyrs, and the Venerable![^481]

*Instead of the Gospel Sticheron, Idiomelon, Tone 6. Verse:*[^482]

*Arise, O Lord my God, let Thy hand be lifted up (Ps. 9:32 [LXX]).*

*Doxasticon / Idiomelon:* The time has arrived, the beginning of spiritual contests...

...victory over the demons, the full armor of abstinence, the angelic splendor, boldness toward God! For through this Moses became a conversationalist with the Creator, and invisibly received a voice in his hearing (Exod. 33:11; 34:28). Therefore, O Lord, account us also worthy to worship Thy Sufferings and holy Resurrection, as Thou art the Lover of mankind!

*Glory: the same sticheron. Both now:*
*Thou art most blessed, O Virgin Mother of God... Great Doxology. After the Trisagion, the Resurrectional Troparion only. And the Dismissal. Exit into the narthex. Glory... Both now... The Gospel Sticheron. And the Instruction of the Studite. And the reading. And the final dismissal.*[^483]

*At the Hours: Resurrectional Troparion. After the Trisagion, Kontakion from the Triodion.*

*AT THE LITURGY. The Beatitudes in order, Tone of the Oktoechos, 6; and in the Triodion from the Canon, Ode 6, 4. Prokeimenon of the Epistle, Tone 8:* Pray and make vows unto the Lord our God *(Ps. 75:12).* *Verse:* In Judea is God known; His name is great in Israel *(Ps. 75:2).* *Epistle to the Romans, pericope 121:* Brethren, now is our salvation nearer... *(Rom. 13:11–14:4).* *Alleluia, Tone 6. Verse:* It is good to give thanks unto the Lord, and to sing praises unto Thy name, O Most High *(Ps. 91:2).* *Verse:* To proclaim Thy mercy in the morning, and Thy truth every night *(Ps. 91:3).* *Gospel of Matthew, pericope 17:* The Lord said: If ye forgive men their trespasses... *(Matt. 6:14–21).* *Communion Hymn:* Praise the Lord from the heavens *(Ps. 148:1).* *Another:* Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous *(Ps. 32:1).* *Alleluia. Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom.*[^484]

*It should be known that from this Cheesefare Sunday until the Exaltation of the Precious Cross on every Sunday throughout the whole summer, at Matins they omit the Polyeleos on regular Sunday Vigils; it is chanted only at the Vigils of the Master's Feasts and of great Saints on Sunday Vigils.*[^485]

*ON THE SAME CHEESEFARE SUNDAY AT VESPERS,*
*After the Initial Psalm,*
*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 10 verses, beginning with:* Bring my soul out of prison *(Ps. 141:8).*
*And we sing 4 penitential stichera of the Oktoechos in the Tone of the week (that is, the two sung in the evening at the Aposticha and the two at morning Aposticha). (Likewise on the other Sundays, from this one until the Fifth of the Holy Fast, at "Lord, I have cried" we sing in the Tone that is in order in the Oktoechos). And in the Triodion, 3. And in the Menaion, 3.*[^486]

*Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
*The composition of Lord Joseph:*[^487]

*Sticheron 1:* Let us all make haste to humble the flesh through abstinence as we traverse the divine stadium of the blameless Fast! With prayers and tears let us seek the Lord Who saves us, and let us make a total oblivion of malice, crying out: "We have sinned against Thee, O Savior, as the Ninevites of old, O Christ the King (Jonah 3:5–10); and make us partakers of the Heavenly Kingdom, O tenderhearted One!"

*Sticheron 2:* I lament myself, considering my deeds, O Lord, worthy of all torment! For lo, having despised Thine honorable commandments, O Savior, I spend my life prodigally. Therefore I pray: having cleansed me with the rains of repentance through fasting and supplication, as the only merciful One enlighten me, and turn not in disgust away from me, O Benefactor of all and most good!

*Another, of Lord Theodore the Studite. Tone the same, Podoben: "Surpassing understanding" (Па́че [ᲂу҆ма̀]):*[^488]

*Sticheron 3:* Let us begin the season of the Fast with brightness, submitting ourselves unto spiritual contests! Let us wash the soul, let us cleanse the body; let us fast from every passion as from foods, delighting in the virtues of the Spirit, in which persevering with love, may we all be accounted worthy to behold the all-honorable Passion of Christ God and the Holy Pascha, rejoicing spiritually!

*Then in the Menaion to the Saint, 3 stichera. If it has an Idiomelon, Glory: If not, Glory... Both now... Theotokion in the same Tone of the Menaion.*

*Entrance: the Priest vests in a purple phelonion, with censer, and the taper-bearer.*[^489]

**Priest:** *Wisdom! Stand upright!*

*Then: O Joyous Light (Phos Hilaron).*

*Great Prokeimenon, Tone 8:* Turn not away Thy face...

...from Thy servant, for I am in trouble; speedily hear me; give heed unto my soul, and deliver it *(Ps. 68:18–19).*
*Verse:* Let Thy salvation, O God, uphold me *(Ps. 68:30).*
*Verse:* Let the poor see and be glad *(Ps. 68:33).*
*And again with the highest voice:* Turn not away Thy face...

*It should be known that on all Sundays of the Holy Forty-Day Fast, in the evening the Entrance takes place for the sake of the Great Prokeimenon. Ektene: "Have mercy on us, O God..." "Vouchsafe, O Lord": 3 Metanias. And throughout the whole month at evening from the beginning we do not make metanias, but beginning from "Vouchsafe, O Lord" we make 3 prostrations. Then the Ektene: "Let us complete..." After the exclamation, both choirs having assembled together chant:*[^490]

*At the Aposticha, Idiomelon Sticheron, Tone 4:*

*Sticheron 1:* Thy grace has shone forth, O Lord, the illumination of our souls has shone forth! Lo, the acceptable time, lo, the time of repentance (2 Cor. 6:2)! Let us cast off the works of darkness and put on the armor of light (Rom. 13:12), so that having crossed over the great abyss of the Fast, we may behold the three-day Resurrection of our Lord and Savior Jesus Christ, Who saves our souls!

*Verse: Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes, O Thou that dwellest in heaven (Ps. 122:1).*
*And again the same Sticheron.*

*Verse: Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us (Ps. 122:3). Then the Martyr's Troparion:*

*Sticheron 2:* O Christ God, Who art glorified in the memories of Thy saints, being implored by them, send down upon us great mercy!

*Glory: Idiomelon, if the Menaion has one. Both now: Theotokion according to the Tone of the Menaion. But if it has none, Glory... Both now... Theotokion, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́бля въ мꙋ́ченицѣхъ):*[^491]

*Theotokion:* The angelic ranks glorify thee, O Mother of God; for thou, all-pure one, didst bear God, Who is co-eternal with the Father and the Spirit, and Who by His will established the angelic armies from nothingness. Implore Him, O all-pure one, to save from corruption and to enlighten the souls of the Orthodox who hymn thee!

*Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart. Trisagion. 3 Metanias. Then Troparia, Tone 4:*
*Rejoice, O Mother of God and Virgin...*

*O Mother of God and Virgin, rejoice, Mary full of grace, the Lord is with thee; blessed art thou among women, and blessed is the fruit of thy womb, for thou hast borne the Savior of our souls! And 1 prostration.*
*Glory: O Baptist of Christ, remember us all, that we may be delivered from our iniquities; for unto thee grace was given to pray for us. And one prostration.*
*Both now: Intercede for us, holy Apostles and all saints, that we may be delivered from dangers and sorrows; for we have acquired you as fervent intercessors before the Savior. And 1 prostration.*
*Then: Beneath thy tender compassion we flee, O Mother of God; despise not our supplications in adversity, but deliver us from dangers, O only blessed one!*[^492]

*Without prostration and without singing: Lord, have mercy (40 times), in a soft and quiet voice, and all with uniformity. Then: Lord, bless! The Priest exclaims:*

**Priest:** *He that IS blessed is Christ our God, always, now and ever, and unto ages of ages.*

**Reader:** *Amen. And the Prayer:*
*O Heavenly King, strengthen our faithful sovereigns, establish the faith, subdue the nations, pacify the world, well preserve this holy Monastery, settle our departed fathers and brethren in the Tabernacles of the Righteous, and receive us in repentance and confession, as Thou art good and the Lover of mankind! And we make 3 great metanias.*[^493]

*Then lifting up our hands, we stand, praying in our own mind and saying within ourselves the Prayer of Venerable Ephrem the Syrian:*[^494]
*O Lord and Master of my life, drive away from me the spirit of sloth, despair, lust of power, and idle talk! One prostration.*
*But grant the spirit of chastity, humility, patience, and love unto Thy servant! One prostration.*
*Yea, O Lord and King, grant me to see my own transgressions, and not to judge my brother; for blessed art Thou unto ages of ages. Amen. 1 prostration.*

*And we venerate the Precious Cross which each of us wears, or the holy and honorable icons at our places where we stand according to custom. Then the Priest: Glory to Thee, O Christ God, our hope, glory to Thee! And there takes place the prayer of the Priest, and the complete Dismissal.*[^495]

*SMALL COMPLINE according to custom on account of the labor of the All-Night Vigil, only with Metanias and Lenten Prostrations. At "Glory to God in the highest," 3 metanias. Canon to the Mother of God. "It is truly meet." Trisagion, 3 metanias. And the customary Troparia. "Lord, have mercy" (40 times). "Thou Who at all times and every hour..." The Priest: "O God, be gracious unto us..." 3 great prostrations, with the Prayer: "O Lord and Master of my life..." Then: "O undefiled, unstained, uncorrupted..." Trisagion, 3 prostrations. "Lord, have mercy" (12 times). Prayer: "And grant unto us, O Master..." And the Dismissal.*[^496]

*And in the same manner as the Service of Vespers and Compline of this Sunday was indicated (behold!), so let it be done on the remaining ones also, even unto the Fifth Sunday of the holy month [of the Fast], changing certain parts, concerning which it will be noted in their proper places.*

*It should be known: If it should happen that a brother of ours departs unto God during the days of the Holy Fast, during the week no third-day memorial is made for him until Friday evening; for then a Panikhida is celebrated for him. Likewise also on Saturday the Liturgy is offered for him. And on the next Saturday his ninth-day memorial is held, whether the number of days falls then or not. But his fortieth-day memorial takes place whenever the number of his days is fulfilled. But the oblation, that is, the Prosphora and commemorations for him, begin from New Sunday after Pascha [Thomas Sunday], even unto the completion of 40 days.*[^497]

*Concerning the Feast of the Meeting of the Lord, Chapter 49:*[^498]
*If it happens that the Feast of the Meeting falls on Monday of the First Week of the Fast (for it never occurs later according to the perpetually unalterable Paschalion):*[^499]
*On Cheesefare Sunday Evening: At Small Vespers, all the Stichera are of the Feast, and the Troparion; only at the Aposticha, the Idiomelon of the day twice, and of the Martyrs.*
*AT GREAT VESPERS: After the customary Psalm, we sing:* Blessed is the man *(Ps. 1), the first "Glory..." Stichera on 10: 2 Idiomela from the Triodion for the day, 2; and Prosomoia, 3; and of the Feast, 5. Glory... Both now... of the Feast. Entrance. Great Prokeimenon:* Turn not away Thy face... *with its verses. And the Readings of the Feast. At the Litiya and at the Aposticha, of the Feast. At the Blessing of Loaves, Troparion of the Feast, 3 times; and the customary order, and the Reading of the Feast.*
*AT MATINS: At "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Feast, 3 times. And we recite 3 Kathismata. After the 1st Kathisma: both Sessional Hymns in the Triodion, and the Reading of the Feast. After the 2nd Kathisma and after the 3rd: Sessional Hymns of the Feast, and the Reading of the Feast. Polyeleos. Megalynarion. And the Reading of the Feast.*
*CANON of the Feast with Heirmoi on 6; and the Triodion on 8; and after the Heirmos in the Triodion. But the remaining Odes of the Feast on 14, with the Heirmos; Katavasia of the Feast. After Ode 3, Sessional Hymn of the Feast, 2 times; and the Reading of the Feast. After Ode 6, Kontakion and Ikos of the Feast; and the Prologue. And the 4th Ode and the other we sing as also the 1st with the Triodion. We do not sing "More honorable than the Cherubim." Exapostilarion 3 times. At the Praises, all of the Feast. At the Aposticha, Idiomelon of the day twice, and of the Martyrs. Glory... Both now... of the Feast. After "Our Father," Troparion of the Feast. Litany. 3 great prostrations, and the Dismissal. And the First Hour: Troparion and Kontakion of the Feast; and at the end 3 great prostrations, and the Dismissal, and the customary exit. Likewise on the 3rd day, an exit outside the Monastery, and procession...*

*...of the Monastery. We chant the Third Hour with the Kathisma; Troparion and Kontakion of the Feast; and the Paroimia at the Sixth Hour of the day; and the Reading of the Feast. At the Ninth Hour, we chant the Ninth Hour with Kathisma; and the Reading from the Catechesis of the Studite. The Beatitudes without singing, and "Remember us, O Lord," we say simply. After "Our Father," Kontakion of the Feast; "Lord, have mercy" (40 times); and 3 prostrations, and the Dismissal. We chant Vespers without Kathisma and without prostrations.*
*Stichera on 10: 2 Idiomela of the Triodion for the day, 2; and Prosomoia, 3; and of the Feast, 3; and of Saint Symeon, 3. And we say the 3rd Sticheron of the Saint with the addition, Verse:* Thou art a priest forever after the order of Melchizedek *(Ps. 109:4). Glory... Both now... of the Feast. Entrance with the Gospel. Prokeimenon and Reading of the day. And "Let my prayer be set forth." And the Little Diaconal Litany. Troparion. Glory... Both now... Kontakion of the Feast in the Tone; and the Trisagion. Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, Gospel, and Communion Hymn of the Feast; and the Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom.*
*At the Trapeza: we eat dry food and drink wine; but we do not eat fish.*
*On that same day, at Compline we chant according to custom from the Canon of Andrew the appointed portion, but we do not make prostrations; only at "Glory to God in the highest," and at the Trisagion, 3 great prostrations each; and at "More honorable than the Cherubim," 3 with the Prayer, and 12 other [metanias]; and at the final Trisagion, 3 prostrations, and the Dismissal.*[^500]

*If it happens that the Finding of the Head of the Forerunner falls on Monday of the First Week of the Fast, the feast of the Forerunner is sung entirely on Cheesefare Sunday, outside the church of the dedication; but if it is the church of the patron, it is chanted here, just as for the Meeting, only with the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts.*[^501]

---

### Notes

[^38]: Title formula of the Triodion: *Triodion, that is, the Three-Ode Canon* (*ТРІѠ́ДІѠНЪ си́есть: ТРІПѢ́СНЕЦЪ*). So named because the weekday canons of Great Lent contain only three odes (ordinarily Odes 8 and 9 plus one preceding ode), rather than the customary eight or nine odes found in festal and Sunday canons.
[^39]: Great Vespers hymnographic structure: The typikon combines the Sunday Resurrectional cycle of the Octoechos with the specialized penitential hymnography of the Triodion, signaling the entry into the Pre-Lenten liturgical period.
[^40]: Luke 18:14; Matthew 23:12: "For whosoever exalts himself shall be abased; and he that humbls himself shall be exalted."
[^41]: Scriptural paradigms of tears: King Hezekiah delivered from death (4 Kings 20:1–6 [2 Kings 20 MT]; Isaiah 38:1–8), and the repentant sinful woman who washed Christ's feet with her tears (Luke 7:36–50).
[^42]: Mark's Chapters (*Сѵ́главъ Ма́рка І҆еромона́ха*): Liturgical rubrics attributed to Mark, Hieromonk of the Monastery of Saint Sabas near Jerusalem, codified across medieval Byzantine and Slavic Typika to resolve calendar coincidences when movable and immovable feasts intersect.
[^43]: Chapter 42 of the Typikon: Governs the coincidence of the Feast of the Meeting of the Lord (February 2) and its associated Forefeast and Afterfeast with the Pre-Lenten Sundays (Publican and Pharisee, Prodigal Son, Meatfare, Cheesefare).
[^44]: Festal Matins rubrics: Prescribes the Polyeleos and the Sunday Resurrectional Evlogitaria (*The Angelic Council*) when the Great Feast of the Meeting intersects with the Sunday service, showing the fusion of festal and dominical hymnography.
[^45]: Psalm 97:2 (LXX Ps 97:2): Appointed as the festal Entrance Verse (Introit) for the Meeting of the Lord.
[^46]: Patristic Homilies from the *Tolkovoye Yevangeliye* (Commentary Gospel): Appoints the classical homilies of St. John Chrysostom and St. Cyril of Alexandria on the Parable of the Publican and the Pharisee to be read at Sunday Matins.
[^47]: The Triodion Penitential Sticheron (*Мно́жество съдѣ́ѧнныхъ мои́хъ ѕѡ́лъ*): The 1640 Kyivan edition preserves this ancient post-Gospel sticheron based on Davidic compunction, which in later Synodal editions is grouped with *Open unto me the doors of repentance, O Giver of Life*.
[^48]: St. George of Nicomedia (*Творе́нїе Геѡ́ргїа*): Metropolitan George of Nicomedia (9th century), celebrated hymnographer who composed the triodia and tetraodia of the Pre-Lenten and Lenten seasons in the Byzantine rite.
[^49]: Katavasia of Ode 1: *I shall open my mouth* (*Ѡ҆тве́рзꙋ ꙋ҆ста̀ моѧ̑*), theirmos of the Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos (Tone 4), standardly appointed during the Pre-Lenten period.
[^50]: Ode 3 Heirmos: *There is none holy as Thou, O Lord my God* (*Нѣ́сть ст҃ъ, ꙗ҆́коже Ты̀, Гдⷵи Бж҃е мо́й*), based upon the Third Biblical Canticle (1 Kings 2:2 [1 Sam 2:2 MT], the Song of Hannah).
[^51]: Podoben incipit: *Speedily anticipate* (*Ско́рѡ предварѝ* / Greek: *Тахὺ προκατάлаβε*). A standard Tone 4 prosomoion model hymn frequently appointed for sessional hymns, sedalia, and apolytikia.
[^52]: Ode 4 Heirmos: *Christ is my power* (*Хрⷭто́съ мнѣ̀ си́ла* / *Christos mou dynamis*). Based upon the Fourth Biblical Canticle, the Prayer of the Prophet Habakkuk (Hab. 3:2–19).
[^53]: Philippians 2:7: "But made Himself of no reputation, and took upon Him the form of a servant, and was made in the likeness of men."
[^54]: James 4:6; 1 Peter 5:5; Proverbs 3:34: "God resists the proud, but gives grace unto the humble."
[^55]: The washing of the disciples' feet: John 13:4–15, Christ's supreme manifestation of kenotic humility, setting a physical paradigm for Christian discipleship.
[^56]: Ode 5 Heirmos: *By Thy divine light* (*Бж҃їимъ свѣ́томъ* / *Tō theio phengei sou*). Based upon the Fifth Biblical Canticle, the Prayer of Isaiah (Isa. 26:9–20).
[^57]: Classical allusion: The Lydian chariot (*Лѵді́йскїѧ колесни́ца* / *Lydion harma*). An ancient proverbial Greek metaphor for swift racing and athletic velocity, adapted into Byzantine hymnography to illustrate that the humble Publican outstripped the fast-moving, virtuous Pharisee.
[^58]: Psalm 109:2 (LXX Ps 109:2 / MT Ps 110:2): "The Lord shall send the rod of Thy power out of Zion: rule Thou in the midst of Thine enemies."
[^59]: Ode 6 Heirmos: *The sea of life* (*Жите́йскагѡ мо́рѧ* / *Tou biou tēn thalassan*). Based upon the Sixth Biblical Canticle, the Prayer of Jonah from the belly of the sea monster (Jonah 2:3–10).
[^60]: Podoben incipit: *Seeking the highest* (*Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀* / *Ta anō zētōn*). Tone 2 melodic model appointed for the Kontakion and Ikos.
[^61]: Nikephoros Kallistos Xanthopoulos (c. 1256–1335): Eminent Byzantine church historian, poet, and hymnographer of Constantinople who composed the definitive verse and prose Synaxaria for the movable cycle of the Triodion and Pentecostarion. In this 1640 Kyiv edition, the Lavra press translated the Greek synaxaria into elevated Early Modern Ruthenian (*prosta mova*) prose for the instruction of clergy and faithful.
[^62]: St. Cosmas the Melodist, Bishop of Maiuma (d. c. 760): Renowned 8th-century Byzantine hymnographer, foster-brother of St. John of Damascus, who composed the original three-ode canons (triodia) for Great and Holy Week.
[^63]: St. Theodore the Studite (759–826) and his brother St. Joseph the Studite, Archbishop of Thessalonica (d. 825): Byzantine monastic reformers and hymnographers who systematically completed the hymnographic corpus of the Lenten Triodion at the Studios Monastery in Constantinople.
[^64]: Emperor Leo VI the Wise (866–912): Byzantine Emperor, scholar, and hymnographer who authored liturgical poetry, including the Four-Ode Canon for Great and Holy Saturday.
[^65]: Bishop Mark of Hydrus (Otranto, 8th–9th century): A monk of the Lavra of Saint Sabas near Jerusalem and subsequently Bishop of Hydrus (Otranto in Apulia, Southern Italy), who completed the full nine-ode canons of Holy Saturday and other services of the Triodion.
[^66]: Marginal gloss *Catachrestice* (*Катахрисикѡ̃с* / *catachresis*): A rhetorical figure denoting an extended or improper use of a term; here explaining that the title *Triodion* ("Three-Ode Book") is applied by extension to the entire volume, even though Sunday and festal services contain full eight- or nine-ode canons.
[^67]: Military pedagogy in Mohylan Ruthenian prose: Metropolitan Petro Mohyla and the Kyiv-Pechersk scholars employ a vivid homiletic comparison between Cossack hetmans briefing their regiments before military engagement and the Church Fathers preparing Christians with spiritual armor for the ascetic warfare of Great Lent.
[^68]: Isaiah 14:12: "How art thou fallen from heaven, O Lucifer, son of the morning! how art thou cut down to the ground, which didst weaken the nations!"
[^69]: James 4:6; 1 Peter 5:5; Proverbs 3:34.
[^70]: Luke 18:14: "I tell you, this man went down to his house justified rather than the other: for every one that exalts himself shall be abased; and he that humbls himself shall be exalted."
[^71]: Publican (*мытникъ*): A tax-farmer or collector of state customs and road tolls (*myto*), infamous in the ancient and early modern world for extortion and financial injustice.
[^72]: Acts 23:8: "For the Sadducees say that there is no resurrection, neither angel, nor spirit: but the Pharisees confess both." The Kyivan synaxarion notes the etymology of Pharisee from the Hebrew *perushim* ("separated / set apart") and Sadducee from the priest Zadok (*Tsedek*, "righteousness").
[^73]: Ode 7 Heirmos: *The God-fashioned furnace* (*Богозда́ннꙋю ѹ҆́бѡ пе́щь*). Based upon the Seventh Biblical Canticle, the Song of the Three Holy Children in Babylon (Dan. 3:26–56).
[^74]: Romans 12:16: "Mind not high things, but condescend to men of low estate. Be not wise in your own conceits."
[^75]: Genesis 28:12: The Vision of Jacob's Ladder ascending from earth to heaven, universally interpreted in Byzantine and Slavic hymnography as a type and figure of the Mother of God uniting heaven and earth through the Incarnation.
[^76]: Ode 8 Heirmos: *From the flame Thou didst pour out dew* (*И҆з̾ пла́мени ст҃ы̑мъ ро́сꙋ и҆сточи́лъ є҆сѝ* / *Ek phlogos tois hosiois drosos epēgasas*). Based upon the Eighth Biblical Canticle, the Praise of the Three Holy Children (Dan. 3:57–88).
[^77]: Ode 9 Heirmos: *It is not possible for men to see God* (*Бг҃а чл҃кѡмъ невозмо́жно ви́дѣти* / *Theon anthrōpois idein adynaton*). Based upon the Ninth Biblical Canticle, the Magnificat and Benedictus (Luke 1:46–55, 68–79).
[^78]: Matthew 5:3: "Blessed are the poor in spirit: for theirs is the kingdom of heaven."
[^79]: Podoben incipit: *Give ear, ye women* (*Же́ны ѹ҆слы́шите* / Greek: *Gynaikes akoutisthēte*). Tone 2 exapostilarion model melody.
[^80]: Psalm 9:33 (LXX Ps 9:33 / MT Ps 10:12): "Arise, O Lord God, let Thy hand be lifted up: forget not the poor." Appointed as a psalm verse for the Stichera at the Praises.
[^81]: Psalm 9:2 (LXX Ps 9:2): "I will confess Thee, O Lord, with my whole heart; I will recount all Thy marvelous works."
[^82]: Internal folio cross-reference: *Зрѝ лѝ, б҃* ("See fol. 2", physical leaf p16), referring back to the first two Lord, I have cried stichera (*Let us not pray like the Pharisee* and *A Pharisee, overcome by vainglory*), which are chanted again at the Praises without repetition.
[^83]: The morning Gospel Stichera (Eothina) are normally chanted at the Praises at Sunday Matins. In Studite and early Kyivan practice, during the Triodion and Pentecostarion seasons they are additionally appointed to be chanted during the Litiya in the narthex at Glory... Both now...
[^84]: Domestic dialect (*дїале́ктомъ дома́шнимъ*): Petro Mohyla explicitly prescribes reading the patristic catecheses in the spoken Early Modern Ruthenian vernacular (*prosta mova*), rather than Church Slavonic, so that the monastics and laity might comprehend the ascetical instructions with total clarity.
[^85]: St. Theodore the Studite (759–826), Abbot of the Studios Monastery in Constantinople. His Great and Small Catecheses (*Ѡглаше́нїа*) formed an indispensable part of the monastic Lenten disciplina.
[^86]: Common Troparion of a monastic Father and Teacher of the Church, Tone 8: *Правосла́вїѧ наста́вниче* ("Instructor of Orthodoxy, teacher of piety and purity, light of the universe, divinely-inspired ornament of monastics, all-wise Theodore: by thy teachings thou hast enlightened all, O lyre of the Spirit; entreat Christ God that our souls may be saved").
[^87]: Liturgy lections for the Sunday of the Publican and the Pharisee: Epistle: 2 Timothy 3:10–15 (Pericope 296); Gospel: Luke 18:10–14 (Pericope 89). Communion Hymn: Psalm 148:1 (LXX Ps. 148:1).
[^88]: Artzivourion (*А҆рцивꙋ́рїевъ*, Armenian *Arachavorats*): A traditional pre-Lenten fast of the Armenian Apostolic Church occurring ten weeks before Easter. Byzantine and Kyivan polemicists vigorously rejected this fast, interpreting it as heretical and instituting a complete dispensation from fasting (fast-free week) for Orthodox Christians in direct opposition to it.
[^89]: The monastic Typikon prescribes partaking of dairy and eggs (*сыръ и҆ ꙗ҆йца̀*) even on Wednesday and Friday at the ninth hour during this week, while the laity eat meat on all days.
[^90]: Meatfare Saturday (Saturday of the Souls / Ancestral Saturday) is dedicated to the universal commemoration of all departed Orthodox Christians. If the Feast of the Meeting of the Lord (February 2) falls on this day, the services must be combined or transferred.
[^91]: Cemetery chapel / church (*гробница*): Kyivan Lavra custom possessed dedicated cemetery sanctuaries where parastases and memorial liturgies could be chanted without displacing festal services in the main Katholikon.
[^92]: Internal folio cross-reference: *ꙗ҆́коже ли́стъ, а҃. ѹ҆каза́ся* ("as indicated on folio 1", physical leaf p15), referring to the order of the All-Night Vigil set forth at the opening of the Triodion.
[^93]: Internal folio cross-reference: *Ли́стъ, г҃.* ("folio 3", physical leaf p17), referring to the detailed rubrical table for the concurrence of the Meeting of the Lord with pre-Lenten Sundays and Saturdays.
[^94]: Commentary Gospel (*Толковое Евангеліе*): The Gospel text accompanied by the patristic commentaries of St. Theophylact of Ohrid or St. John Chrysostom, appointed for monastic refectory and liturgical lectern reading.
[^95]: Luke 15:11–32 (The Parable of the Prodigal Son).
[^96]: St. John Chrysostom, *Homily on the Prodigal Son* (*Слово о блудном сынѣ*), beginning: *Възлю́бленнїи послꙋ́шайте само́гѡ Хрⷭта́* ("Beloved, listen to Christ Himself").
[^97]: Polyeleos (*Мнѡмлⷭтиво*): Psalms 134 and 135 (*Praise ye the name of the Lord* and *Confess unto the Lord, for He is good, for His mercy endures forever*).
[^98]: Psalm 136 (LXX Ps. 136:1–9 / MT Ps. 137:1–9): *By the rivers of Babylon* (*На рѣ́цѣ Вавѷлѡ́нстѣй*), appointed as a third psalm of the Polyeleos on the three Pre-Lenten Sundays (Prodigal Son, Meatfare, Cheesefare) with an elaborate red-letter Alleluia (*съ А҆ллилꙋ́їа кра́сною*).
[^99]: St. Joseph the Hymnographer (c. 816–886), prolific Byzantine hymnographer who composed the Matins canons of the Lenten Triodion for the pre-Lenten Sundays and weekdays.
[^100]: Ode 1 Heirmos: *Moses' song...* (*Мосѵсе́йскꙋю пѣ́снь*). Based upon the First Biblical Canticle, the Song of Moses at the Red Sea (Exod. 15:1–19).
[^101]: Exodus 15:1–2: "Then sang Moses and the children of Israel this song unto the Lord... The Lord is my strength and song, and He is become my salvation: He is my God, and I will prepare Him an habitation."
[^102]: Tone 4 Heirmos incipit: *I shall open my mouth* (*Ѿве́рзꙋ ѹ҆ста̀ моя̀* / Greek: *Anoixō to stoma mou*), the standard model melody for Ode 1 of the Canon to the Theotokos.
[^103]: Ode 3 Heirmos: *My barren mind...* (*Непло́днаго ми ѹ҆ма̀* / Greek: *Steirōthenta mou ton noun*). Based upon the Third Biblical Canticle, the Prayer of Hannah (1 Sam. [1 Kgs.] 2:1–10).
[^104]: Tone 1 podoben incipit: *The soldiers keeping watch* (*Гро́бъ тво́й, Сп҃се* / Greek: *Ton taphon sou, Sōtēr*).
[^105]: Luke 15:18, 21: "Father, I have sinned against heaven, and before thee, and am no more worthy to be called thy son."
[^106]: Ode 4 Heirmos: *Foreseeing Thy birth from the Virgin...* (*Ѿ Дв҃ы твоѐ рожество̀*). Based upon the Fourth Biblical Canticle, the Prayer of Habakkuk (Hab. 3:1–19).
[^107]: Habakkuk 3:2–3 (LXX Hab. 3:2–3): "O Lord, I have heard Thy report, and was afraid: O Lord, I considered Thy works, and was astonished... God shall come from Teman, and the Holy One from the mount of Paran [the mount overshadowed]."
[^108]: Ode 5 Heirmos: *The night having passed...* (*Но́щи преше́дшей* / Greek: *Tēs nyktos parelthousēs*). Based upon the Fifth Biblical Canticle, the Prayer of Isaiah (Isa. 26:9–20).
[^109]: Ode 6 Heirmos: *The depth of transgressions...* (*Глꙋбина̀ съгрѣше́нїй* / Greek: *Vythos hamartēmatōn*). Based upon the Sixth Biblical Canticle, the Prayer of Jonah (Jonah 2:1–10).
[^110]: Alternate Ode 6 Heirmos: *In the depth of sins...* (*Въ глꙋбинѣ̀ грѣхѡ́въ* / Greek: *En vythō ptaismatōn*). Also based upon the Sixth Biblical Canticle (Jonah 2:1–10).
[^111]: Tone 3 podoben incipit: *Today the Virgin* (*Дѣ́ва дне́сь* / Greek: *Hē Parthenos sēmeron*), the famous kontakion melody of St. Romanos the Melodist.
